Transkrip_YouTube
=================

Everyone else woke into the Lord continent on a wooden boat. I woke drifting on a single wooden plank, a broke nobody raised on government aid, and the channel laughed at the man too poor for a hull. Then Shiono, the will of the continent, owed me one compensation draw, and out of it came a map fragment pointing to an island.

While everyone else drifted, I planted my territory core and built Bare Rock into an empire. The nobody named Hashin, the man with the plank, became the lord they came begging to. Before we continue, please like and subscribe. It really helps the channel. Before any of that landed, I was nothing. Let me start there.

I opened my eyes to a boundless azure sea, a drizzling gray sky, and the sickening sway of a single wooden board under me. For one stupid moment, I thought I had died in my sleep. I had stayed up half the night gaming, and now here I was, soaked and floating with no land in sight.

Then a voice spoke inside my skull before I could finish panicking, smooth and ungendered, welcoming me to the Lord continent. It told me I needed to find an island, plant my territory, and become a lord. Then it added that this month was a disaster month and that I should sail carefully and that the will of the continent Chano was watching me at all times.

My head spun as I wondered whether I had transmigrated, whether I had become the chosen one, whether the universe had finally decided to pay me back for a miserable life. Then I looked down at the plank I was straddling and decided the universe had a cruel sense of humor. If this was compensation, why was the compensation a wet piece of timber in the middle of nowhere?

I closed my eyes and breathed until my heart slowed. Then willedled the system panel open the way the voice had told me to. A bright screen unfolded in the air in front of me, split into two rows. The top left read personal information, and the top right read territory, dark and uncclickable, which I figured meant I could only touch it once I actually had a territory of my own.

The bottom row held five tabs: chat, trade, construction, personal shop, and technology. I went into personal information first because the most useful thing in any survival situation is knowing exactly how badly off you are. What I read was not encouraging. My player number sat at the top. My level was one with no experience to speak of. My one skill was spirit eye.

My profession was hero. And my talent field said none. I clicked spirit eye and learned it was a starter skill granted by Chianmo that let me view basic information about objects, a support skill with no offense in it at all. Wonderful. I had been dropped into the sea with a glorified pair of reading glasses.

I backed out and opened chat which split into a world channel, a regional channel, and a friend list. The World Channel was a torrent of refreshing text, mostly people screaming into the void, some looking for relatives and a few delusional types announcing that they were the chosen one and intended to conquer the world. I rolled my eyes hard enough to hurt and thought, "Conquer the world.

Sure, kid. " I had nothing to add and no one to add it to. I had spent my whole life alone, raised on government aid, so a wall of strangers shouting would not move me. One line did snag my attention because somebody was wailing that he had woken on a small boat and had a crippling fear of the open ocean.

I mourned him for a polite second, then looked at my plank again and decided I deserved a full century of mourning since everyone else got a wooden boat and I got a wooden board. Feeling thoroughly cheated, I addressed the sky directly and asked Chano whether she had made some kind of mistake. The panel answered before I finished, declaring that an initial error had been detected and compensation was activated.

One random draw. My stomach lurched at the realization that she had actually heard me. Apparently, you really could not curse the goddess out loud because she really was watching. " A square box appeared in front of me with a single round hole on top, just wide enough for a hand.

I leaned over and peered down through the opening and saw nothing but clusters of pale light, no labels, no hints, nothing to grab on logic. So, I did the only thing a man with no information can do, reaching in and yanking out the first one. My fingers touched. The system congratulated me on receiving a random gift package.

The box vanished and a red present materialized in my palm. Random again, and my luck has always been garbage, while the sea was clearly not going to change that, so I told it to open anyway. A flash in my hand was empty, and the panel informed me my items had been stored in space and that I should go check.

After hunting through the tabs, I finally found the storage tucked inside personal shop. 20 slots, 10 of them already full. I went through the hall with the careful greed of a poor man counting coins. The draw had handed me 10 wood, 10 plastic, 10 copper block, one fishing rod, two blueprints, and one talent orb that was also flagged random, and those filled six slots.

The last four held 500 g of bread, 500 ml of water, another fishing rod, and the territory core, the starter rations everyone got. I did not stop to read item descriptions since my most urgent problem was that I was sitting on a single plank in open water, which is a very efficient way to drown. I opened construction which gave me upgrade build and deconstruct and found two upgradeable items waiting.

A fishing rod in the wooden boat. I tapped the boat. The description called my wooden plank a piece of wood that floated and noted that only someone with an intelligence problem would rely on it to sail. " The upgrade demanded exactly 10 wood, and I happened to have exactly 10 wood, which felt less like luck and more like the system grudgingly meeting me halfway.

So, I hit upgrade. White light washed over my feet, and the plank thickened into a small boat. 3 m long and a meter and a half across at the widest. The readout listed it as a wooden boat at level one with no facilities, no power, no technology, and no special abilities. It was a three- nothing product that was somehow still an upgrade over my old four nothing situation.

The next level wanted 20 wood and would add a cabin, but I had no wood left. So, that dream went on the shelf. For now, I had a boat that could not flip out from under me, and that was a real improvement. Over the morning, I started pulling the rest of my useful supplies out and reading them one by one.

The basic fishing rod was green quality and could catch fish and treasure chests with fish kept it at green and everyone got one. Nothing special. The seawater converter, also green, turned seawater into fresh water at 1,000 milliliters a day. The resource fishing rod was the interesting one. Randomly hauling up supplies, materials, blueprints, and special items. Three uses a day.

The last was a map fragment, green quality, which could set an initial location and show the surrounding sea area from that point. The map fragment was the thing I cared about most because Chan Mo had told me to find an island in the sea looked infinite so I could drift for a year and never bump into land.

I picked up the fragment and set my initial point to where I currently was. Then held my breath while the blank blueprint slowly bled in color. The background filled blue. A red dot pulsed at the center for my position. And then in the upper right corner, a black dot resolved into being. There was an island and I actually laughed out loud at the gray sky and thanked whatever heaven was listening.

While everyone else was drifting and leaving themselves to fate, I had a destination. Having a goal and not having one are entire worlds apart. Since a goal is hope, hope is fuel, and a man with no fuel rots fast out here. The only thing I did not know was the map scale because the dot might be close or a year of paddling away with no way to tell yet.

I set the worry aside and checked what it would take to build the seawater converter since nobody survives without water and water was the first thing I wanted locked down. The recipe called for 10 plastic, three wood, and five white stone. My plastic covered itself, but I had no wood to spare and no idea what a white stone even was.

So I opened personal shop again, this time reading instead of grabbing. The shop carried four sections, assets, space, buy and sell. And right now it only bought fish with no listed price, so I would have to catch one and try selling it to learn the rate. The sell side offered just two things to buy. Wood went for two whit stone a piece, which told me white stone was the shop currency.

The second item was a technology card described as something used to light up technology priced at 500 white stone and stamped weekly special item presumably refreshing every week. What threw me was the countdown beneath it which read a little over 5 days. If a week here had a 5-day plus timer left on a fresh special, then maybe a week in this world ran only 6 days.

I filed the oddity away since it would answer itself soon enough. And I had a converter to build. Building that converter still needed three wood and five white stone which meant I had to fish. So, I pulled out the basic fishing rod rather than the resource one because the shop only paid for fish and I needed cash first.

Catching a few fish would tell me the going rate and how precious these white stones really were. There was no bait, which confused me until I read that the rod generated its own with quality tied to the rod's level and grade. While I was in the menu, I noticed the rod itself could be upgraded for a single copper block.

So, I did that at once since the next level wanted two copper block and one wood, and I was not spending wood I did not have. While I waited for a bite, I took out the talent orb and gave it the full superstitious treatment, muttering a little prayer for the king of luck to possess me and hand over something monstrous.

Then I used it. The system congratulated me on an epic talent. 100 transformations and just the word epic made my pulse jump. So on a hunch, I asked Chano what the talent qualities even were. And to my genuine surprise, she answered, listing common, uncommon, rare, epic, and legendary. So the goddess would actually field newbie questions, which was good to know.

Epic was not the top rung, but it was high. And I was getting greedy now. So, I read the full description. 100 transformations epic could cause any item to undergo a positive random change three times per day. I deflated a little because that did not sound powerful at all. More like a slot machine than a weapon.

I decided to reserve judgment and tested on something later because a positive change is still a change in my favor and I have learned never to sneer at free upside. Just then the rod dipped and I hauled with both hands and pulled up a fish barely 10 cm long.

Spirit eye told me it was a small yellow croker white quality level one and absolutely nothing else which by itself told me it was worthless. I opened the shop and sold it on the spot for a single white stone. The fish vanished in white light and my assets ticked up by one leaving nine more to go.

So I fished in earnest and by the end I had pulled nine more level one fish, one of them green quality, enough to scrape together 10 white stone. The grind taught me the math. A level one white fish sold for one white stone and a level one green fish for two.

While my current rod could only land white and green grades, so I spent six white stone on three wood, dumped my 10 plastic in, and synthesized the seawater converter. The materials dissolved in a blue cylinder formed in front of me, a wooden base with a white plastic pipe below, and a plastic drum above. Green quality level one, 1,000 ml of fresh water a day.

One liter a day was enough to keep me alive, maybe with a little to spare to sell. I figured that starting tomorrow, most people out here would begin running dry. And a man with water is a man with leverage. So, I scooped sea water into the drum and left it to work. Glad the converted water collected in the base so I would not need a cup standing by.

With water handled, it was time for the resource fishing rod, the one that could not be upgraded and had no quality at all, where the hall came down entirely to luck. I cast it and while the line settled, ate half my bread and drank half my water to quiet a stomach that had been complaining for an hour.

Then, to kill time, I opened the regional channel where the early panic had cooled into chatter. People were comparing catches. A wooden chest with a bottle of water. An iron chest holding a perimeter wall blueprint. Somebody crowing about an ore blueprint that finally let him row. I skimmed it for anything useful.

The perimeter wall caught my eye as a likely territory piece for later, but the ore interested me more since a destination is useless if you cannot move toward it. The rod tugged and I pulled up a leather bundle, opened it without ceremony, and got 10 wood, which was basically 20 white stone falling out of the sky. That is what a resource rod is for.

I peeled off three wood to finally test my epic talent. And when I aimed 100 transformations at a single piece of wood, the one piece became three, underwhelming exactly as advertised. I used it again and this time the wood became a card that spirit I called a pet card. Blue quality able to randomly summon a blue quality pet.

Now that was worth something with one use left. I triggered 100 transformations a third time and a scroll appeared which spirit I named an ice element messenger class scroll purple granting the ice element messenger class. My luck had just detonated because an epic grade item in a class scroll was the kind of thing people would kill for.

The talent leaned on chance, sure, but with enough rolls, the odds clearly bent my way. So, I used the scroll at once, and as it dissolved, I felt a strange new kinship with the seawater itself. I had the class, though no skills to go with it yet, and I could only hope the skills would come, or the class was a pretty decoration.

I used the pet card next, partly out of curiosity, and partly to console my wounded pride. The instant it vanished, the sea heaved and a blue whale surfaced beside the boat, 5 meters long, blew from snout to tail, calling out twice in a voice I could feel pressing affection straight into my chest. It laid its great head against the hole, and I stroked it, the skin smooth and cool.

Spirit eye read it as a deep sea giant whale, blue quality level two, with the skills charge and bite, a strength of 20 and an agility of 12. I frowned because this thing had attributes, and the small yellow croker had not, and neither did I, but it clicked after a moment.

The whale was level two and attributes only seemed to surface at level two, which raised the obvious question of how I leveled at all. I reopened my panel and found my experience had crept up while I worked. Selling a level one white fish gave two experience, a green one gave four, and selling fish to the shop apparently counted as killing them.

I wondered idly whether buying live fish from other players and reselling them to the shop would also grant experience, and decided to test that someday. For now, I had a plan and a pet. I patted the whale's head and declared that from now on he would be called Little Blue because I am terrible at names and refused to burn brain cells on something better.

Then I picked the resource rod back up, casted in a clean ark and sank back into the simple bliss of fishing. It was not long before the line jerked again, and this time I reeled in an iron chest, my first one, and I rubbed my hands together like a child before opening it. Inside were five chicken legs and 1,000 milliliters of water, food and water, and most importantly, meat.

So, I bit straight into a chicken leg, the whole thing palmsized and golden and fragrant. The meat soft and somehow tastier the longer I chewed. These beat anything I had eaten back on Blue Star, and I said so out loud to nobody. As I ate, I started planning. Water was handled and food I could fish for. But I had no fire, so cooking meant buying flint or something like it.

I opened trade where a fair number of listings had already accumulated and browsed to read the market, finding glass for wood, straw for wood, a white fence blueprint for 10 wood, rope for wood, on and on. Almost everyone hungry for wood to upgrade their wooden boat.

A level two boat got a cabin, and the rain had not stopped since I woke, so nobody wanted to sleep soaked, and I wanted that cabin, too. I searched the word fire and found a flint, white quality, which struck and rubbed to make a spark. I traded one wood for it, a 5cm stone. It solved the cooking problem the moment I had a stove.

Housing would have to wait on the boat itself. I did the grim arithmetic of how much I still needed. The boat wanted 20 wood for its next level. The fishing rod wanted one wood and two copper block. And the converter already built was done. I had nine wood and nine copper block and nothing else basic, which meant fishing like a madman if I wanted the cabin tonight.

Still, I had nine more wood than the average drifter out here. And that head start was the only thing keeping me ahead. I held off upgrading the rod again because I did not want to spend the wood and frankly a higher rod might hook a fish too strong for a level one nobody who could not even see his own attributes.

Play it safe, I told myself. The resource rod twitched and I pulled and a card dropped into my hand. The system announced a legendary hero card before I had even looked at it. So my hand shook with pure greed at the thought that I had been possessed by a golden luck guy at last. I put the rod away and used the hero card immediately.

Golden light bloomed at the bow, and when it faded, I stood there gaping like an idiot. A tall, slender beauty had appeared. Over a meter seven, light gold hair, light gold eyes, a white high-waisted dress, a curved figure she did nothing to hide. She held a small white wooden staff maybe 40 cm long.

She glanced around, found me, frowned faintly at whatever expression I was making, and still bowed, introducing herself softly as Windsor, greeting her lord. It was my first time watching a real person resolve out of a card. And even knowing this was a fantasy world, I was shaken.

I waved off the formality and asked bluntly whether she could fish because as a pragmatist, a pretty face is the last thing I need in a survival game. I need a worker. She paused plainly, not expecting that from a lord who had just been staring at her like a starving dog. And I could read the slight recalibration in her eyes, the suspicion that maybe she had misjudged me.

If she could have heard my thoughts, I would have told her she had judged me exactly right, that I was that man. But capable hands beat a beautiful face when the alternative is drowning. Because beauty is a bonus and usefulness is the necessity. She smiled and said yes, my lord, and asked whether I needed her to fish.

I told her to go fish to knock out whatever she caught without killing it. And I handed over the rod because I wanted a flood of fish to hit level two and finally see my own numbers. She knelt at the bow, her dress pulling Todd across her frame. And I sighed inwardly that a beauty was a beauty, even casting a line.

Then I remembered to be useful and scanned her with spirit eye. Windsor red is legendary, level two, with strength, agility, defense, and wisdom. All at 30, a mana pool of 300, the profession hero and light element messenger. Her skills were healing, light arrow and light shield, a white staff for equipment loyalty at 100, an affinity at 50, which the system marked friendly.

Her stats were nearly double little blues. And if legendary was this terrifying, I was in love with the word. Three skills and one of them healing meant injury and illness stopped being a death sentence. The staff added two strength, a pitiful piece of gear for a hero like her. And I made a quiet promise to get her something high-end and dignified eventually because a legendary swinging a white twig was an insult.

The loyalty and affinity fields were new and hero specific with loyalty sitting maxed, so summoned heroes apparently start fully loyal. Affinity took me a moment to grasp until I landed on the office analogy. A boss hands you a task and you have to do it, but the spirit you bring changes everything.

High affinity means the hero throws herself into it and thinks for the Lord, while low affinity means she goes through the motions like a machine. I stroked my chin and wondered about the scale. And Chon Mo, helpful again, popped a newbie tip listing the tears.

They ran from undying hatred at minus 100, up through enemy, hostile, indifferent, normal, friendly at 50, intimate at 70, admiring at 90, and unwavering loyalty at 100 with a warning that very low affinity would drag loyalty down. So loyalty could fall, which was sobering. I did not yet know how to raise affinity, so I decided to experiment.

I took out a chicken leg, walked over and offered it, asking Windsor whether she wanted something to eat. She tilted her head and smiled faintly and thanked me for the concern, but said she was not hungry. Looking at that smile, I felt the gray drizzle lift a little off my mood, and maybe the road ahead would not be so lonely after all.

A chime sounded, and the system told me that moved by my concern, Windsor's affinity had risen by two. So kindness worked even if only a sliver at a time. A small mosquito is still meat as the saying goes. And slowly that bar would fill because no matter how long the road you reach the end if you keep walking.

The problem was that my only working rod was in Windsor's hands and the resource rod was out of uses for the day. So I wished I could conjure a few more rods with my talent tomorrow. The sea rippled and little blue surfaced from his hunt and the sight of that massive body lit an idea in my head.

So I opened trade, searched ropes, and found a list. All going for one wood, some for one rope, some for two or three. I spent two wood and walked away with six ropes, each about 4 m long and 2 cm thick. Then I tied them in pairs into three 8 m lengths, looped each into a circle, and fastened all three to the bow.

Then I told Windsor to stop fishing and slide toward the middle because I was running an experiment. She gave me a puzzled look, but obeyed, moving to the center of the boat. And I called Little Blue over and had him bite the rope, telling him carefully to swim forward. Nice and slow. He chirped that he understood and started pulling and the boat lurched on the first stroke.

Inertia throwing both of us off balance. Windsor tipped back into me, her softness pressing flat against my back before she scrambled upright. Scarlet to the ears, touching her cheek and sneaking a look to gauge my reaction. I noticed none of it because I was watching the boat actually glide forward.

I burst out laughing and shouted that it worked, that who needed oars now since I had dreaded the ore problem and little blue had just erased it faster than any ore besides.

I told him to speed up and he did and the boat picked up pace and I had him accelerate again and again until I found a clip that was quick but stable while Windsor watched me grin and caught the grin herself without meaning to. I called little blue to a stop since this was only a feasibility test and I had not aimed us along the map yet with no sense in overshooting.

Then I patted his head and sent him off to hunt telling him to come back at dark and he dove. I told Windsor to keep fishing and as she passed she met my eyes by accident, looked away fast and went pink again. I stared after her, baffled, certain I had missed something, but unable to name it.

I gripped to myself that those two ropes had cost me precious wood, leaving seven, and the boat upgrade wanted 20, which was 26 white stone counting purchases, and I doubted I could catch that much before dark. So, I prayed Windsor's luck beat mine. To pass the time, I watched the chat where players traded news.

A silver chest that coughed up a green talent for an hour of underwater breathing, a white talent giving five white stone a day, somebody who killed a level two fish and got a white skill stone. So chest could yield talent orbs too and killing monsters could drop items and I wondered why I had fished up no talent.

Grumbling that I would have loved the underwater breathing one. The killing drops items part stung because I had sold them not killed them so they were different mechanics. Windsor handed me a fish and I scanned it on reflex finding a blue arrow fish green level two strength 12 agility 13 defense 12 with skills water arrow and extreme speed.

It was the same level as Little Blue, but green quality, and its stats ran about five points under as blue, which told me quality mattered enormously and made me wonder what quality I was. I drove my fist down hard into the fish. It died. Green light flashed and the body vanished, leaving a green stone the size of a cobble.

The system congratulated me on a skill stone, water arrow technique. So, killing dropped the loot and erased the corpse. While my experience ticked up eight points, I asked Chano how the mechanism worked and got silence. So, I let it go, certain I would figure it out within a day or two.

I picked up the skill stone and spirit eye told me water arrow technique green raise strength by 20% while active and was learnable by a water ice element messenger. So, I learned it directly and information slotted into my mind as if I had drilled it for years.

I waved a hand and a water arrow burst out of nothing and dented the hole and I winced then shrugged since I would upgrade the boat anyway. I loosed five more in a row before the well ran dry, which I figured was my mana giving out. So I sat and rested, recovering and firing, recovering and firing, learning the rhythm of my own pool while the sky slowly dimmed.

By dusk I had gathered enough white stone with some despair because Windsor's afternoon had been generous. 15 level one white fish, one level two white, one level three white, five level one green, and one level three green. All of it together selling for 35 white stone. Killing them dropped nothing. But the selling taught me the price formula.

Level times quality. So a green level three was 3 * 2 six white stone. And a blue level three would be three times 39 and so on up the ladder. I spent 34 white stone on 17 wood, then poured 20 wood into the boat upgrade.

The boat stretched to six meters long and three across the middle and a wooden cabin sprouted in the center just over a meter high, two long nearly three wide. Doors four and a half opened onto the decks at either end. I pushed through the door and ducked inside, clean and dry.

And for the first time since waking, I was out of the rain, which was only a drizzle, but had been gnawing at me for hours. I opened build and spent one wood on two benches, a basic function alongside beds and tables. Then I skipped a bed since the room was too cramped for it.

I called Windsor in to rest, woke little blue, pointed him along the map, and had him tow us into the dark while I went back to eat and sleep. Windsor sat stiffly on her bench, and when I walked in, I caught the thought crossing her face that she would be sharing the small room with me all night, and her cheeks warmed.

I was calmer about it since the cabin was 3 m wide after all, though I will admit to a flicker of anticipation. I sat beside her, took out two chicken legs and two bottles of water, and handed her a set, telling her the conditions were rough now, but should be much better tomorrow.

I was already planning to upgrade the boat again, though I worried little Blue might not be able to tow it once it got too big. She said it was fine, Lord, in that gentle voice that I half suspected was an effect of the light element. I told her to eat and rest, then checked my own sheet while I chewed.

I had reached level two with strength, agility, and defense all sitting at 9, wisdom at 10, mana at 100. My profession was hero and ice element messenger. My skills spirit eye and water arrow technique and my talent 100 transformations epic. My stats were a third of windsor's all clustered around 10 and I still could not see my starting attributes which I would only learn at the next level.

Level three wanted 200 experience and I doubted I would clear it tomorrow. The extra profession line came from the scroll and the new skill from the green stone. So I closed the panel, finished the chicken leg and drank a whole bottle without guilt since the converter would make two tomorrow.

I pocketed the empty bottle to hold water later, lay back on the bare boards, full and tired, and opened chat one last time. The channel was a chorus of misery, people sleeping in the rain, people too scared to sleep at all, fishing through the night because they were alone on the sea, and one lucky soul had summoned a character to row for him tomorrow.

The replies just envy stacked on envy. Watching them, I felt almost cheerful because life is comparison, and by comparison, I was doing wonderfully. I glanced at Windsor lying with her back to me and admitted the bare boards needed to become a bed soon because sleeping wrapped in a warm quilt with a beauty nearby would be a far better existence.

I cut the thought off there, telling myself to sleep now and struggle tomorrow, and both of us drifted off as the boat rocked on toward the distant black dot. I woke to a weight on my chest and a delicate face inches from mine. Long lashes, straight nose, slightly parted lips with a faint sheen on them. Windsor had wrapped herself around me in her sleep.

One leg thrown across me, her skirt rucked up to bear a long pale stretch of thigh. Early in the morning with all my furnaces lit. This was a brutal test of willpower. So, I shut my eyes, steadied my breathing, killed myself free, and got up to step outside.

It was not that I had no thoughts about her since any man with eyes would, but her affinity was still far too low for any of that, and the living conditions were too miserable besides. You only think about desire once you are fed and warm, and I was neither. I did not wake her, and I could see her lashes trembling, so I knew she was awake and only pretending.

And to spare us both the awkwardness, I pretended right back. Two chimes rewarded me, her affinity rising three for my thoughtfulness and five for the close contact in the night, an unexpected bonus. So I smiled and took out the resource rod. The drizzle outside had thinned, but I knew my clothes would soak through if I sat in it long.

And worse, if the rain kept building, standing water could pull on the boat and sink it. Windsor came out and asked softly whether I needed anything. So I handed her the rod, and while I was at it, spent two copper block and one wood to bring it to level three, safe enough with her present.

She set a bench beside me and sat and after a while her skirt grew damp and clingy and I caught myself staring and forced my eyes away which she noticed and she flushed somewhere between embarrassed and annoyed. Then she raised a shield against the rain and a wash of white mist rose as the heat dried her clothes.

I gaped at the idea that light could do that simply radiate heat on command and I had genuinely learned something. Element messengers clearly were not limited to their listed skills since they could improvise their own uses which I filed away for later. My three resource casts for the day paid off well. 55 wood, 10 iron block, and one green staff.

And the wood nearly covered the next boat upgrade by itself. The boat's level four wanted 80 wood and 200 iron nails, and one iron block forged into 10 iron nails, which left me 10 iron blocks short of the nails. An easy buy. The staff read, raising strength by 10 and mana by 10%.

Far better for Windsor than for me, since on me, the percent gave 10 mana, while on her it gave 30. So, I handed it over and took back her white staff for myself. She thanked me and smiled and a chime said the gift had pushed her affinity up too and I smiled back.

Right then Shan Mo cut in with the string of global announcements telling me that this week's disaster was rain and today would be light rain. The points ranking opened starting today settling on the weekend with rewards by rank and as beginner welfare. Everyone got 3 hours of underwater breathing and 3 hours of wooden boat diving today. The voice closed by urging everyone to develop hard and establish a territory quickly.

Then asked whether I wanted to use the welfare now and I clicked no because I needed to digest all of this first. The rain disaster being only light now meant nothing since something called a rain disaster would not stay this gentle. So I had to solve drainage before it turned ugly or I was doomed. The ranking with weekend rewards had to be lucrative with billions competing.

So I opened the panel and found a new ranking list tab between personal information and territory. I sat at the top with 170 points with Danton second at 70, Lee Kio third at 69, Ebake Pong fourth at 65, and Hagawa fifth at 60. I was a full 100 points clear of second place, which I credited entirely to Little Blue and Windsor doing the heavy work while I schemed.

The boat's special ability now read diving, temporary, and not yet activated, which was the welfare effect. And chat was already buzzing about me with people demanding to know who this Hashin was, sitting so far above the pack.

Someone declared I was clearly a heavyweight worth latching on to and asked, half joking, if he could latch on, while someone else pointed out it had been a single day and there was no telling yet who was a real heavyweight. A few swore they would surpass me and someone bragged about sailing out onto the open sea and how beautiful it was.

Immediately followed by a man screaming that a 30 m giant shark was staring him down. I scanned it all, found nothing useful, and cleared out a stack of friend requests with various notes, some of them with revealing photos attached, which I reviewed with a critical eye purely for due diligence before deleting them all. I could not reach any of those people.

And even if I could, why would I, when Windsor was right here, 100% loyal, no risk of a knife in the back. As for the ones vowing to surpass me, I did not care in the slightest. Let the miserable talk big since it cost nothing. My 100 transformations only ever produced positive changes. And I wondered whether anyone grasped how valuable that was.

Using it on a green item would get me at minimum green and at maximum golden legendary, while using it on blue guaranteed at least an exceptional result. Yesterday, I had only had Wood defeat it and still pulled a golden legendary by sheer luck. So tonight, I meant to feed it something higher and aim for at least a purple epic.

Windsor broke into my scheming, handing over a blueprint with her fair little hand, and Spirit. I read it as ship drainage technology, which once built was a sharp tool for rainy weather, requiring me to light up the ship technology tree first. This was the core answer to the disaster, and since it came off the initial rod, nearly everyone would have one.

But the catch was the lightup condition, and the technology card to do it cost 500 white stone in the shop. People would have to fish themselves raw to afford it, and if they fell short before the rain turned cruel, their boats would sink under them. Many would pull this blueprint today.

And with the welfare letting everyone hunt fish directly in the sea, plenty would gamble on a hunt to grind cash, which meant plenty would die today. I opened the regional channel and counted 9,841 people left. Over a hundred dead since yesterday, the unlucky ones who had stumbled into something too strong.

I called Windsor in, ate yesterday's two leftover chicken legs, split a bottle of converter water with her, refilled the converter, and went to wake Little Blue at the bow since he had towed us all night and had to be tired. The whales sleep little. I roused him, told him to ready himself, and activated the diving feature. So, a blue spindle-shaped shield sealed over the whole boat.

Then I told him to dive, and he chirped, bit the rope, and pulled us under. The instant we sank below the surface, the beauty of it stopped me cold, with fish of every shape and color drifting past, some glowing, some crackling with little arcs above their heads, some trailing black liquid from their jaws. Windsor stood transfixed beside me, having never seen such a thing either.

So I shook myself loose and scanned a school not far off. Three or 400 small white fish about half a meter long, level two. Basic stats around five or six. I set little blue straight at them. They swarmed and bit and shot water arrows from their mouths, nicking gaps in his hide and pattering off the shield. But his defense of 18 made it nearly impossible for white great attackers to hurt him.

So he simply opened his jaws and ate. Windsor and I loosed light arrow and water arrow from inside. One light arrow per kill. And when our mana drained, we just sat and recovered since nothing under 60 strength could crack the shield, a number I had checked before we ever went down. When the school realized it could not win, it scattered and fled.

I collected the corpses, only about 30 since Little Blue had eaten most for over 60 white stone plus a wooden box that held a single wood. I let him keep devouring the rest because I wanted him to level since a bigger whale towes a bigger boat. I caught myself thinking the technology card looked easy to afford after a haul like that, then immediately checked my own arrogance.

Most people had no whale to tow them and rode slowly, often unable to catch the fish at all, while I could hide in the cabin and cast, and I had a legendary hero killing alongside me, which is why my numbers swelled. Honestly, of the 10 water arrows I had fired, I killed only six and missed four, and Windsor handled the rest.

So, I had earned about 12 Whit Stone myself, which meant Whit Stone was not as easy as it looked, but it beat plain fishing by a mile. I sent little blue hunting again and only targeted low attack schools at my own level because I had noticed that killing monsters below my level granted zero experience while only equal or higher levels paid out which was its own special kind of annoying.

The hall climbed and I also discovered the sea was full of absurdities. White bears swimming past, even monkeys. I could not work out how land animals breathe down here, whether they had grown gills until I watched boats speeding by underwater and accepted that I simply had not adapted to this world yet. Inertial thinking is hard to break in a day.

An hour in, Little Blue leveled and grew noticeably larger, and Windsor leveled right behind him. Then another hour and Little Blue leveled again, and I leveled shortly after. I checked my sheet. I was level three now. Every attribute up four points, strength, agility, and defense each at 13, wisdom at 14, mana at 140, and the four-point jump meant my starting attributes had been five, with wisdom a point higher.

Little Blue had reached level four, strength 26, agility 24, defense 24, three points per level, while Windsor hit level three with everything up five into the mid-30s, mana at 350, affinity now 62, and a new green staff equipped.

Even though I expected it, the gap stung because if Windsor leveled twice, she would nearly match my entire current self in raw stats, and she was sure to level again soon, having killed almost three times the monsters I had, slowed only because many were below her level and paid nothing. The legendary hero earned her name, and I half wondered whether I would just be living off her from here on.

A man should not live off his hero, of course, but a man could certainly live off other soft and tender things. And I let that thought drift somewhere warmer before yanking it back. I summarized what I had learned. That level gains were fixed and could not be allocated, only averaged across the board, and that the higher the quality and talent, the bigger the gains.

White adding one per attribute per level, green two, blue three, purple epic four, and golden legendary five. I marveled all over again at drawing a purple epic talent on day one. Because had I gotten it later, I would have wept for all the attribute points lost. A moment of silence for everyone who drew theirs late. I meant to keep hunting, but the sea around us went quiet.

No beasts surfacing anywhere, and the silence but a cold weight in my gut. So I warned Little Blue to slow down and stay careful. And Windsor's face had already gone grave as she swept the water. Little Blue eased the boat past an underwater mountain range, and a shadow exploded out of the rocks so fast he had no time to react, and it goured him clean through the side.

Crimson bloomed in the water, two bloody holes opening on his flank, and only Windsor's instant light shield kept the blow from killing him outright. The beast had clearly not expected her speed either, and boked of an instant kill. It pulled up and began circling us, which finally let me get a good look.

It had a body like a catfish, entirely white, five or six meters long, with two long spiraled horns whose tip still flickered with white light. Spirit eye named it a seabbrite twin horn beast. Purple, level six, strength 40, agility 41, defense 40, with the skills stealth, ray, charge, and extreme speed. My face went tight at the thought of a purple monster, epic grade, and level six.

Because only Windsor could meaningfully hurt it, and only with the staff lending her 10 strength. The single mercy was that its strength fell short of 60, leaving the boat safe. I told Windsor to attack while Little Blue and I harassed it because running was hopeless against something faster than my whale. Then I threw a water arrow that landed cleanly.

My aim sharp now after a morning of practice, but it barely registered against 40 defense with my 15 strength. The point was not damage, but insult, and the beast obliged, wheeling and charging the boat with extreme speed. The shield held, the impact shoving us back through the water, and the beast looked genuinely stunned that the blue bubble had not shattered.

But I did not wait for us to stabilize. I shouted at Winer to aim for the eyes because no matter how high the defense eyes are soft and her strength was 45, so she loosed two light arrows, one taking the right eye, the other glancing off its head. Purple blood streamed from the socket and the beast roared, its remaining eye fixing red on the boat.

Then light patterns flowed along its horns and gathered at the tips, and two beams of light shot out at us, its ray skill. I ignored the beams and told Windsor to take the left eye because the beast was not stupid and might flee once it realized it could not break the shield. And a blind beast cannot flee since this thing was money.

Both attacks crossed in the water at once. Her arrow blinding the left eye while its ray only rippled the shield. Blind in both eyes, maddened by darkness and pain, it thrashed everywhere, still spitting beams from its horns and hitting nothing. Windsor kept calmly drilling its eyes without further instruction until it slowed, slowed, and finally died.

A purple flash and a staff floated up where it fell and I burst out laughing because money I could earn a dozen ways now, but equipment was the hard thing to get. I stepped off the boat and my feet found solid footing on the water itself. And I ran to the staff without pausing to savor the strange sensation because spirit eye had already floored me.

Authority light purple with three traits. Raising light attribute strength by 30%, raising mana by 30%, and carrying the stealth skill to hide a target on use. So three traits, two of them percentage boosts plus a skill. I carried it back and gave it to Windsor, taking her green staff for myself, and a chime said her affinity rose five at the gesture.

I smiled and told her to heal little blue and then we would press on, and she cast healing and his wounds knitted shut in moments. We hunted again, and not long after, we ran straight into a second incident. Two sea beasts already locked in a brutal fight. Both of them wrecked by the time we spotted them. One looked like a flood dragon with a snapped horn.

Most of its scales gone, bleeding from everywhere, while the other was a white bear with one arm hanging useless, and one eye blinded, its body a map of wounds. And they faced off, watching each other for any twitch. I had Windsor cast stealth to hide us, then scanned both. The deep sea demonic flood dragon was purple, level 11, strength 80, agility 83, defense 81, skills, demonic flame, and demonic illusion eye.

While the extreme ice giant bear was purple. Level 11, strength 83, agility 79, defense 83, skills intimidate, freeze, and mighty strike. My eyelids twitched and I nearly turned to run, then force myself calm because they had not noticed us. So, I would simply wait, and if they bled each other to death, I would walk away rich.

The plan was sound, but as time dragged, the two beasts slowly backed apart and finally turned and fled in opposite directions. I sat there dumbruck, debating whether to chase, then sent little blue trailing the giant bear from far back. I left the flood dragon alone because I did not know its skills and it felt the more advanced of the two, harder to handle.

So, I took the safer gamble. Before long, the bear settled onto a great undersea mountain, found a cave, and lay down inside. Following at a distance, I doubted it had reached any real den since it had simply needed somewhere to collapse because its injuries were too severe. It had bled the whole way, the arm especially. So I figured if the bleeding did not stop, it might die within hours on its own.

The trouble was my welfare clock had under 20 minutes left. So I sat there torn, weighing the risk, and finally gritted my teeth and decided to go for it. Wealth lives in danger, and I had no idea when another chance like this would come because killing a highquality beast so far above my level had a strong chance of dropping something excellent, and it would surely level Windsor.

So, I had her attack the eyes, the old method, and the bear was already half blind, which helped. The snag was that it lay with its eyes shut, and I doubted her arrows could pierce closed lids, and if she failed and exposed us, I was finished.

I would not take that risk, so I used Little Blue instead, having him pull the boat slightly nearer, swim well off, then drop his stealth, baiting the bear to open its eyes, and look, since as long as it did not pounce on him at once, Windsor could blind the open eye and we would be safe.

It worked because the moment Little Blue unccloaked, the giant bear's eyes snapped open toward him, and Little Blue pretended to drift past the cave oblivious. The bear, dismissing the weak whale, decided to eat it for nourishment. But before it could move, a water arrow struck its blind right eye from the right.

Its good eye swung that way and found me, and in that instant, two light arrows hit its left eye from the left. With its attention split, it could not dodge, and the first arrow took the open eye, while the rest struck the closed lid and did nothing. That harmless deflection was the whole reason I had played it cautious.

And I congratulated myself quietly because the bear never saw the boat or Windsor and went fully blind, roaring, smashing the cave with its paws and hurling broken rock at random. I was already back aboard and we picked out its eyes and ruined arm from a distance, bleeding it steadily. And 10 minutes later, it collapsed on its last breath and unable to move.

So Windsor finished it with a few more light arrows. My priority was leveling her since hers was the most cost-effective leveling and I now lost four attribute points to her gains on every level of my own. So, I had little appetite to level myself when her protection was enough. I would grind seriously only after finding a way to push my talent to legendary for the five points per level.

But for now, I let things ride. A purple flash and all that remained floating was a palmsized model of a snow mountain which I collected before ordering Little Bloom to rush us topside since time was nearly gone. On the way up, I checked the bear's drop, an extreme ice giant bear soldier nest. Epic, which let me recruit an extreme ice giant bear troop once used, restricted to a lord with a territory.

Just then, little blue broke the surface, the boat leaping clear of the water and slamming back down in a sheet of spray, and I looked out over the vast gray sea, where even the rain could not touch my mood. I laughed and pulled Windsor into a hug beside me, and whether the timing was an accident, only I knew.

When I let go, her face was scarlet, and I had held her too tightly. And afterward, she felt a strange emptiness, missing the fullness of the embrace, wishing she could feel it again. Then turning red at her own thought.

Watching me beam, she caught the smile, and a bright one of her own broke across her face, and a chime said her affinity rose three for being affected by me, and a second chime said it had reached intimate.

I was a little confused about what affected meant, like the game thought I was contagious, but I had not expected to hit 70 affinity this fast, and I was not complaining, so I would keep at it. Then I had Windsor take up the rod and fish while little Blue went off to hunt and level on his own.

Already 10 m long now, and I waited for him to keep growing so he could tow a bigger boat. The technology tab lit up, split into territory technology, still locked, and ship technology, which I could now open, and it was crammed with options across energy, power, and weapons.

Page after page, every entry grayed out with a light up button beside it. I stopped browsing before my heart gave out and used the ship drainage technology blueprint instead and a glow and ship drainage technology appeared at the top of the tab with a build button which I clicked. Building costing 200 white stone. So the drainage alone had run me 700 whit stone all told.

A grim thought surfaced that maybe everyone would simply die. But I shook it off because Chano would not haul billions here only to wipe them out in one stroke since that probability was too low and surely other changes were coming.

Back in the cabin, I checked the boat sheet. Level three at 10 x 5 facilities cabin and toilet. No power. Level one ship drainage technology.

Windsor had reached level four and little blue was still level four, leaving me the only one at level three, which did not bother me at all because I had Windsor and could wait and finally raise my talent to legendary for five points a level instead of four.

As for the toilet smell, there was none since it dumped straight into the sea and the boat was always moving. The reply did not go the way I expected because the seller, a man named Lee Chung in big shorts and a floral vest fishing on a level one plank in the rain, was stunned that I had reached out at all. And his first thought was plainly to latch on to me as a heavyweight.

He gushed that he must have done good deeds in a past life to have me contact him and I felt a little helpless since the man was too much, nothing to be done. So I told him flatly that I wanted his blueprint to stop the useless talk and hurry up. He said he would change the listing and designate it to me.

And he had his own calculation underneath that if he was going to latch on, he wanted a heavy weight who would carry him up, not crush him on a whim. So, he was testing me. The blueprint had sat unsold nearly a day and was no use to him anyway. So, why not risk it? Since if he hooked a ride, he could skip a great many detours.

Then, I watched the trade change to designate the blueprint to me for one wood. And I stared wondering if his brain had been kicked by a donkey. I did not take it for one wood because damaging my reputation for so trivial a gain is not what a clever man does and I did not want to owe anyone a favor.

I told him to change the price that I would buy it for 10 white stone and that when negotiating a trade he should negotiate the trade and skip everything else. His face lit up at that I could practically hear it through the text because apparently he liked a heavyweight with principles, the only kind worth sucking up to and he said to consider the blueprint his trade for a friend slot.

I frowned at the long-windedness and was about to reply when another message came through that he had a second item to sell me and I should name a higher price. I asked what it was and he sent an image of a talent quality enhancement orb green with a small chance to give a small improvement to a talent.

My pulse spiked at the thought that such an item existed and I had to have it. So I told him to make an offer and he sent three words back that in his telling rire of a rich boss. He hesitated then said 300 white stone since it was something he had drawn the first day and saved to use himself.

But after today's announcement in his own drainage blueprint, he had grown nervous and wanted to cash out. He wavered then agreed partly to leave a good impression and partly because the item had little market right now while white stone still held strong purchasing power.

He said he would just give me the blueprint and only ask for a friend slot and that he would sell me good items in the future because Lie Chong understood that providing value was the only way to matter. I agreed, added him as a friend, and paid him 160 rather than 150. The extra being for the blueprint, a small show of generosity that costs little and buys a loyal supplier.

I used the orb at once, and the system told me to use the talent quality enhancement orb, congratulated me on a successful enhancement, and announced the talent had changed. So, I was ecstatic because a small chance landed on the first try. 100 transformations now read that it could cause any item to undergo positive random changes with the quality guaranteed to rise by one tier three times daily.

So, a white item would become at least green and an epic item would become at least legendary. Not bad at all, though. Epic grade items were brutally hard to come by now. So, I cleared the lingering friend requests and counted the survivors. 9,11 nearly a thousand dead. A heavy toll, but I was struggling myself.

So, I grieve over strangers since I could only wish them luck because helping others was not on my agenda. If I had spare capacity, I would not mind a little kindness. But not yet, since my motto has always been to perfect myself when poor and benefit the world when successful, provided they never cost my own interests.

The demon domain opened once a month, and killing demon domain monsters rewarded whit stone, while killing demon domain novice lords rewarded merit points and whitest stone, though spirit domain novice lords granted no merit points.

Merit points were the currency of Chan Mo City, which opened at the end of each month, and the merit point ranking was now open, and the voice closed by urging all novice lords to fight bravely and build merit.
