Windsor was already moving, a light arrow streaking out while she dropped a light shield over me.

Now that I wasn't about to die, I could finally see the thing. A black octopus floated on the swells. Its bloated heads standing 2 meters tall, seven or eight tentacles five or six meters long, flailing against Windsor's barrier. My spirit eye read it as a hundred armed demon octopus. Purple quality level five with strength and agility and defense all in the high30s, carrying entanglement, whip, and mist.

Its level was low enough that Windsor could manage. This was the first time anything had attacked me out on the sea. Two whole days in the spirit domain and nothing had touched me. And here in the demon domain, a monster jumped me the instant I arrived. Bad luck partly, but I suspected being a spirit domain lord made me a juicier target on enemy waters.

When the octopus couldn't crack Windsor, it started swinging at me instead, though her shield held. Each whip dimmed the light a little, so the barrier wasn't going to last forever. The thing switched tactics, snaking tentacles around the shield to crush it, and hairline cracks spread across the globe before Windsor stacked a fresh one over me. That sent it into a fury, lashing wild.

I joined in, firing water arrow technique at its eyes to keep it rattled, and wounds slowly mounted across its body. Then it vomited a flood of ink. A black mist swallowed everything. And by the time the cloud thinned, the octopus was gone. I frowned at the empty water. That fight exposed my real weakness. The total lack of control skills.

The second an enemy decided to run, I had no way to pin it down. Skills weren't cheap to come by, and control types least of all. Nothing to do but sigh and send Little Blue cruising again. A school of fish swarm the ship before long. Flat elongated bodies all red freckled with blue spots each about half a meter.

My spirit I tagged them blue spotted red fish. Green quality level five with stats in the teens and a water arrow skill slightly above my own numbers but nothing to Windsor or little blue who scattered the school in moments. The survivors fleeing in every direction. Here was a pleasant surprise. Each kill paid out 10 white stone and the corpses didn't vanish, which meant I could sell them on top of the reward.

Every fish was worth double. That single wave dumped over 800 white stone into my account, plus a civilian summon card and some food. I swept it all into my space to sort later. Right now, the only goal was stacking white stone fast. This was nothing like yesterday. Yesterday, I'd hunted for prey myself, but here I just had to keep drifting and the monsters came to me.

Mostly white and green quality, mostly around level five or six. Then a blue one showed up and went straight for little blue, punching a small dent in his head. Blood kept welling out and the wound darkened, turning black at the edges. Sea viper, blue quality, level seven, stats in the mid-30s, armed with entanglement and poison arrow.

Windsor threw shields over herself and me first, then poured healing into Little Blue, then opened up on the Viper. I added water arrows while Little Blue wrapped the thing and held it in place. He took hits doing it, but he's enormous. The poison wasn't going to drop him soon, and the healing kept flowing.

The Viper tried to bolt and Little Blue blocked the escape, buying Windsor the window she needed to finish it. This time, the Viper vanished outright in a flash of blue light, clearly leaving a drop. The reward was 210 white stone, which made me sit up. So, blue quality kills paid roughly 10 times what green and white ones did.

Then, a notification told me I'd gained a skill stone, an entangling spell. I checked it. Blue quality raising strength by 30% when cast, learnable by an elementalist. I burst out laughing right there on the deck. A control skill, exactly the gap I'd just been cursing, dropping straight into my lap like a pillow for a sleepy man. I decided it went to Windsor.

She was my strongest piece, so the skill would do the most damage in her hands. And frankly, it wasn't precious enough for me to hoard. If it had been purple, I'd have kept it for myself without hesitation. I handed it over and told her to use it, and she took it with a quiet thanks, calling me Lord, and learned it on the spot.

Her affinity ticked up four points, just like I expected. These little gifts paid me back twice over. We set sail again, and about an hour later, I ran into something new. Not a sea monster, but a rookie demon domain lord. His ship wore black and red coloring with a wooden house aboard, a tier 2 build. And besides him, three other men crewed it, all male.

One bailed water with a bucket while the lord and two others rode. The moment he spotted me, he stopped dead, and all four scrambled to their feet. His face curdled into something between surprise and dread, staring at my hull, which dwarfed his. He'd heard Shano's announcement about spirit domain lords coming to be killed for merit and white stone and clearly hadn't expected the first one he met to outclass him.

He kept glancing around my deck, baffled that something so big moved with so few hands, probably wondering if I'd already unlocked propulsion technology. The longer he thought, the worse he looked, I didn't waste time on his feelings and just ordered Little Blue to charge. As we closed in, the Lord caught sight of my whale and his face drained into raw fear.

The realization landing that I was towing the ship with a monster pet. He cast his appraisal skill on me. My spirit eye saw him reading it and I could guess what he got. Me as epic talent level three. Windsor as a legendary hero level four. Little blue as blue quality level four. Since everyone could hide their real stats and I'd hidden the whales.

Even that scrap was enough to gut him. A spirit domain lord with an epic talent and an absurdly beautiful legendary hero at his side. While he stared, I read him back. Joshin, excellent quality, level three, another talent holder. The three men with him were ordinary commoners. All level two. Once we were close, I didn't strike. I asked which country he was from.

Josh Shin must have read mercy into the question because he answered fast. Said he came from the Tien Lling Empire, then tacked a respectful boss onto the end and turned it back on me. Tien Lingling Empire. Never heard of it. Definitely not a blue star place. Which confirmed these demon domain lords were pulled from some other planet entirely.

My curiosity satisfied. He outlived his usefulness. So, I figured he could contribute to my cause instead. I told little blue to slaughter them and told Windsor to keep the lord breathing. Little blue rammed the hull while Windsor's light arrow started picking the crew apart. Joshin had embraced for an attack the instant he named his empire.

Half convinced I was some enemy from his home world, but he didn't sit waiting to die either. All four snatched up bows and loosed arrows at us. And Joshins carried flames, his flame attachment talent. The light shield ate every shot. Windsor, meanwhile, dropped one man per arrow until only Josh Shin remained. And her entangling spell snapped a rope of light around him before he could run.

We rode little blue across to his ship. Joshin looked at me like he wanted to chew through my throat, but the look did nothing for him. I asked what his planet was called, straight to the point, because I wanted real information out of him. Different countries, even dragged across to the same world, developed differently. People from small nations tend to think small.

They lack the scope and hunger of a great nation upbringing, and that ceiling follows them when they build a territory. So, the answer mattered to me. Josh gave me nothing but a glare. I almost laughed at him. Told him that if Glaring killed people, nobody would bother leveling up, and offered him a clean death if he just talked.

He kept his mouth shut. I'd never killed anyone before all this. Never been in a real fight, just an ordinary broke loner, so what came next didn't come naturally. But I'd come to a world that ran on blood, and I had to train myself to stomach it. So, I took the dagger I'd looted off a monster earlier, and I made it hurt, working on him until the pain broke his nerve.

He screamed and cursed, and I kept my voice light, asking again whether he'd talked, reminding him the information wasn't even worth this much suffering. He held out longer than I expected, then folded and begged. I asked everything I wanted, got my answers, and finished him myself. A clean cut to the throat. My stomach turned, this being my first kill, but I breathed through it, and let the nausea pass.

The system rewarded me for wiping out a demon domain rookie Lord Force, 66 Mara points, and 660 white stone. The corpses dropped a heap besides food, a territory core, a green fishing rod, 36 plastic, 12 iron block, and three copper block. There was also a white wall blueprint, a green arrow tower blueprint, and another 321 white stone.

Then a third prompt offered me the ownerless ship to occupy or destroy. Three surprises stacked one on the next. A genuine windfall. Killing a lord handed over all their resources and paid merit on top. Far better value than grinding sea beasts. Only a fool would occupy that ship and tie himself down.

So I chose to destroy it and even that paid out a tier one ship core, a green ore crafting blueprint, and 15 wood. I swept everything into my space and sat back down with Windsor and Little Blue, suddenly eager to find the next lord.

The hall also sharpened a worry, killing one of my own kind earned nothing from Chan Mo, but it still handed over the dead lord's resources, which meant other people would do exactly this the moment they got the chance. I refused to be naive enough to think I was the only spirit domain lord killing demon domain lords or that someone wouldn't come for me.

Everyone in the spirit domain knew I held the top survival score. The bigger the target painted on my back, the more careful I'd have to be. I tucked the thought away and went back to hunting monsters, and the run stayed smooth. No real danger, just the occasional demon domain lord who paid out generously every time I put them down.

With maybe an hour left before the demon domain closed, I crossed paths with the spirit domain lord. She was under attack. A jellyfish about 5 m long swarming her tier 2 boat. An orc beside her in leather armor, goldenhaired and built like a lion tribe fighter. My spirit eye named them No Cena, excellent quality level four. And her hero Baru, excellent quality level four.

I knew the name No Cena sat sixth on the survival board. When she saw me coming, she went from startled to overjoyed, recognizing a fellow spirit domain lord. And after reading my information, she lit up brighter still and screamed for me to save her. While she shouted, she said something to the orc. The orc hero hurled a pink sphere at me.

Windsor shot it out of the air and it burst into a cloud of pink mist. That was when I caught no scene's smile, brilliant and wrong. And the jellyfish abruptly broke off its attack on her and came knifing toward me red and fast. A sheet of crimson cloth gliding through the water. My face went cold. The trap clicked into place.

She'd been worried I wouldn't bother saving her, so she'd use some item to yank the sea beast aggression straight onto me. I cursed and swore I'd settle the score the second I dealt with this thing. Watching her already paddling away. My spirit eye read the monster as a crimson lotus jellyfish, blue quality stats around 30, carrying bewilder and entanglement.

If she just asked me politely, I'd have killed it for her and pocketed the reward myself. No harm done. Instead, she pulled this. Windsor loosed an arrow and missed, swaying midshot, and the same dizziness washed over me. The bewilder skill at work. In that gap, the jellyfish reached us. Red threads erupted into the air and lashed at the boat, and Windsor threw up two light shields.

The threads coiled around them and tightened until I could hear the barriers creaking. Little Blue joined the fight, but couldn't keep pace with the thing, and he had no range skill to reach it. I fired at it non-stop and kept missing the creature too quick despite its size. My aim drifting sideways whenever the bewilder hit. Then Windsor cast entangling spell, two ropes of light clamping down on it.

We piled on together, though my shots still wandered off under the days. The jellyfish thrashed, but Windsor's entangling spell now carried over 60 points of strength and simply wouldn't break. So, we focused it down and killed it. A skill stone dropped and I scooped it up, delighted. Bewilder, blue quality, interfering with creatures whose intelligence sat below 30% of mine, learnable by an illusionist or light element controller.

Excellent skill. If we'd had it during that fight, the jellyfish wouldn't have lasted seconds. The only thing nagging me was that I couldn't read intelligence stats on sea monsters, so I had no idea how a blue jellyfish managed to bewilder Windsor in the first place. I handed the stone to her anyway and watched her learn it, her affinity climbing another three points.

With the jellyfish dead, it was time for the part I'd been promising myself. I looked toward where No Cena had fled, still able to make out a faint shadow on the far water, and sent Little Blue tearing after her. No Cena was paddling along with Baru, smug as anything, certain she'd made the number one lord eat a silent loss.

She must have figured I'd be tangled up with that sea monster for ages, and that by the time I broke free, she'd be long gone. And the thought of my future fury delighted her. Things didn't go her way. She heard a sound, turned, and saw a blue whale hauling a boat closing fast. I'd caught her inside a few minutes.

Her rowing was simply too slow. I looked at her shocked, frightened face and gave her a cold smile. She tried to gut me and still thought she could outrun me. If she'd asked nicely back there, I'd have helped since I profit from killing CBS anyway, but she chose tricks instead. I called out that she hadn't expected me so soon.

Watching her flinch, I spoke the language of the Lord Continent, which I'd picked up on my first day here without trying. She was terrified now because my speed meant I'd shredded the crimson lotus jellyfish and she had nothing that could stand against that.

She started negotiating, offered me money, pointed out there was no reward for killing her, so she'd clearly never killed a lord and didn't know I'd inherit all her resources the moment she died or she'd never have said it. I asked her flatly how much she could offer, then leaned in close to Windsor's ear and murmured to find a moment and strike.

Windsor breathed back her understanding, her ears flushing red where my breath brushed her. No, Cena took the question as hope and named 500 white stone. Clearly a real sum to her. I was almost offended. A top 10 lord with a hero, and that was the whole of her treasury. I asked her if she was trying to bribe a beggar.

Just as Windsor was about to act, a third boat slid into view. A tier one build with two men paddling, and they plainly seen the standoff and were drifting closer. I frowned while Noina's face lit with hope. The newcomer used spirit eye, then froze when he realized he'd stumbled onto two top 10 lords, one of them me.

Before he could even greet us, No Cena yelled across the water that he should watch out that I was extorting her, robbing her. He nearly wept on the spot, having thought he'd found a heavy weight to latch on to, and instead walked between two tigers, terrified of being crushed in the middle. This was weighing mock, by the way, and I'd come to like the man more than I expected to.

I read his obvious despair, and no Cena kept twisting the knife, warning me that if I killed her, everyone would know I'd broken the unwritten rule against killing your own, that everyone's opinion of me would curdle.

I smiled because I didn't care in the slightest how people looked at me strong enough and they all look up regardless and I refused to believe I was the only spirit domain lord who'd figured out that killing a lord yields their resources so the rule was already a fiction.

Wang Mock begged off pleading that he was small fry with no meat on him that he'd leave and we could pretend he was nothing. No Cena wouldn't let him slip away calling out that I'd never let him go now that he'd witnessed me trying to make him my enemy by reflex. He stared at me frightened. I ignored him.

I'd had enough of No Cena and gave Windsor the look. No Cena was still grinning, sure I wouldn't move. When two light ropes snapped out and bound her and Varu fast. Only then did the fear truly hit her because she understood that the instant I acted, she was already dead and she regretted not simply handing over the money.

She didn't know it wouldn't have mattered. I told her plainly that the moment she tried to screw me, her death was decided and I finished her and borrow with two water arrows myself. I did it with my own hands on purpose. The way I'd kept the demon domain lords alive for me to kill. Training myself against the blood of my own kind.

The method worked. The discomfort had nearly faded. Wang Mock's legs gave out watching no Cena die without a word from me. When my eyes turned his way, he babbled that he'd seen nothing, knew nothing, would hand over everything good he owned, and started pulling items out fast. I felt a strange pull between laughing and pitying him.

I had no intention of silencing him, but among the things he was dumping out, I caught something I wanted. Before I could ask, a blue fox erupted from the sea and lunged at us. I grabbed Windsor and threw us both flat against the deck. And even so, the fox tore a chunk of flesh from my arm, blood pouring down at once.

Windsor's eyes went murderous, and she cast entangling spell before she'd even risen. But the fox slipped it. We scrambled up. I read the thing as we moved. Frost snow fox, purple quality, level eight, stats in the mid to high 50s, carrying freezing spell, ice shield, sharp claws, and bite. My stomach dropped at those numbers, and I genuinely worried whether Windsor could handle it.

Wang Mach looked stunned senseless, convinced his luck had finally run out, that surviving me only to meet a purple sea beast meant certain death. He'd seen the fox's stats, too, and even knowing Windsor was a legendary hero, he couldn't picture her, level four, and me, level three, beating a level eight purple monster. He'd already started cursing me internally, certain that if I hadn't killed Nosina, we'd have had a chance.

And now we'd all die for it. He didn't understand Windsor at all. Windsor wasn't thinking about any of that. All she felt was rage that she'd failed to guard her lord, that I'd been hurt shielding her. She went berserk. Two light arrows flew at the frost snow fox, which raised an ice shield and turned them aside.

Then the fox cast freezing spell, ice creeping up Windsor's body and spreading, and she answered by pouring out light element heat to burn it back. She followed with bewilder, then two entangling spells. The fox's mind stuttered for a heartbeat, just long enough for two light ropes to seize it. Her base strength was 40. Her staff added 30% and the entangling spell stacked another 30% on top, pushing the binding near 68 points.

The fox couldn't break loose at all. In that frozen window, she fired light arrow after light arrow and killed it outright. A flash of purple light marking the drop. She didn't even glance at the loot. She came straight to me, eyes rimmed red, and laid healing over my arm, apologizing that she'd been careless, that I'd had to protect her.

I just stared at her. That string of moves had genuinely stunned me. I knew she was strong, but I hadn't grasped how cleanly and efficiently she could erase an enemy, and it was obvious the two new skills had transformed her combat power.

Watching her blame herself, I reached out and stroked her hair and told her it was nothing, a scratch, and that if the fox had hit her instead, it would have been serious.

I wasn't about to pass up an affinity boost while comforting her, sincere as the comfort was, she still looked guilty, but my gaze softened her, and she swore inwardly to be more careful, while I pocketed the fox's skill stone without checking it and turned back to Wang Mock. Wang Mock was rattled, half wondering if Windsor's display had been a hallucination.

Stunned that she was that strong. When he met my eyes, he jolted upright and blurted that I should just name what I wanted, that he'd never refuse, that he only hoped to be spared. I told him I was interested in the flower producer he'd flashed earlier and to name his price. He offered to just give it to me, no charge.

Relieved that wanting to buy meant I didn't mean to kill him. I cut him off and told him to set a price because I needed him to do something for me afterward. Namely, spread my story through the chat channel. Killing no Cena and a purple sea beast in the same stretch would make people fear me and save me a mountain of future trouble.

Trading fairly instead of robbing him would tell everyone I didn't bully the weak and was someone worth dealing with, which meant the people sitting on good items might come find me to sell. Wang Mock decided no Cena must have had a private grudge with me since I was clearly letting him walk and named a 100 white stone for the flower producer.

He admitted later it could fetch more being a steady food income and that he'd only sat on it because he just looted it and building it was too pricey for someone at his level. I didn't haggle, just close the deal. Not out of generosity, but to manufacture exactly the reputation I wanted. The open-handed buyer making a public show of generosity.

I brought him along as we kept moving. The demon domain nearly closed, happy to let him scavenge scraps in my wake and absorb a deeper sense of my strength. By the time the teleportation date reappeared, he was looking at me with something like worship.

Parting reluctantly, far richer than a day of solo grinding would ever have made him, and quietly certain that a good man like me would only have killed no Cena if she'd wronged me first. People believe the story they want to believe. Back in my own waters, I started sorting. First, the fox's skill stone, which I used at once.

Freezing spell, purple quality, boosting the user's freezing strength by 40%. Purple skills really were obscene. Nearly half again is strong, and that percentage would only compound as my stats grew. Next, I spent white stone buying the materials to upgrade the ship, then read the new readout. My wooden boat had hit level four, 20 m long, and nine wide with a cabin, toilet, living quarters, warehouse, and fishing deck.

No power yet. Running ship drainage technology at level one. Three facilities had been added. The warehouse, a larger wooden house, and the fishing deck. My old room had swollen. Two new rooms had appeared beside it. And for once, the buildings didn't eat the whole deck, leaving a meter and a half walkway roofed by boards that finally kept the rain off me while I fished.

The next level wanted 500 wood, 800 iron nails, and 50 copper block. A lot, but reachable. I couldn't push it yet, though. Little Blue was already straining to tow this thing, so I had to solve the power problem before I dared add more weight. I sent Windsor to fish. Spent some white stone bumping her rod to level 12.

Then got told it couldn't go higher. So quality set the ceiling. Green capped at level 12, white at level six, and I figured talent worked the same way that my epic talent would top out at level 24 and rise no further without a quality upgrade. Then I went back inside to count my gains.

Setting aside what I just spent, I still held 9,982 white stone, nearly a full gold stone, the largest sum I'd ever owned. Beyond that, there were four territory core, four ship core, four green fishing rod, 97 plastic, and four green ore blueprint.

I'd also stripped a white wall blueprint and a green 13 green arrow tower blueprint, and a blue one, a tailor shot blueprint, a white malicious soldier nest, a pile of unenchanted weapons, and assorted food. All of it taken from three demon domain lords and no Cena from the sea monsters.
