A fat hall and the white stone mattered most. Wang Mock had carried a little over 800, and I sat on more than 10 times that. I burned the summon cards and pulled three commoners, two men and a woman, and only the woman caught my eye. My excellent quality level one singledigit stats. Profession chef with the simple ability to make delicious food.

Commoners had no skills, only a profession and whatever ability came with it. So, the two men, both listed with no profession at all, were just hands. I gave each of the three a fishing rod and sent them to the deck. Then, I opened the regional channel and found fewer than 7,000 people left in my area. The losses this round still brutal.

The world channel was louder, full of people comparing demon domain halls. One bragging about hundreds of white stone, another about summoning a hero, and a third wailing that a level five monster had killed his only commoner and left him bailing water in place begging for a loan. Someone noticed Noina had dropped off the points list and wondered aloud if she died, and the channel sent her off with mock morning.

Then the merit ranking went live. I opened it and sat first with 183 merit points. Bake Seol held second with 132. Lee Kio third with 72. Danton fourth with 69. Yay Bake Pong fifth with 67. And Hagawa sixth with 52. Bake all surprised me. She ranked seventh on the survival board just under No Cena. And here she'd shot to second on merit with that score.

It made sense once I thought about it since merit only comes from killing lords and not everyone runs into lords. So luck plays a heavy hand. I'd simply gotten luckier. The channel crowned me an insurmountable mountain among the newcomers, and Hagawa Hina snapped back that I was just lucky to have met more lords, that he'd have outmarited me with the same chances.

The crowd buried him, mocking the Joe Kingdom's pride for being jealous, calling him not even a fraction of me until he was cursing the whole channel. I smiled and closed it since you lose if you stare at a clown long enough, and tomorrow's survival points would shut every mouth anyway. Then I opened technology and started spending.

500 white stone built ship thermal energy technology and an upgrade option appeared behind it costing a thousand. So I poured another thousand into pushing ship drainage technology to level two. When I tried to light up ship power technology, it demanded a prerequisite, ship automation technology. So I went hunting for that instead.

Lighting it up cost a,000 white stone, which had me grumbling that I'd be in trouble if I ran dry before the power tech. But I clicked it anyway, then paid 500 more to build it. Pleasantly surprised it wasn't worse. After that, I went back to light up the ship power technology itself, which wanted 2,000.

I actually swore out loud and asked the system if it was robbing me, but the power problem had to be solved. So, I lit it. Instead of a build button, a drop down unfolded, a whole menu of options, single or propulsion power, multi or linkage propulsion power, single screw, multi-crew, on and on, each one carrying its own lightup cost behind it.

I nearly cursed again. If this were a merchant, I'd have left him a one-star review and reported him for good measure. Single ore lit up for 200, multi-ore for 500, and the screw options refused to light at all. Conditions not met. I chose multi-re linkage propulsion power, lit it, then spent another 500 to build it, and the power problem was finally solved.

The whole technology spree cost me a full 6,000 white stone. Playing with technology really is just setting money on fire. I pulled on a raincoat and went out to see what had changed. The drainage circle had grown a little larger. Red patterns now lace the entire hole, and the most obvious addition was 10 oes, five to a side.

I started the ship, and the oars began rowing themselves, the boat sliding forward. The speed couldn't match Little Blue's old towing pace, but it wasn't slow. And the catch was that it only slid in whatever direction I picked with no obstacle avoidance and no turning. Both of which were locked behind more technology, which was to say more paying to win.

No wonder everyone back on Blueest Star always said the only way to get stronger in a game was to open your wallet. Three more mouths meant the next priority was the seawater converter. So I checked its upgrade cost. Plastic I had plenty of, but wood, iron block, and copper block had all gone into the ship.

So I spent 20 white stone on 10 wood and pushed it to 4 L of water a day. The next tier wanted 20 plastic, 30 wood, and 30 white stone. And I took it, reaching 10 L daily. I bought more wood and upgraded again since 10 L for five people was tight once you counted cooking and stopped at 20 L a day, plenty.

After that, I took out the flower producer blueprint I bought from Wang Mach and built it, buying the missing materials from my personal shop. And a strange thing appeared, a cylinder of golden yellow sitting on a tapered base with a small door for collecting the flour.

According to the description, it needed no raw ingredients at all, simply pulling molecules from the air to synthesize 3 kg of flour a day, which struck me as wonderfully high tech. I upgraded at one level for 480 white stone, doubling output to 6 kg, then boked at the next tier's 960 and stopped.

Next, I built the cooler I transformed that morning, meaning to keep it in the warehouse for preserving the mountain of food and vegetables I'd looted. It lowered temperature by 2°. So, I upgraded it level after level to level four and 8° of cooling. The materials climbing each step.

When everything was done, I had 2,327 whit stone left with the seawater converter at level four, the flower producer at level two, and the cooler at level four. I nodded, satisfied. I filled the converter, bought 20 wood, and went to work like a man who'd finally remembered he could build a home.

Four wood became two large barrels for water and flour, three wood a table, two wood for benches, one wood the chopsticks and cutlery, and six wood a single bed in a double bed. The single for my cabin and the double for the living room. 30 white stone bought straw and cloth to line them. I carried the cooler into the warehouse and packed all the food and vegetables inside.

Then thought it through, hauled up a barrel of sea water, and used freezing spell to make ice and dropped the room's temperature further, stopping only when the cold bit at me. The chill felt right. Outside, the sky was all rain, but it had clearly started to darken. So, I called Mory and told her to start dinner. Windsor and I, having already eaten lunch.

While she cooked, I took her rod and sat fishing beside Windsor, who smiled at me, soft and easy, and we talked a while as I worked in the question I kept turning over where exactly these summoned people came from. What she told me cleared up a real curiosity. The summoned weren't foreigners at all. They were the native people of the Lord continent, descendants of first generation lords like me.

For their own reasons, they'd contracted with Chano, entered a special space, sealed themselves away, and waited to be called by a new lord. Windsor had signed her own contract because of an incurable disease. When I pressed for more, she had nothing, no memory of any of it. Shano having wiped everything but the single reason each of them chose to sign.

So the goddess kept them blank on purpose. Just enough self left to explain the bargain, nothing more. Mory finished cooking scrambled eggs with tomatoes and a hot and sour fish, and the five of us cleared every dish over bread and steamed buns. I left Mory to the washing and had the two men carry the beds and straw and cloth into the living room to sleep.

Then I set Windsor to releasing light element heat to charge the boat's thermal technology and told my to keep a fire burning because with no sunlight and a low temperature, the ship's stored heat couldn't carry us through a whole night of sailing. It didn't take long for Windsor's mana to run dry, and waiting on its recovery wouldn't fill the gap either, which told me she couldn't fish at all the next day.

I just have to park her indoors as a living charging treasure. For tonight, we'd go as far as the heat allowed. I told little Blue to swim escort behind the boat, set us sailing, and turned in with Windsor while Mory slept on the single bed in our room. Tonight, we rested down to our inner layers.

When I suggested we shed the outer clothes, Windsor's neck went red, but she didn't refuse, just undressed slowly and shily, still keeping a white inner lining while I stripped to almost nothing myself. I drew her in and breathed the faint clean scent in her freshly washed hair, her body warm and soft against me, deeply content, glad I'd bought the toiletries off the trade channel the day before.

She looked up at me with a face full of color, and I knew she was thinking about the wound I'd taken saving her, her eyes brimming so full of tenderness they seemed about to spill over. I lowered my head and kissed her forehead and told her gently to rest, that the day's fighting must have exhausted her.

She murmured that she wasn't tired, that I was the one who'd been hurt because of her, and gripped my arm tighter, as if she wanted to burrow straight into me.

I told her that you can't fight out on the sea and come home untouched, that getting hurt was ordinary, that she had to stop blaming herself, and she finally answered that she understood, the guilt easing out of her voice before she closed her eyes and pressed her face into my chest. I stroked her hair and turned my mind to the afternoon's materials.

Four people fishing, plus that level 12 rod had pulled in a solid pile of white stone. So, I bumped the other five rods to level four, leaving me six rods total. The gift one, the talent one from the first night, and four looted off a demon domain lord. That left me 24,472 white stone. And then came the part I'd been waiting for, the talent.

First, the underwear blueprint, which I'd never bothered building since the trade channel overflowed with cheap clothes. So, I ran 100 transformations on it and got a trajectory fixed point teleportation blueprint, a one-time tool that records your movement path and lets you jump back to any point along it just once.

Not much use this second, but it made a fine escape charm, so I used it, watching a line trace itself across the blueprint as my trajectory began recording. Then I took the blue water cup and used my talent with real hope. And out came a purple pet card. Excellent. Little blue, summoned from a blue card, had carried me through everything, so a purple one should be better still.

I used it, and a wisp of red flame bloomed in the air, spreading and gathering until a 20-cm spirit took shape. A tiny figure in a little red dress, long red hair, red eyes, fair skin, small wings at her back, a little red staff and hand. She opened her eyes, saw me, and broke into a delighted smile, flying over to land on the quilt.

I poked her soft cheek, then touched her arm, warm as a hand warmer, and read her information. Fire elemental spirit, purple quality, level five stats in the high30s, carrying fireball, blazing body, and firebinding. Three skills in level five, even with little blue. This morning's demon domain battles had pushed both Little Blue and Windsor up a level to five, while I sat at level 32 experience short of leveling tomorrow.

Honestly, I wasn't desperate to level. What I wanted was talent quality. But the things that raised it were so rare. I'd never once seen one on the trade channel. The only such item I owned having come from Li Chung. I named the fire elemental spirit Huawa. Plain and direct as always. What Huawa really gave me wasn't combat power, but a better charger for the boat.

A way to keep sailing through the dark. I sent her to the fishing pier to cast blazing body and feed heat into the ship. And she kissed my cheek and flapped out on her little wings. That left the question of what to spend my talent on. Everything in my space was white or green.

The lone blue being an arrow tower blueprint I didn't want to burn since I'd need defenses once I had a territory. Remembering the 10% discount coupon I'd earned the day before, I spent a 100 white stone converting to a purple stone, then ran 100 transformations on it with high hopes and got a purple crystal, a precious elemental crystal for leveling and advancing elemental spirits.

I turned the irregular rather beautiful thing over in my hand, frowning, so elemental spirits couldn't level by killing monsters. If that was true, Hua would be miserable to raise since I couldn't conjure crystals daily and couldn't even guarantee a crystal each time I transformed. Annoying, but I'd been here only 3 days and knew too little, so I sheld it.

I opened the chat channel, my nightly ritual, and found the world channel chewing over me. Someone swore he'd watched through a looted telescope as I chased Noina down and killed her. No doubt about it. Hagawa pounced, calling me vicious for turning on my own, claiming I'd hunted her because killing a lord hands over every resource.

A few players, the ones who'd never met a lord, were genuinely shocked to learn that was even possible. Half of them envious and half of them grateful they hadn't since meeting a stronger lord just means serving yourself up. Hagawahina kept dragging the topic back, demanding I come explain why I had broken the agreement and kill the spirit domain lord.

And Danton joined in to stir the pot. And still, Hagawa taunted, asking if my silence meant I was afraid to admit it. So I answered. I typed that yes, I'd killed No Cena and asked Hagawa whether he wanted to avenge her and whether he had the strength to. Pure mockery, no explanation because I knew that nothing I said would land.

That an accusation only loses its sting when someone else clears it for you. He got angrier, didn't take my bait, and instead started telling people not to trade with me, painting me a fierce and evil man they'd all be cheated by. The channel split. Some bought that I'd killed for resources and called me cruel.

Others pushed back, asking how my score could possibly hinge on No Cena scraps when it towered so high. And a third group insisted you couldn't even find a lord in the spirit domain. Three days in and they'd never seen one. The argument churned. Nobody settled. Then Wang Mock stepped in and said it had been a personal grudge between me and Noina, not a snatch for resources, and that he'd been there.

He didn't know my real reason, but he reasoned that if I'd been after loot, he'd be dead, too. And he attached a photo he'd secretly snapped of Windsor and me cutting through sea beasts, a shock he'd meant to keep as a souvenir. The picture detonated the channel. People decided I was handsome, gushed over Windsor as a god-level beauty.

While a serv voice barked that the point was our strength, that we could both wield magic. Haguahina tried one last jab, asking what if Wang Mo was my accomplice and got buried again because everyone could see that if I'd truly killed for treasure, I'd simply have silenced him, not paid him. They called Hagawa jealous and moved on.

Then Wang Mock thanked me openly for letting him tag along and pick up enough scraps to earn a heap of white stone and added that I was generous and didn't haggle. I went a little speechless at that. I'd wanted him promoting me, sure, but the line about me never haggling boxed me in since how would I save face haggling with the next buyer?

I thought it over and decided Face wasn't worth much. I'd haggle anyway. People started peppering Wang Mo with questions about how strong Windsor and I really were. Hagawa Hina and Danton and Bakes all curious and he answered carefully, calling Windsor a legendary hero who killed purple sea monsters like a game, withholding the exact level numbers. The man handled things well.

I closed the channel pleased, settled Windsor comfortably against me, and slept. The fourth day opened with Windsor's light golden eyes fixed on my face, unblinking, and the instant I woke, she shut them, and played asleep. I found her endlessly entertaining, so I teased her, told her to quit pretending that I'd already seen her, that she could look all she liked for free.

But hearing that she nearly died of embarrassment at being caught. And yet curiosity won. So she opened her eyes and asked what I meant by butt. I looked down at her beautiful face close enough that I could feel her breath and told her that she'd have to give me a kiss. Then kissed her soft lips, laughed and got up to dress, leaving her hidden under the blanket.

She touched her own mouth beneath it, heart pounding, her thoughts a sweet tingle, smiling without noticing she was smiling. I told my to make breakfast and washed up, then collected the flour and water and topped off the sea water converter. After that, I took the resource fishing rod, dragged a stool to the platform, and started the day's fishing.

And Huawa flew over to perch on my shoulder, looking worn out from her night of charging. I handed her the purple crystal from yesterday and told her to eat well and rest well.

Her two small hands strained to hold it since fist size was huge against her 20 cm frame, and she breathed the thread of flame to roast it slowly, then licked the melted purple liquid up with her little pink tongue, eyes squinting in contentment. The sight made me smile. The little one had a real healing quality to her.

The rod tugged and I reeled in a silver treasure chest, opened it, and a barrel of mixed fruit appeared on the deck. Dozens of pounds of it, more food than I'd ever pulled at once. I grabbed an apple and bit into it. Tossed one each to the two commoners beside me. And they thanked me at once. Face is bright.

I knew their loyalty wasn't maxed, though I couldn't read the number and only judged by feel. Probably another of Chon Mo's tools for training rookie lords. It didn't worry me. I only needed my heroes loyal since a territory once built would run on heroes managing things and there was no way I could do everything myself. I ate fruit and teased Huawa and the next catch was a letown just 10 plastic.

By then my had finished cooking and we sat to eat. Windsor's expression nearly normal now only her darting eyes and faintly red ears giving away the storm behind them. I let her be. Afterward everyone had fruit and I sent the commoner to carry the rest of the warehouse.

I'd meant to keep Windsor indoors charging the ship today, but plans been a to circumstance, and with Huawa handling the heat, there was no need to waste her, especially since her level 12 rod pulled up far better catches. Five people, five stools, five rods, and beyond the resource rod, I still had one spare sitting idle since every rod needed its own hands.

Huawa, done with her crystal, had curled against my neck and fallen asleep on my shoulder. Windsor kept stealing envious glances, having gone stareyed the second she saw Huawa that morning. Only Huawa clung so tightly to me that she'd never gotten so much as a hug. I couldn't quite take the look, so I lifted Huawa off my shoulder and handed her over.

Windsor cradled her carefully, then hugged her close, her heart all but melting at the tiny sleeping face. Her whole mood gone soft and lovely. I shook my head, smiling. Huawa had charmed me, but Windsor had fallen for her completely. My last resource cast of the day brought up a fertile soil, green quality, multiplying crop growth tenfold. Useless out here, but perfect for a territo's planting beds later.

It nudged a thought loose that I should buy seeds now while they were trash to everyone since their price would surely climb once territory started rising. So, I browsed the trade channel and spent a 100 white stone on every kind of seed I could find. Vegetables, fruits, grains, all of the dirt cheap at roughly a stack per whit stone.

And even at that price, almost nobody else was buying. I had a commoner move the seeds to the warehouse and switch to my ordinary rod since the day looked empty otherwise. Of course, the moment I thought that Chon Mo proved me wrong. She pushed out three global announcements in a row. The probability of rookie lords being attacked by sea beasts in the water had increased.

The drop rate from killing sea beasts had increased. The personal shop had updated so it would now buy anything, not just fish. And she closed with a line about opportunity hiding inside danger, urging everyone to build a territory soon. The channel erupted, and I ignored the noise to chew on what those three lines actually meant. The first one mattered for a phrase I almost skimmed past.

Those words about sea beasts in the water. Why specify in the water at all? Because right now those are the only attacks rookies face, which meant attacks were coming from elsewhere soon, and the one direction I was certain of was the sky. Far harder to handle than anything swimming.

The second announcement was plain, easier loot from kills, but it carried a hidden edge since sea beasts vanish once they drop items, which meant their corpses could no longer be sold for white stone. That fed straight into the third. The first two had been paving the road for it.

The personal shop used to buy only fish, and now it bought everything, which widened the ways a rookie could turn survival into white stone. Strung together, the message was exactly what Chanu said. Opportunity inside danger. Survive the sea beasts and you profit. Whether from the corpse or the drop, every kill convertible into stone for upgrades or trades.

Endure the attacks and you either grow stronger or end up in a fish's belly. The road to an island in a territory was a bloody one, and I was thrilled by it because here the strong only grow stronger. I wanted the attacks to come soon. Not too strong though, I added inwardly.

A friend message chimed and I opened it curious since I'd handed Wang Mach a friend slot when we parted and wondered what he wanted. He said a friend of his wanted to trade with me and attached an image. A white quality militia soldier nest that let you recruit militia units. White but acceptable. I asked why his friend didn't just list it on his own personal shop.

And Wangmock explained that after he'd mentioned our deal in the channel, the man figured I was generous and might pay more than the store would. So, they'd already decided I was a rich mark. Compared to the rest of them, I supposed I was. I asked the price and Wong Mach said he was only making the connection that I should talk to the seller directly, a player named Lim Hi.

I told Wang Mock yes and then handed him a real job. I asked him to put the word out in the channel that I was buying anything decent, that selling to me would beat selling to the store and to have interested players contact him first so he could compile a daily list and bring it to me each evening.

I'd missed it at first, but the shop update was about to flood the store with good items, and I didn't want every worthwhile thing disappearing into Chama's mom when I could collect it instead. Price be damned since right now these things could only be had from other players or pulled from the sea myself. Wang Mock lit up at being trusted with the task since it meant daily contact and a closer tie.

I added that he should take a commission that if something sold for 20 whit stone in the store and a player sold it to me for 30, he could keep a cut of that extra 10. His call how much? That gave him a profit motive to work hard. Spared me paying him separately and let me run the whole operation on other people's money.

Two birds with one stone, and I gave myself a private thumbs up for it. Wang Mach was ecstatic, reading it correctly as my support. And unlike Li Chong, he genuinely admired me rather than just chasing profit. The kind of devoted fan who gives without asking for anything back. I closed his box and found Limi and my friend applications since he'd already messaged confirming Wang Mock had said I wanted the Soldier Nest.

I told him to name a price, no pleasantries, and he offered it. A little nervous about asking above store value, but unwilling to leave money on the table.

Whatever number he landed on, the deal was already as good as done in my head because a soldier nest wouldn't be useful to me for a while yet, and the real value of the trade was the next stone laid in a reputation I was building, one generous handshake at a time. Lim He's counter offer asked for 10 more white stone, which wasn't outrageous.

And to me, 10 white stone barely registered as money anymore. Still, I didn't snap it up the moment he named it. Agree too fast and word gets around that you're not just rich, but stupidly rich, the kind of buyer everyone lines up to fleece. So, I told him I'd make it 108 white stone.

I said I'd originally meant to offer 105, but since he was the first person Wang Mach had ever introduced to me, he could keep three extra on the house. I added that the soldier nest wouldn't be useful to me for a while yet. So, if that price worked for him, he should go ahead and sell. I was confident he'd take it.

The thing went for an even hundred in the shop, so anything over that was free money landing in his lap, and giving only one or two more would have felt almost like an insult. Better to look open-handed than cheap. This wasn't a oneshot deal, either.

Lim would tell his friends about trading with me, and if I lowballed, some of them wouldn't bother haggling over a single white stone and would just dump their goods in the shop instead. Generosity now was advertising later. Limy's reply came back fast and pleased, the way I'd guessed it would. To a level three player still grinding fish for every coin.

Those extra stones were real effort saved and he closed the trade happily. A soldier nest dropped into my space and I went back to fishing. After the chaos of the first 3 days, sitting still and listening to the rain had its own quiet charm. The rain reminded me of the chat from breakfast.

The downpour had been heavy enough that level one drainage couldn't keep up, so plenty of players were still scooping water out by hand. Upgrading drainage to level two ran a thousand white stone and almost nobody had that kind of cash, which meant the world channel was half complaints and half grim survival reports. Folded in among the griping was the new survival ranking.

And after yesterday's hall, my points had jumped hard. Hagawa had slipped a place and a dark horse named Bake Seol came out of nowhere to see second. I pulled up the survival points leaderboard and skimmed it. I sat on top with 830. Bake seal behind me at 310. Then Danton at 290, Lee Kio and Yay Bake Pong tied at 280 and Hagawa trailing at 270.

The trip into the demon domain had scrambled everything below me. I closed the panel just as something hit my line. A wooden box this time, and I already knew not to expect treasure from one of those. Sure enough, three pieces of straw, good for nothing but feeding the stove at lunch. After that came the usual trickle of wood and copper blocks and the like.

I sold one of each in the shop and discovered the buy price and sell price for basic materials were identical, which made me wonder who runs a market where you can never turn a profit. Not my problem to solve. I just dumped my wood, copper blocks, and iron blocks straight into the shop so they wouldn't clog my space.

Noon crept up on me before I noticed. None of the five of us had pulled anything worth bragging about, and I started to wonder whether my good luck had burned itself out in the first 3 days. Then Windsor hooked something interesting, a weapon blueprint. Spirit Eye told me it was a crossbow bolt design, white quality, and it needed the blacksmith profession before anyone could even learn it.

Useless to me at the moment. I didn't mind in the slightest because every scrap like this was capital banked against the future. Once I found an island and raised a territory, all of it would detonate into real value at once and save me enormous effort. I let myself picture it for a second. Soldiers on demand, heroes on demand, gear and defensive towers whenever I wanted them, and the feeling was honestly fantastic.

Then I cut the daydream off because a dreamer who won't stand up and act should go stare at a wall and reflect on himself. What I actually wanted was noodles. I called my walked into the house, mostly to teach her since I couldn't keep cooking for myself every time a craving hit. Get her trained once and I could just order a bowl whenever the mood struck.

I poured flour into a wooden basin, added water, and worked it until most of the flour was damp, then stopped and kneaded it down into a smooth ball of dough. I rolled it out flat on the cutting board with a rolling pin. All of which I'd synthesized from a little wood off the panel, though I doubted many players would spend wood on cooking gear.

Then came cutting the noodles. They came out very white and even, the kind of pale that made me think of Windsor. I had my fetch eggs and vegetables from the warehouse because I meant to make the sauce first. There was only one pot, and if I boiled the noodles before the sauce was ready, they'd be cold and gummy by the time everything came together.

Tomatoes, eggs, green peppers into the pot. The smell spread fast and got my mouth watering before I'd even tasted it. I spooned out the sauce. Red and green shot through with golden egg, and it looked good enough to eat raw. I boiled the noodles, ladled the sauce over five bowls and called everyone in. The first bite hit me all at once.

The chew of the noodles, the freshness of the egg, the crisp, bright tang of tomato, and I couldn't help blurting out how good it was. I'd eaten hot meals these past days, sure, but they'd been pure fuel. This was the first one I'd cooked with any care, and only now did I feel the real pleasure of food.

Humans genuinely cannot live without carbs. Eating noodles and listening to the rain, I felt like I was back on Blue Star. I'd always liked noodles on a rainy day. The way the warmth settled something in my chest. After I finished, I drank down the noodle water thick and white and faintly sweet. And right then, I felt a tickle at my neck.

Huhawa had woken up and was nuzzling against me, so I stroked her tiny head, watching Windsor and the others wolf down their bowls. A strange warmth moved through me. I grew up an orphan on Blueest Star, raised on government aid, and I had never once known what a home felt like. Somehow, in this cruel world, I felt I had one.

I smiled to myself and quietly swore I'd build a territory. Work it hard. Raise something of my own. As if she caught the shift in my mood. Windsor gave me a bright smile. Little Blue let out a low cry from the water, and Huawa flew up to my face and wrapped both little hands around my head. I pressed my forehead to Huawa and gave a shake that set her giggling.

Then checked her panel. The purple crystal she'd eaten that morning had carried her to level six, lifting every stat by four points, with her lowest now sitting at 40. I'd leveled once myself during the morning's fishing and was at level four. My own panel had quietly changed overnight.

Beyond the bump in base stats, my freezing spell now showed up as a listed skill, and every skill carried a quality tag I hadn't seen before. An adjustment from the morning announcement that Chan Mo apparently hadn't bothered to flag for me. I read down the line. Level four with 28 experience toward 400 strength 17 with a bonus 10 stacked on top.

Agility 17, defense 17, wisdom 18, mana 180 with 18 added. Hero ice element messenger. My skills ran spirit eye water arrow technique in green. Freezing spell in purple with a green staff equipped and 100 transformations marked epic. I wasn't thrilled. My stats had started criminally low at level one, a permanent handicap, and there was nothing to do but live with it for now.

After lunch, Mory stayed to clean up while the rest of us went back to the afternoon's fishing. I left Huawei inside to keep pouring out blazing body and topping off the ship's heat. Now and then, I'd haul in a load of basic materials, selling wood and iron and copper straight to the shop and keeping the odd thing like plastic.

Then I cracked open a black iron box and got a small jolt of pleasure when a blueprint slid out. Spirit eye named it a window blueprint. Two wood and six glass to build. The whole point being you'd no longer feel boxed in and could look at the sea from your bed. A bit of a letdown, honestly. Such a trivial little thing.

I tucked it away. I had no glass on hand and saw no reason to burn white stone buying any just to cut a window so it could wait until I fished enough up on my own. I cast again and reset my head fast because this was the norm for everyone who fished. Good things did not come easy.

Almost everything worthwhile I'd pulled so far had come from the resource fishing rod and my 100 transformations, not the plane rod. And if Chion Mo hadn't handed me that compensation draw at the start to give me a head start, I'd be scraping by exactly like the rest of them.

Just then, Windsor let out a soft exclamation about something being cute, and I turned to find her hugging a white plush unicorn half a meter tall, beaming over it. I hadn't pegged her for the tide to melt over a stuffed toy. She always read as gentle and easily flustered. Nothing more, and the little glimpse of girishness caught me off guard.

The sky went dark before I knew it, so I sent my to start dinner and called it a day. The only real prize was the white quality soldier nest I bought off Lim He. We cooked, ate, undressed, and turned in. Windsor and I were close enough now that she wouldn't shy from a little quiet contact.

But with Mory sharing the room, she kept stiff and bashful and uneasy, which made me itch to upgrade the boat. Pushing it to level five would run around 2,300 white stone and basically drain my funds. So, I hesitated. Income off pure fishing was too slow. Anyway, today I'd cleared a little over 400, and that was only because Windsor's rod was level 12 and five of us were casting at once.

With a single rod, clearing 200 a day would count as a good hall. 400 a day was a fortune to most people, but I wasn't satisfied. I had a lead and I needed to hold it and widen it because if I sat still, others would close the gap sooner or later. I wasn't the only one who'd ever catch a lucky break.

Bakes sudden climb up the survival board was proof enough that adventures found other people, too. Tomorrow, I'd hunt for some way to grow my income. For tonight, there was the essential ritual, opening blind boxes with my talent. Every use of hundred transformations felt like cracking a mystery box, except this one always paid out something good, so I never tired of it.

First up was the white wall blueprint. I had two, one green and one white, so I burned the white one. 100 transformations turned it into a green ring, plus five defense and 10% agility, the accessory class, which around here covered three rings, a necklace, and a belt for men or a hairpin for women. I slid it onto my own finger without a second thought since you can never own too much defense.

Next, I tried a purple stone just to see what it became, and it stubbornly turned back into a purple crystal. A little disappointing. I'd half hoped for a discount coupon or something, but never mind. Huawa could eat it in the morning. For the last use, I dithered between the window blueprint and the fertile soil.

The window was white and low value with correspondingly low odds of a good transformation, while the green fertile soil promised much better. I gathered enough glass that afternoon to just build the window outright, and a window would lift my mood on a long voyage. So, I leaned towards saving the glass route and feeding the soil to my talent.

Right then, Wangmoke forwarded the day's list of players hoping to trade with me. And one entry made me very glad I hadn't burned the fertile soil yet. A planting space box, green quality, with a small interior space for growing plants. That was real gold. Drop the fertile soil inside and I'd have a steady supply of vegetables. I told Wang Mo I wanted it and to have the seller reach out to me.

A player called Bang Wool messaged almost at once, opening with a string of flattery and a greeting to the boss. I told him I was interested and to name his price. He said it sold for 200 whit stone in the personal shop and asked for 240 since the thing was precious and at least he was direct about it.

So, I haggled. I granted it was precious, but pointed out it only grew plants, and by the time anything was ripened on my plate, two months out, I might already have found an island. He chewed on that, agreed I had a point, but didn't want to sell low and lob the question back, asking what I'd offer. I said I wouldn't lowball him.

220, 10% overshop. Fair enough. He decided to take the deal while it was good and tossed in a little extra flattery, promising to find me again next time he had something worthwhile. I just smiled and let it slide. After enough of the over-the-top graveling that clogged the World Channel, I built up a thick skin, an ordinary flattery couldn't move me at all anymore.

I'd lost a whole flavor of life experience embarrassingly young. The trade closed and a box arrived a meter long, half a meter on the other sides, solid black. I opened it and found the interior space was no joke, a full 100 square me. I poured all the fertile soil in and planted cabbage, leafy greens, and chili peppers.

Cabbage and greens ripened in 60 days, peppers in 80. But with the soil's ten-fold growth bonus folded in, I'd be eating in about a week, which I could live with. I shoved the box into a corner of the room, climbed back under the quilt, hugged Windsor, and slid a hand inside her clothes to the warm, smooth skin beneath.

Now that the fertile soil was gone, what should the rest of my hundred transformations target the window? Again, then my eye landed on an apple sitting on the bedside table. And I thought, why not try this? It struck me I'd never run my talent on food before and maybe there'd be a surprise well worth one chance to find out.

So, I triggered hundred transformations on it. The apple in my hand didn't change at all, which baffled me since I already knew the talent refused to work on people or pets. And now it seemed maybe plants were off limits, too. I used spirit eye on it instead, and the reading floored me. A primal apple, white, that randomly raised one or two attribute points when eaten.

I broke into a grin, not expecting an actual windfall, a rare treasure I could just bite into. I'd meant to save a seed and plant it, but the second I took a bite, the whole apple dissolved into a wisp of white air and flowed into my mouth, leaving nothing in my hand. So much for overthinking it. My luck held and it gave me two points all dumped into strength.

If I could pull an attribute fruit out of my talent every single day, I wouldn't even be picky. White ones would do, and I'd skyrocket without ever bothering to level. I knew perfectly well I was daydreaming again. I skimmed the world channel before sleep and saw more deaths that day, mostly from being attacked, which was a reminder to stay careful myself.

I drifted off with Windsor in my arms, and in the dream I'd built a thriving territory, a luxurious palace, a lavish bed, Windsor honest with me at last, and beside her, a few other beauties besides, beastiered girls and the like. I woke on the fifth morning, savored the dream, and then my face froze solid, expressionless.

I bought a fresh pair of underwear off the trading channel, and changed out of the pair I had on. Windsor caught sight of the state I was in, went scarlet, and despite herself, burst out laughing. The laugh stung and out of halfreal annoyance, I pounced on her, raining kisses across her face until I reached her lips, my hands starting to wander.

After a while of that, I got up, walked past my cooking with slightly quickened breath, and headed outside, leaving Windsor flushed and gasping on the bed. I came out to the fishing corridor, and Huawa flew up to greet me while two commoners were already working their rods.

I scooped Huawa up, took out the resource fishing rod, sat down, and handed her the purple crystal, which she took happily before tipping up to kiss my chin. Windsor came out before long and settled beside me, a little afraid to meet my eyes. After a stretch of silence, she said in a tiny voice that she was sorry, she hadn't meant to laugh at me.

My face darkened since she'd hit exactly the sore spot. A young man full of energy, holding a great beauty in his arms night after night, was bound to let some of it go. I turned and gave her a mock glare and threatened to eat her tonight, pure teasing. Since with Mory in the room, I had no intention of putting on a live broadcast.

Windsor, of course, didn't know I was joking and thought I meant it. Her heart pounded and she fretted over whether she'd resist if I actually made a move tonight before deciding she absolutely had to. With Mory right there, she put on a fierce look and snapped that no, Mory was still here.

I pushed it, asking lightly whether that meant it was fine once my wasn't around, which left her so flustered she didn't know how to answer, only managing a defiant little huff that she wouldn't let me get my way. So, I told her I was only teasing and to look how scared she'd gotten because if I didn't spell it out, she'd stew over it all day.

I knew the truth better than she did. Windsor's affinity toward me sat at 85. The last few points earned through everyday closeness and play, and I figured we'd need 90 before we could truly be honest with each other. My words settled her a good deal, though she stayed sulky, and I just smiled and let it be. The day's first catch came up then, and I hoped for a decent start.

It was a civilian summon card, white, which I used right away. A male commoner appeared, and his panel was another blank slate with no profession at all. I handed him the last spare rod and pointed him at the water. With no idle rods left and one more body aboard, housing was going to get tight. So, for now, the three commoners could squeeze into the living quarters together.

And once the boat upgraded, I'd rearrange them and finally get rid of Mory's awkward placement. While I was thinking it over, the rod jumped again, and I pulled up fertilizer white, the note promising it doubled crop growth speed. I carried it inside and scattered it over the planting box, which meant vegetables in 3 or 4 days instead of a week.

Soon, another blueprint surfaced. An ice cream maker in green, five wood, and two copper, and three plastic. The kind of thing that improves life without doing anything useful in a crisis. Maybe I could churn ice cream and sell it later, though I doubted many buyers existed while most players were still clinging to the survival line. I put it away.

The rain was a true downpour today, and I could already see my level two drainage barely keeping pace. A thin film of water always left behind. If it grew any heavier, I'd be bailing by hand. I decided that if nothing good turned up by evening, this ice cream maker would feed my talent that night. After fishing, I checked my balance.

A little over 2,600 white stone. Then open technology and search diving. I meant to start actively hunting in the sea because pure fishing brought in too little a day. The search threw a long string of entries at me. Ship diving technology, the weakened version, diving detection, diving weapon, and more. I clicked the full ship diving technology, which gave the ship the ability to submerge and came bundled with a shield.

Then hit light up and saw it wanted 5,000 white stone just to unlock. I quietly closed it, deciding a poor man like me didn't deserve it yet. Lighting it alone was already brutal and even unlocked, I couldn't have afforded to build and upgrade it. So, I clicked the weakened version instead, which only cost a thousand to light up.

That I could swallow. Then, I hit construct and it asked for 1500 white stone, which I paid without flinching because a harvest demands investment, and if I'd had the cash, I'd have unlocked the full version outright. The weakened ship diving technology granted 1 hour of diving per day. 1 hour. I checked the next upgrade, and it ran 3,000, double the cost, which told me technology was a bottomless money pit.

After all that, I had barely over a hundred white stone left. I didn't dive immediately since I'd planned on the afternoon anyway, and one hour a day left no reason to rush. I swapped back to the rod and kept fishing, occasionally landing a fish that dropped a basic material or two when killed. The drop rate noticeably kinder than the first couple of days.

The boxes I pulled were almost all food, with water oddly rare, and I never did understand why water bothered to be so scarce. Chatting with Windsor and teasing Huawa, the morning slid into noon. After lunch, I had Mory and the three commoners each grab a piece of attributless gear from the warehouse, then triggered the diving technology. A streamlined white shield instantly wrapped the boat.

It looked protective, but I knew it only sealed out the sea water and offered no defense whatsoever. I started the boat down into the dark. I didn't go deep this time because the deeper you went, the nastier the sea beasts. And unlike last time, I had no proper protective shield to fall back on. Having a real shield changes everything.

With one you needn't fear ambush and you can sit and recover mana to your heart's content while without one someone has to stand watch every second which grinds you down fast. The moment we submerged a school of fish swarmed us all white quality level one and two things fully red about 40 cm long blowing bubbles that left a sticky film wherever they brushed skin.

The five of us my included struck back while Windsor held her power in reserve for any real surprise. Little Blue threw itself in, swallowing mouthfuls with every bite and easing the pressure of the encirclement because the sheer number of fish would have been a headache without him thinning them. I leaned mostly on water arrow and dropped the occasional freezing spell.

And I noticed the freezing spell had no range cap at all. So with enough mana, I could freeze a wide stretch of water. Only the damage stayed fixed, scaling off my strength while the range kept expanding. The drops beat last time handily, something flaking off nearly every kill, mostly basic stuff like wood. And by the time the school scattered, I banked around a hundred white stone.

I smiled at that because hunting was clearly the answer. Fishing alone was just too slow. Before long, I ran into two blue sea beasts, green all over, long and flat with black stripes and red eyes. Black striped redeyed fish, blue, level 9. Every core stat around 49, packing arc lightning, electric net, and whip.

One look told me I couldn't take them, so I had Windsor handle it while I sat to recover mana. The two fish opened their mouths and loosed electric arcs at all of us. Windsor threw up a light shield to block the ark, and the fish exploited the moment she was busy to close on the boat fast.

My others crouched behind the corridor railing, firing arrows, but the fish were too quick to hit. They reached the hull, one swinging its tail to whip at Windsor, while the other flung a black electric net at Mory and the rest. The pair even coordinated, having clearly read how dangerous Windsor was, so one pinned her down while the other went for the four soft targets first.

Windsor saw the fish swinging at her and simply ignored it. Instead casting light shield over Mory and the other four to stop the net cold. The fish that lunged at her never landed its blow because a rope of red flame snared it. And though it tore free of Huawa's binding quickly, that delay was all Windsor needed.

She cast bewilder on the one harassing Mory's group, then put a light arrow through the one still ripping loose from the flame and killed it outright. On the far side, with that second fish dazed by Bewilder, Huawa hit it with a head-sized fireball and Windsor, handsfree now, finished it with a light arrow. Their first real collaboration ran like they drilled it for weeks.

Eerily smooth. Two sea beasts down without much trouble. I'd been genuinely afraid of casualties when those things went for my commoners. So seeing Windsor and Huawa clean it up themselves left me limp with relief. I stroked Huawa's little head with my thumb and told her she was amazing and she tipped her head back narrowed her eyes and made the smugest little face that said she knew it.

I handed her over to Windsor since after fighting side by side the two of them had warmed to each other. I sold one of the fish and the other dropped a blueprint, a salt field manufacturing blueprint. Blue 50 stone to build, which dried sea water into salt with a cheerful note that living things can't go without it.

A solid prize. With my tiny crew, buying salt off the market covered us for now. But once I had a territory, I'd be feeding not just troops, but a whole population. And none of them could survive without salt either. This blueprint meant that early on at least, my territory would never sweat its salt supply.

I let Windsor and Huawa start recovering mana while the rest of us, Little Blue included, pushed on hunting. Then I spotted an underwater peak rising ahead, chasing the extreme ice giant bear. Last time, I passed a peak just like it, but run out of time and missed it entirely, and I hadn't expected another shot.

The whole mountain was riddled with holes, plastered in colorful underwater plants with sea beasts starting in and out of the gaps and skirmishes flaring up everywhere. What truly startled me was watching a kelp-shaped plant suddenly snare a passing beast and weep green fluid that corroded it on the spot. So plants could level up here, too.

That was vital intel, and from now on, I'd have to guard against ambush from the greenery as much as anything with teeth. I looked the peak over and steered straight for it. I knew the danger, but here, danger was constant, so the move was to guard against it carefully without letting it scare me off, because a man who flinches from every threat forfeits every chance to grow.

Fortune favors the bold, and I believed it down to my bones. We reached the peak soon enough, and I spotted a fruit tree clinging to one ledge heavy with bright yellow fruit, which struck me as promising the second I remembered yesterday's primal apple. I edged toward it, careful the whole way, and still an accident found me crossing a patch of coral.

Wary of plants, I'd struck one coral and broken it without gaining experience. So, I'd written the lot off as dead stone, and I picked the sparest stretch to cross anyway. Halfway through, the surrounding coral suddenly moved, ringing the boat in and oozing a clear liquid that smeared across the hall. Spirit eye named it glazed coral white level six with move glazing and stealth which explained why I'd never spotted it lurking.

Glazing was what changed my face because when I checked the hole, I found the smeared patches slowly turning transparent. If this kept up, my whole boat would become glass and the second the diving ability ran out, it would sink. I ordered everyone but Windsor to attack at once. Little Blue charged the densest cluster head-on. Mory and the others poured in arrows.

Huawa flung fireball after fireball, and I chained freezing spell to lock the coral in place. Lucky for me, their level and quality were low because at any height, that would have been catastrophe. We battered a path out of the coral. Our slow speed nearly sinking us. And in the escape, I didn't dare even scoop up the drops for fear of losing more.

The attack taught me two things. First, you cannot blindly trust a reading skill like Spirit Eye, since now and then it leads you straight into a fatal misjudgment. Second, you cannot underestimate low-level creatures, because anything that has survived this long owns its own tricks for survival, and writing it off costs you dearly. Sometimes your life. I grew warrior still after that and crept up at last before the fruit tree.

Except my eyes didn't linger on the tree at all. They fixed on the thing beside it, a water shadow leopard, blue, level 10, every core stat in the mid-50s. Carrying dark beam, extreme speed, and savage strike. Stats that high made me curse under my breath. And the worst of it was the agility at 56. Even buffed, Windsor's agility only reached 50.

So her odds of landing a hit were thin. I looked to Windsor and she read my worry but stayed confident, telling me softly that it was no problem. She could handle him. Only I should take Mory and the commoners back into the house and leave Huawa to help. I understood that out here we'd just be a liability dragging at her.

So I pulled everyone back inside, though not before reminding her to be careful. Once we'd gone, Windsor turned her full attention on the leopard prowling restlessly before the boat. Its body was black and hairless, its eyes a beautiful azure soured by the cruelty in them. The leopard sensed a vague threat off Windsor, but counted it only a threat, not enough to surrender its treasure.

Huawa, perched on Windsor's shoulder, opened with a firebinding that the leopard dodged clean since her agility was only 41 against its far higher number. Agility governs not just movement, but casting speed. Missing, Huawa switched to fireball, conjuring one after another off her little staff and hurling them at the leopard. Though few connected, and those that did barely heard it, it only enraged the thing.

The leopard's speed spiked and it pounced at Windsor. Its extreme speed skill. Windsor instantly raised a light shield around herself and gave ground. But the leopard was too fast, reaching her at a blink, its head-sized paw swiping down with claws gleaming cold. The light shield held a heartbeat before it shattered. But by then, Windsor had already retreated.

The leopard landed on the boat, body low, back arched, a guttural growl rolling in its throat, its fierce gaze fixed on us. Windsor cast light arrow and Huawa cast fireball. And while it dodged its spat, the occasional dark beam, a black ray which Windsor's light shield blocked while the leopard could only eat the damage in return. Before long, the leopard wore a dozen wounds.

Some patches charred where Huawa's fireballs had landed. Frustrated by the lack of a kill, it grew more frantic, its azure eyes reening, reason draining out of it as it ran on extreme speed straight at Windsor to settle things up close. Right then, its mind swam and its focus slipped for an instant. And in that instant, two light ropes and one fire rope snared it.

From the start, Windsor had meant to bait it into losing composure, then catch it with Bewilder to bleed off its speed and pin it down. The leopard was so fast that even unraveling, she'd had to cast Bewilder three times before the third finally caught and dazed it. With it bound, neither Windsor nor Huawa hesitated. Raining Light Arrow and Fireball until the thing was finished.

Once it went quiet outside, I walked out and Windsor came toward me, holding out a blue stone. I was delighted, but I didn't grab it first. Instead, I looked her over concerned and asked whether she was hurt since she was my main force and an injury to her would hit me hard. She told me she wasn't and even if she were, there was no need to worry.

She had healing and my concern plainly made her happy. I said that was good, though privately I knew healing burned mana, too, and out here every point of mana was precious. Then I took the skill stone she offered, shadow lurk blue, which let the user melt into shadow to hide form and aura with a 30% damage bonus on attacks launched from the shadows.

And it required a dark element messenger to learn. A dark skill no use to me now. I'd keep it since a thing this valuable should never be sold or fed to my talent. And if I ever found a fitting hero, I'd hand it over. I steered the boat up to the fruit tree and examined it.

A Linguan gold fruit tree, a rare treasure bearing green Linguan gold fruit that raised base attributes when eaten. I smiled since, of course, it was another attribute treasure. Well worth the effort. Counting the fruit, I found 13 on the tree, and I plucked one. Spirit eye read it as randomly raising three or four attribute points. So I ate it on the spot and got lucky.

Four points, two into agility, and two into wisdom. I picked a second to eat next, but a prompt stopped me, warning that eating one of these treasures repeatedly in a short span gave no further attributes. With an effective interval of 4 years, I hadn't expected the limit, which meant any more Linguan gold fruit I ate inside four years would do nothing.

No wonder the leopard had never touched the fruit and just guarded the tree.

I picked every green Ling and goldf fruit and then turned my attention to the tree itself which stood barely over 2 meters and hardly earned the word tree even dug up I couldn't plant it now and hauling it along for later was hopeless when I had no idea when I'd reach an island giving up galled me though after a moment my eyes lit and I grinned tied the tree off with rope had little blue clamp down and heave upward until it tore free then open the personal shop and chose sell that was the plan all along sell the tree whole the shop quoted two gold stone and my eyes blew wide I'd known It was precious, but not that precious.

Two gold stone. Since arriving on the Lord continent, every coin I owned put together came to about that much, maybe less. I sold it without hesitation, and two gold stone landed in my assets, a full 20,000 white stone. I'd struck it rich, and with that kind of money, I could launch a whole new wave of upgrades, starting with diving to stretch my time in the sea past its measly 1 hour.

With that settled, I turned to head back since little dive time remained, and I had no intention of dying to greed. But just as I made to leave, every creature on the peak began thrashing in sudden chaos. A dragon's roar rolled out deep and heavy with majesty. And at the sound, every beast froze and pressed itself flat to the stone.

Even the plants drooping their branches low. I've been baffled by the panic until that roar explained it. And my own heart shook because this was something far stronger than the deep sea demonic flood dragon I'd met before. I dropped the boat down onto the peak and had Windsor cloak us with stealth. And then I saw a sight that rattled me to the core.

Approaching from the north of the peak came a procession. At its head ran hundreds of giant pythons, jet black with crimson eyes, each over 200 meters long, and behind them four flood dragons of Azure scale, twin horned, around 400 meters each. What stopped my breath was what those four were hauling, not a carriage, but a palace.

The things stood over 30 m tall across three floors, mainly blue, chased with golden patterns, and along the railing of every floor stood demihumans of every race, human-bodied with foreign heads, merman, and turtle men, and the literal shrimp soldiers and crab generals of the sea races. Behind the palace came four more flood dragons. Then hundreds more pythons.

The whole host advancing with overwhelming force. I checked the flood dragons. See azure dragon purple level 24. Strength over 400, agility in the 300s. Defense near 400 with skills I couldn't even read for the level gap. The numbers made me shudder. It was absurd that beasts this strong were reduced to pulling a carriage, four hauling, and four guarding the rear.

And if the drivers ran out powerful, how monstrous was the owner? As if to answer me, the door of the third floor palace opened and every guard up there knelt as one, hailing the third prince. A group stepped out. 9 m tall in a cyan robe, two azure dragon horns rising from his head, flanked by two lovely dragon girls and several graceful mermaids.

He nodded to his guards, swept his gaze around, and settled it on the peak where I hid. I watched him and the dragon girls and mermaids at his side, and I'll be honest, I felt a stab of envy and thought I ought to acquire a few of my own someday, purely as scenery. Windsor caught where my eyes had gone and pressed her lips together.

Then I watched him leap up and transform into a true dragon hundreds of meters long. No flood dragon, but the genuine article body all azure, vast, fearsome horns, white flowing mane streaming off his huge head. Two dragon whiskers trailing from his mouth. Four legs each ending in five savage claws. He roamed the water lawless and unbound and loosed an enormous roar.

And every creature on the peak flattened itself, shuddering. My own body went weak, the instinctive surrender of a lesser thing before the irresistible. And Mory and the three commoners had already dropped prostrate that Windsor and Huawa stood mostly unaffected. Huawa even flitting about studying the dragon with open curiosity.

I checked his panel and snapped a few photos with my system panel while I was at it purely to light a fire under myself because someday I'd command grandeur to put this in the shade. Azure Sea Dragon King, legendary hero, level 24, strength 473, agility 387, defense 401, skills unreadable past the level gap. So, the Azure Sea Dragon King was a legendary hero and not a sea beast at all.

The power staggered me, but what gripped me more was his identity because a legendary hero belonged to some lord's forces and all this around him was a territory's army, a fearsomely strong territory at that. The guards had called him third prince, which made him the lord's own descendant. I found myself wondering where the sea races even built their territories, whether on islands or somewhere down in the sea.

And it dawned on me how vast this world truly was. The endless sea brimming with secrets. The world wasn't the bleak thing I saw every day. Nothing but survival and water to the horizon. Out in the places I couldn't reach, there had to be whole civilized worlds. And I could only touch any of it once I grew strong.

Since right now I was an infant, I burned with longing for the future. Certain I'd one day stand against existences like this. And certain I could do it. The first step toward that was raising my own territory. Fighting spirit flooded me. Up in the air, the Azure Sea Dragon King looked thoroughly satisfied with the reverence of every beast below, and he spoke human words from his dragon mouth deep and clear.

Just one reward. Then he shifted back to human form, gathered his dragon girls close, and withdrew into the palace. I was still puzzling over the word when sea creatures hauled great boxes out from the first floor, and began flinging the contents across the peak. A flatout prodigal, scattering his fortune like a man tossing coins in the street, a windfall dropping out of the sky.

Good thing he wasn't my kid, because if he were, I'd have beaten him senseless, and I left whichever lord owned him to deal with that headache himself. My job was to grab what I could before the other beasts. Already going feral over the loot, took it all. I sent Little Blue in to fight for the spoils and set Windsor and Huawa to snatching items with their control skills.

Soon, the Azure Sea Dragon King and his host had moved off into the distance, leaving only the increasingly rabid sea beasts who'd already turned on each other over the hall. I took one look at that and chose to leave because the place had grown lethal, and my dive time was nearly spent.

I started the boat and watched the chaotic peak shrink and vanish from sight, and gradually, I heard rain pattering into the sea and knew I was about to surface. When we broke the surface, I looked at the gloomy sky and the endless gray water and replayed the whole scene. And if not for the gains sitting in my space, I might have taken it all for a hallucination.

I composed myself and told my to take the four of them fishing, handing out the rods, then told Windsor to head to the residence with Huawa and recharge the boat. She nodded, said she understood, and carried Huawa off toward the residence. Back in the room, before any spending, I checked exactly what I'd managed to snatch. Three items in all.

The first was a pet's quality on use. Though the higher the level, the lower the success rate. That one was a real gift. Purple quality and a rare tool for lifting aptitude, which in this world basically dictates a creature's strength and growth ceiling. I weighed who to use it on. On little blue, it would push him to purple with near certain success.

While on Huawa, it risked failure, but if it took would hand me a second legendary level fighter. I decided on Huawa since purple enhancement tools don't come cheap, and I'd upgrade Little Blue later when another surfaced. The second item was a crisis warning device manufacturing blueprint which detected hostile creatures within 300 and sounded an alarm needing 30 iron, 30 copper, 20 broken circuit board and 10 glass to build.

A fair list and I had none of the broken circuit board, though a few panes of glass which joged my memory that I still hadn't used the window blueprint. So I took out the window blueprint and built it and a glass pane appeared in the wooden wall raind drumming against it. The warning device looked genuinely useful so I meant to build it fast.

I checked the trade channel, found neither component listed and posted two purchase requests. 100 white stone for 10 glass and 200 for 20 broken circuit board. Then dropped a line in the chat asking whether anyone had glass or circuit boards to trade. After that, I stopped watching it.

The last item was a small glass tube of cyan liquid Azure Sea Dragon blood blue, which granted the Azure Sea Dragon Clan bloodline on use with the condition that every base attribute reach above 51st. There really was such a thing. If I drank it, I wouldn't become Dragon Clan, only carry their bloodline.

But still, I put it away since I couldn't touch it yet and would need to hit level six or seven before I met the requirement. Then I opened technology, found ship diving technology in its weakened form, and chose to upgrade 3,000 whit stone, took it to level two, doubling my daily dive to 2 hours.

After that, I opened multi-or linkage power propulsion technology and spent 1,000 to push it to level two as well. Because after seeing the Azure Sea Dragon King, my hunger for a territory had only sharpened and I wanted to reach that island faster. If my energy supply could have kept up, I'd have poured more levels into the propulsion outright.

But for now, the others weren't worth upgrading since they'd offer me little at the moment. Then I bought the materials to upgrade the boat itself. 500 wood, 800 iron nails, 50 copper, and chose upgrade. The boat hit level 5, 30 m by 9. Its facilities now a core cabin, a large living area, two small living areas, a warehouse, a toilet, and the fishing corridor running on multi-or linkage propulsion.

Its technology stacked up across drainage at level two, thermal energy at one, automation at one, propulsion at two, and diving weakened at two, with diving as its special ability. The upgrade added a large living area and a small living area, stretched the boat 10 m longer at the same width, and renamed the cabin to core cabin, which I guess was groundwork for bigger changes to the ship down the line.

I stepped out of the core cabin and Mory and the four commoners all started to rise and salute, but I waved them off and told them to keep fishing, then assigned Mory to a small living area for the night and the three commoners to the large one, which they all acknowledged. I glanced at the oars and counted four extra, clearly the result of pushing the propulsion to level two.

Then I went to the small living area where Windsor and Huawa were, and the second I opened the door, a wave of heat rolled over me, along with the sight of Windsor's rain soap dress clinging to every line of her, a sight I won't risk describing in detail. I ranged myself in.

With the awkward third presence of Mory moving out of the room tonight, I'd finally have room to deepen things with Windsor anyway. I cleared my throat and told them to pause a moment. I interrupted Windsor and Huawa, and the two stopped. Windsor flushed first, then quickly dried her clothes while Huawa flew over happily and hugged my head.

I caught Huawa, took out the pet quality enhancement card, and used it on her. The card dissolved into purple light and sank into her body, her eyes snapping shut as her aura churned. Slowly, she grew until she stood around 40 cm, double her old size, a flame pattern blooming on her fair little forehead that lent her small, tender face a touch of noble bearing.

I was overjoyed since it had plainly worked, and I now had a second legendary level fighter at my side. When she opened her eyes, she looked at me happily and pressed her face to my cheek, nuzzling, more affectionate than ever. Then I took out two green Ling Yen goldf fruit and had Windsor and Huawa each eat one.

I checked Huawa's panel afterward. Her tag now reading fire elemental spirit. Gold level six, strength 43, agility 42, defense 40 with fireball, blazing body, firebinding, and fire shield. Her stats had climbed three points, two into strength and one into agility, and the quality jump had handed her the extra fire shield skill while flipping her tag from purple to gold.

The raw numbers barely moved, but her growth ceiling now towered over the purple version. I turned to Windsor and asked how many attributes she gained and she said happily that her agility rose too and her defense rose too. The green Ling Yuen goldf fruit capping at four. I nodded still a little surprised by the result.

I said goodbye to Windsor and the others called Little Blue over and fed him a green Ling Yuan goldfruit which gave him three points all into strength. His panel read deep sea giant whale blue level six strength 35 agility 30 defense 30 with impact and bite. He'd been hunting on his own these past two days and climbed to level six off it.

I patted his head and sent him back to the hunt, then went fishing myself. The afternoon flew and though the drop rate ran higher now, nothing remarkable surfaced. The one decent game was a silk pajamas blueprint. Permanent use five silk to craft. A good thing to keep both for clothes and for business since I could buy silk soil pajamas and resell them on the trade channel at a margin.

After dinner, I built wooden beds, tables, and chairs and set them out across the three living quarters. Then bought straw and cloth and had a few commoners spread them so any future summon could simply move in. I'd wanted proper bedding for them too, but a search of the trade channel turned up none, and I couldn't fathom why comfortable bedding surfaced so rarely.

Back in the core cabin, Windsor already lay in bed while Huawa stayed outside recharging, likely her role for a long while. I didn't lie down yet. I took out the warning device blueprint and built it. Having gathered the basic materials over the afternoon, and a golden sphere appeared on a meter wide base paired with a fist-sized glass ball cradling a small light, the ball being the indicator and the sphere the detector.

I called three commoners to carry the golden sphere to the bow, then shed my outer clothes, drew back the covers, and lay down. Windsor folded into me at once, rubbing her head against my chest, and I bent and kissed her forehead. Then I checked Wangm Mo<unk>'s trade list for the day, which held nothing special beyond a green cup and a green spoon, both of which I bought for 50 white stone each.

Chicken scraps to others, useless junk. So I'd thrown in five extra white stone, and the seller traded at once and praised my generosity. I asked him to spread the word that I was very interested in highquality daily goods, and he agreed gladly. Worthless to everyone else, these were treasures in my hands since my talent could spin trash into gold.

I used the cup first, ran my talent, and got a blueprint. Luxury bedding set, blue, 50 cotton wool and 15 cloth to build, which sped you into deep sleep and restored your mind better. So, it turned out a daily good, and I noticed these comfort items carried quality grades yet added no stats, only making life softer. I put it away to build tomorrow.

I ran 100 transformations again and got a recharging bright lamp, blue, five broken circuit board, five iron, five copper, and two tungsten filament to build, which soaked up sunlight by day and lit the dark by night. Another daily good. I'd started to notice the talent's output leaned toward the kind of item I fed it.

So, daily goods bred daily goods, and I wondered whether that was the direction the talent might evolve, whether I'd one day be able to conjure whatever I pleased. The thought thrilled me. Even as a mere comfort item, the lamp pleased me, since I'd been lighting the cabin with candles bought off the trade channel, and now my standard of living crept up a notch.

If I improved a little everyday, my wooden boat would eventually become a luxury cruise ship, a combat grade luxury cruise ship. At that, I searched the trade channel and managed only two broken circuit boards. So, I posted requests. 30 white stone for three boards and 40 for two tungsten filament, shouted in the chat, and let it go.

For the talent's final use of the night, I took out a gold stone, and my eyes flickered with doubt over whether to gamble it. This was 10,000 white stone, and if it turned into something like a purple crystal, I'd weep. After weighing it, I gambled anyway since I wasn't desperate for cash. And even spending this gold stone left me around 6,000 whit stone after the day's upgrades.

I used 100 transformations. A golden light flashed, and a tree appeared in my hand, 50 cm tall. Its branches hung with white stone and a few purple stone. A money tree, a special item that grows an undetermined amount of currency every day. I burst out laughing because I gambled in one.

I plucked everything off the branches and counted over 500 white stone, which meant that from now on, doing nothing at all, I'd earn 500 a day, and with luck, maybe gold stone. I set the money tree on the floor. Then I pulled Windsor close and kissed her hard since she'd been pressing against my chest the whole time I worked the talent and stirred my blood.

She startled and resisted faintly before answering with soft hums and the occasional nasal sound. She'd seen the way I'd looked at the dragon girls earlier and still felt some of it, which was why she'd spent the day teasing me. A kiss was soon far too little, and I wanted further, climbing a peak to take in the wonderful view, while Windsor let out a muffled groan, her body going slack.

Just as I made to press on, she pushed me with her hand, and I felt it and let her go. Her cheeks were flushed, her eyes shimmerred, and a strip of fair skin showed at her waist from the recent skirmish. Breathing lightly, feeling my heated gaze, she lowered her head and said she wasn't ready yet. Half afraid to meet my eyes.

I'd lost some control myself a moment ago, but I was clearer now, and I told her it was all right. I'd been reckless. She should rest. I was a little let down, but comforted her anyway. I understood our intimacy better than she did since I had a progress bar to read, and I wasn't in any rush.

After a full day together, her affinity toward me had reached 87, and I figured it would hit 90 by tomorrow night. She caught the trace of disappointment in my eyes and apologized with some guilt, then lifted her head and kissed my lips. I told her it really was fine and to hurry and rest, then added that she didn't need to call me Lord anymore.

My name was Hashin and she could just use it. She started to protest with a startled little sound, but seeing my eyes widened slightly, she nodded instead. I told her to try saying it a few times to get used to it, and she shut her eyes and whispered, "Hashin so faintly it was nearly inaudible. Louder," I said.

She managed a touch more volume, then a few more times until it came easier. Her eyes finally opening to look at me and with that our relationship stepped a notch closer. I held her idly toying and she didn't refuse though her breathing ran heavy and so the two of us talked ourselves slowly to sleep.

In a days I felt something poking my face and I opened my eyes to find Huawa proddding my cheek with her little staff. The instant she saw me wake she pointed frantically at the floor and I looked down and snapped fully alert because the room was a wash with water. I scrambled up and went outside where the rain had grown far heavier than during the day.

The drops huge and dense, the distance looking like someone tipping a bucket from the sky. Worse, it kept building and a sheet of water already lay across the whole boat. Windsor came to my side, and I told her to hurry and wake Mory and the others while I opened the technology panel, found drainage, and spent 2,000 whit stone upgrading it to level three.

Even so, the drain couldn't match the rate of accumulation, though it cut it sharply, and I didn't push the upgrade further since the day was nearly over, and I'd bail the rest by hand. Mory and the four others arrived then, and I sent them to the fishing corridor to bail fast, handing out four basins, which they took with a quick acknowledgement before turning to the work.

It was nearly dawn already, and I had no idea how much longer the rain would fall. If a sixth day kept up like this, few people would survive.

I opened the world channel and found it thick with the dune shouting that they were finished, that only standing guards had kept their ships from sinking outright, that Shano meant to wipe them all out in one sweep, just a scatter of desperate lines since everyone was too busy saving their boats to type much. Judging the current intensity, I decided no further upgrade was needed, and Mory plus the four would do.

Every so often across this Lord continent, Chan Mo selects a batch of people, grants them Lord qualifications, and runs them through trial after trial to keep them growing. Each era runs a different length, one or two centuries, sometimes four or five, and the start of a lord trial is the birth of a new era. One of the spirit domain and one of the demon domain. They were one in the same ones, he explained. But for reasons no one knows, they split like one person carrying two separate souls.
