I clicked the box open and she went straight in, asking whether I was still planning to join the two domain competition and proddding it at whether I was secretly short on strength. I rolled my eyes at how nosy she was and told her of course I was participating, then asked why she cared.

She shot back that she had checked the rankings and my name was not even in the top 10,000, so how exactly did I intend to qualify? On her end, she was apparently sprawled on her bed under a velvet quilt, a white dolphin doll propped beside her with a lollipop.

She was thoroughly annoyed since she had wanted to team up once the demon domain opened and had scanned the list three or four times without finding me. She refused to believe I genuinely could not crack the top 10,000 and decided correctly that I was just too lazy to grind matches. I found the whole interrogation a bit exasperating since I had barely interacted with this person.

Yet here she was tracking my every placement and something about her familiarity struck an oddly recognizable note. She was right that my ranking had not entered the top 10,000. But I had not cared because I could grind my way in tonight and tomorrow and I was not chasing first place, only a ticket into the competition.

Spending that grinding time on more ice cubes simply made more money and money was the whole game. So I told her not to worry, that I would absolutely qualify, then asked whether she had fallen for me since she was paying me so much attention.

She snorted that I was unbelievably narcissistic and admitted she wanted to team up and rampage across the field together, roughly the plan I had already guessed she was building toward, but positions were assigned at random, so I asked how she imagined we could possibly end up together. Leio chuckled, rolled onto her stomach, kicked her pale little legs in the air, and announced that she had a way, then sent me a picture.

The image was an appraisal of a binding rope blue, which after use bound two people so that when either was teleported, both arrived at the same spot. I had never seen such a thing, so I asked how it worked.

She had clearly read my reply as agreement since she explained the rope came as a pair, one for each of us, and said she had already listed the second in the trade channel for me to buy. I opened the channel, found the listing, and spent a single white stone on it, ending up with a thin red cord about the size of a bracelet.

She told me to wear it on my hand when teleporting, and I confirmed the plan, agreeing we would meet up when the time came. I was willing to partner with her for two solid reasons. First, a top 10 player was unlikely to be weak. Second, the partnership would deepen our mutual trust and make us comfortable trading once we both had territories since no settlement thrives by sealing itself off from the world.

She replied that she would see me then and told me sharply not to break our agreement. And then with a sly little smile, she snapped a photo of herself and sent it over with a note that I could use it for entertainment on a lonely night. No thanks necessary. In the picture, she lay on the bed, chest pressed flat against the mattress to show a patch of snow white skin.

Her two legs kicked up behind her, eyes blinking, little tongue stuck out. I gave a rise smile and the meaning loaded into her last line darkened my expression further because honestly did I need that? What a joke. When there were two beauties in my room already.

I closed the chat box, made another round of ice, and once my mana emptied, I stepped into the arena to play some matches since I still needed to grind my way into qualifying. The arena was loud and packed as always, so I wasted no time. Put on my mask, took a seat, and started straight in.

The map this round was an ice field, which for me was a battlefield tilted heavily in my favor. And when I checked my opponent, I found a middle-aged man, dark-skinned, burly, a full 2 meters tall, gripping a wolf fang club and wearing a suit of armor. His information named him Jessine, level six, which surprised me since a level six player was not nothing, and I had never heard of him.

Then I worked it out that a man with poor talent or none would naturally have pushed his level higher to compensate, because in the early game, level is the only thing that lets weak talent suppress strong talent. Some players deliberately held their level down to wait for talent boosting treasures, which used to be my own thinking. But once I learned rare spirit treasures could raise attributes, I stopped suppressing mine.

Most people raised their levels up regardless since the higher the level, the safer the sea. And the only reason I dared coast was that I had a legendary hero like Windsor at my side. Otherwise, I would have been leveling like a madman, too. Jesseni read my displayed level five, but did not relax because enough matches had taught him that level does not equal strength and that underestimating an opponent costs dearly.

He hefted the club and charged, his talent being immense strength at green quality. And having drawn the Berserker profession, he carried some real confidence into the fight. I watched him come, raised my staff, and dropped a freezing spell that locked his feet to the ice. And he strained and could not break loose, which alarmed him badly. A lower level opponent pinning him meant my talent had to be excellent.

And the thought flashed through his head that I was very likely the number one lord, since everyone knew by now that the top player wielded ice. He refused to quit and triggered his skill, Frenzy, which doubled his strength and stacked on his immense strength talent, tore him free of my freezing spell. I was honestly surprised he broke loose, but I did not panic.

As he charged again, I simply refro him, slowing him a second time since all his power came strictly from talent and skill, which swelled his strength stat, but left his agility untouched. So, he was still far too slow to dodge anything I threw.

Once I had him pinned, I followed with water arrow technique since my ice element messenger profession ran downward compatible with water skills, which was exactly why the system rated my class purple instead of the blue a pure water messenger would have drawn. The arrow struck only his helmet and did no fatal damage. And seeing him about to wrench free, I locked him down with another freezing spell.

Jini wared that if I had any guts, I would fight him headon instead of disgracing my status as number one. And I ignored the bait and fired another water arrow. I was not stupid enough to walk into danger over a cheap provocation and a scrap of vanity. Because when a problem can be solved the simple way, manufacturing trouble for myself is the act of a fool.

Seeing me stonewall him completely, Jasini gave up arguing and just swatted the incoming arrows aside. Knowing in his gut that he would lose if this continued, but unable to find any way to break the pattern, so he raged uselessly and grew more miserable by the second. I kept cycling control and attack, control and attack, until he was severely wounded and clearly fading.

And right then he let out a furious roar that if he was going to die, he would make sure I did not walk away clean either. He swung the wolf fang club and hurled it at me with everything he had. And the sight startled me because it seemed I had genuinely pushed him over the edge. I threw up a shield for myself just in time and the club smashed into it.

Its force gutted but not erased so it still cracked into my chest and drove me back three steps and forced a mouthful of blood out of me. I did not stop to check the damage and fired water arrow technique straight at him. And he laughed wildly, ignoring the arrows because he had nothing left. Satisfied that he had at least landed a blow at the end.

This time, the arrow took him in the head, his laughter cut off, and he fell dead. I wiped the blood from the corner of my mouth and walked out of the combat zone, then sat down to think through what that match had cost and taught me.

First, I could not keep underestimating players with mediocre talent, because mediocre did not mean harmless, and a single careless second could hand a man like that the win. Second, I had not been cautious enough since against someone as vicious as Jessine, without the shield skill, I would have been the corpse instead of him.

So, I warned myself hard to stay sharper and never drop my guard in the final moments to personally confirm a kill before relaxing because that bruise was a lesson and a piece of expensive experience both. Then, I went back into the arena and across the next several fights, I played far more carefully and won cleanly. When I came out, my mana had refilled.

So, I made a batch of ice, listed it, and turned to today's 100 transformations before midnight expired. The day's three uses. The first thing I pulled was a leap skill stone, blue, which granted the leap skill and raised agility by 30% on use.

Since I had never fed a skill stone through 100 transformations before, I was eager to see whether I could draw a purple item or with luck even a gold one. I triggered the transformation and what bloomed in front of me was a profession temple construction blueprint. Purple, described as an indispensable building for a strong territory that let residents change professions, become professionals, and let professionals learn hidden skills.

The materials it demanded were staggering. 1,000 ancient wood, 1,000 refined iron, 500 adamantine, 100 cloud pattern stone, and 100 each of gold, wood, water, fire, and earth element gem. The description left me slack jawed because a profession temple was functionally an endless supply of skill stones and profession cards rolled into one, which meant I had absolutely hit the jackpot.

The catch was that I had not laid eyes on a single one of those materials yet, and had no idea when I would ever finish it. So, it went to the bottom of the box to gather dust. Though even unbuilt, it added real foundation to my future territory.

For the second pull, I rubbed my chin a while, then took out an ore extractor blueprint, blue, which granted the ability to mine ore and required iron blocks, copper blocks, rubber, and electronic components. A mining blueprint was not especially precious to me right now, and I figured I could easily pick up another after founding my territory.

Feeding it through hundred transformations for at least a purple was the smarter play, especially since I had just watched a blue skill stone bloom into a purple profession temple and felt the gap between the two tiers more sharply than ever. Without further hesitation, I triggered the talent. Light flashed and an object appeared, cylindrical, about half my height, silver white all over, and traced with patterns in many colors.

I stepped up and studied it, finding a smaller cylinder hollowed out of the top with spiraling colored grooves along its inner wall, and beside that hollow set a red button marked with the word start. A blueprint had turned into something this elaborate and frankly advanced looking.

So I ran my spirit eye over it, read the description, and burst out laughing because this was like being handed a pillow the very moment exhaustion hits. The heavens themselves lending a hand. The appraisal called it an element converter, purple, which took items containing elements and converted them into the matching element gems and special purple crystals with a note warning that producing purple crystals burned a great deal of energy.

I read it twice to be sure I had not misjudged. Then I scooped Huawa into my arms, pressed my forehead against hers, and told the little one, smiling, that her food problem was solved from this day forward, and she was truly blessed. I was genuinely delighted since I had been gnawing on exactly how to feed her growth for the long haul, and the answer had simply fallen into my lap.

Fire element items being far easier to acquire than raw gems or purple crystals. From now on, I could buy up cheap, lowquality fire element junk, run it through the converter, and produce the purple crystals that fueled her advancement. Though I still had to test whether the element gems themselves could feed her. I had nothing element bearing on hand or I would have tested the converter on the spot.

So I set it aside to move into the core cabin tomorrow with the rest of the things I could not yet use. The last item of the night was another Talon orb, green, useless to me with my slots full and likely to stay that way for ages. So I decided to gamble it through 100 transformations and see what came out.

I was looking forward to it more than I should have since 100 transformations was the single talent that had carried me this far. The orb transformed into a wood element talent mutation orb blue, which on use forced a positive mutation in wood element talents with a very small chance of raising quality, effective only on excellent and lower talents.

My enthusiasm flattened because the thing was clearly strong in the right hands. Only those hands were not mine since I had been quietly hoping for something that could lift talent quality and bump my own 100 transformations toward legendary. Instead, I got a mutation orb loaded with restrictions that did me no good. So, I resolved to sell it later, though not for whitest stone, the least valuable thing I own.

Right now, the proper move was an item for item trade aimed at squeezing out something genuinely good. And I figured I would open that trade after the week closed when the odds of decent offers ran higher. With that, I was done for the night.

So, I went back to the quiet, profitable rhythm of making ice, draining my mana, fighting arena matches to hold my qualifying spot and refilling around and around as the sky slowly pad toward morning. The instant the first ray of light crested the horizon, the heat slammed down so hard that even the cooler beside me could barely take the edge off.

And within moments, sweat had soaked my clothes flat against my skin. There was no working outside in this. So I checked the cooler, spent white stone on the parts, and pushed it to its maximum, level 12, where it could pull the temperature down by 22°. Each of the late levels shaving off another two.

That helped a little, but it was still brutal, and I could not linger outdoors for long, which meant fishing was off the table for the commoners today. So I would have them haul sea water inside and make ice within the cabin instead.

Right as I was laying out the day's plan, Windsor came over, settled onto the stool beside me and asked whether I was tired and wanted to rest while she kept watch. I patted my thigh and waved her over, and she shot me a half exasperated look, but got up anyway, scooped up Huawa, and sat sideways across my lap.

She poked my forehead and complained that dragging her over here in this heat cannot possibly be comfortable for me. I laughed, kissed her rosy lips, and told her there was nothing to be done, that she was simply too attractive, and a little discomfort was well worth it. She was clearly pleased, but refused to show it, pinching the soft flesh at my waist instead.

And rolling her eyes, she said she had known from the first day that I had designs on her, but had not expected me to be quite this shameless. Then she asked, half teasing, whether I wanted her and little sister Dark Moon to serve me together someday. I sighed and said I wished, but Dark Moon would never agree, looking as helpless as I could manage.

Watching my completely unrepentant face, Windsor got a little annoyed, turned away, and declared that even if Dark Moon agreed, she would not. I understood her far too well. So I turned her face back and kissed her and she resisted for a heartbeat before melting into it.

She breathed that all I knew how to do was bully her, and I told her I had always known she was the best, taking her soft hands and mine, she snorted at me to let go because it was too hot and feeling a certain unmistakable reaction stirring beneath her. Her face flushed deeper still. The long kiss had clearly affected her too.

So she got up quickly and stepped away. I did not stop her, only handed her the resource fishing rod and told her to burn through today's three pools now since it would only get hotter as the day wore on. By the time the sun was fully up, she had used all three attempts and turned to hand me the items.

I twisted around to take them and froze because the heat had soaked her dress through until it clung to every line of her figure and the sight hit me like a wave of thirst. She noticed my stare reened and started to steam her dress dry. But I stopped her, swept her up, and carried her toward the core cabin while she gave a soft cry.

Inside the cabin, I was relieved to find Dark Moon absent since being walked in on would have been genuinely awkward, and I told myself to finish quickly. I set Windsor down, and when she asked with some annoyance what I thought I was doing, I only chuckled since she knew exactly what I wanted and simply could not bring herself to say it.

I kissed her and began my exploration, and she gradually warmed to it and answered in kind, and the two of us went back and forth in a lively contest that I, in the end, won, leaving her begging for mercy while I felt thoroughly smug. Afterward, we lay there spent, and she marveled that I had not slept a wink all night, yet still had this much energy left.

I stroked her hair and told her she had simply been too charming this morning, and she let out a soft snort that did nothing to hide how pleased the compliment made her.

Then she nudged me to get dressed before Dark Moon came back, and with no wish to linger, I washed up and stepped out, except that when I came out of the cabin, Dark Moon was standing right there at the entrance, and our eyes met, and the moment turned distinctly awkward.

I covered it by telling her as evenly as I could that after breakfast I would have the ship dive straight away and that she and Windsor should run the hunt as usual. She felt the discomfort too, but held her cool composure and answered that she understood. Lord, seeing her retreat into that formality stirred something in me, so I beckoned her closer.

When she stepped up looking faintly confused, I leaned in, kissed her on the cheek, and told her smiling that from now on she should call me Hashin the way Windsor did. No more Lord. It was far too stiff. Then I left to go arrange the day. Behind me, Dark Moon stood frozen by the unexpected kiss until my back had already disappeared, her fair cheeks flushing hot.

And only after a moment did she go back inside to find Windsor lounging across the bed, grinning at her, which I imagine made the whole thing worse. Out on the deck, the heat hit like stepping into a furnace. And I told the commoners still sweating through their work in the fishing corridor that there would be no fishing today and they could return to their rooms.

They were plainly relieved since the heat had them on the edge of heat stroke. But I asked a few to stay behind, haul sea water up and bring it to my cabin so I could keep making ice. Then I fashioned a handful of wooden buckets in a large basin and handed them out. After that, I collected Huawa and carried her back to the core cabin.

Ignoring the playful Windsor and Dark Moon, I gathered up yesterday's earnings from the money tree and opened the technology panel. The next ship level wanted 20,000 white stone and was out of reach for a while, which made it the right time to develop technologies instead. I tried lighting up the single screw propulsion technology and the system again refused, telling me the conditions were not met.

So that one evidently waited on the third stage, leaving me to keep upgrading the multiorine. Pushing it from level three to level four cost 4,000 white stone, and I paid without hesitation because a faster ship meant reaching my destination and planting a territory sooner, which mattered more than nearly anything else. Then I opened the diving technology, sitting at level two, where level three demanded a full 6,000 white stone.

After last night's selling, I had the white stone to spare. And I went ahead with the dive upgrade, too, since deeper diving meant longer hunts, which leveled the crew and brought in more white stone besides. Once both cleared, I noticed my reserves had cratered to barely over a thousand, the kind of number that makes a merchant wse.

Right then, my came in to announce breakfast. So, I went to eat, and during the meal, I used the sea water the commoners had carried up to make one more batch of ice. When I finished, I had the ship dive and start the day's hunt, leaving Windsor and the others responsible while I stayed in the cabin grinding ice.

And after my mana ran out, I sat down to appraise the three items Windsor had pulled today. The first was a blue arratin armor construction blueprint, green, which let a blacksmith learn to forge the armor and raise my army's combat power. I lifted an eyebrow since that counted as a good item, though I stored it for after my territory existed, given that I did not even have the blacksmith yet.

The second was a bag holding spirit rice seeds, green, which after planting yielded spirit rice that better replenished the body's energy. And grain seeds were always worth having. More than enough for a territory's early days, even if there were not many. The last was a lounge chair manufacturing blueprint, white, a comfortable chair costing only four wood and 10 iron nails.

And I was thoroughly let down since it carried no real value. Still, I built it on the spot so Windsor and the others would have somewhere to lie around. With that handled, I went back to making ice and stepping into the arena whenever my mana emptied.

After fighting non-stop all last night, I had finally cracked the top 10,000, but I could not afford to stop since everyone else was grinding just as frantically and would shove me back out fast if I let up. While I made ice and fought, though, Windsor and the others ran into a bit of trouble out on the hunt.

Out under the sea, Windsor had everything running normally, with Lee Roo piloting and Little Blue scouting the surroundings, and it stayed calm right up until they crossed paths with a shark 12 m long, blue skinned, marked with purple black patterns, and a single black horn jutting from its head.

Windsor had no appraisal skill and could not read the creature's exact strength, but when one blow from it left Little Blue badly hurt, she understood the thing was not to be taken lightly. She ordered the rest of the commoners to fall back to avoid pointless casualties. Then she and Huawa moved to deal with it while Dark Moon, whose strength dropped during the day, was left to watch over the commoners.

Windsor laid a light shield on herself and on Huawa sent a light rope at the shark, and Huawa followed with firebinding, but fighting in the sea blunt Huawa's power considerably. The shark fired a black arrow from its horn that shattered the light rope twisted aside from the firebinding and without slowing lunged straight for the tower ship where Windsor stood.

Right then, Windsor's light arrow and Huawa's fireball both struck home. And though the shark slipped the arrow, the fireball caught it. The fireball did not do much real damage, but it dragged the shark speed down out of that first furious rush, and Windsor seized the opening to land a light rope that bound it, slashing its movement. After which, Huawa stacked another firebinding to tighten the control.

Watching the creature thrash yet still strained toward the tower ship, Windsor's eyes went cold because she could not let it reach the hole. So, she hit it with a bewilder skill and poured light arrows into it alongside Huawa's fireballs. The shark shrieked, its body pocked with blood holes and seared with burns.

Then, suddenly, when still, the patterns along its skin flickering with pale light from the tail forward until everything gathered at its head. A purplish black orb began forming on the black horn. Arcs of electricity crackling through the seaater around it, and Windsor's expression shifted because that attack looked nasty and she doubted she could block it alone.

She called for Huawa to cast fire shield and Huawa, understanding the danger, layered two on her in quick succession while Windsor stacked two light shields of her own. When the orb launched, Windsor first threw a light arrow to intercept it, but the arrow shattered on contact, and the orb tore past Huawa's fireball.

The purplish black orb did not aim for Windsor or Huawa, but drove straight at the tower ship since the shark had grasped that wrecking the vessel would leave the two of them easy prey. Windsor was never going to allow that because a damaged ship meant their whole journey would suffer even if no one died.

So, she took a deep breath, leapt off the tower ship, and met the attack headon, and the orb smashed through her shields one after another. And when Huawa flung up one more fire shield, it broke the instant it formed. The orb slammed into Windsor's body, hurled her against the outer hole with a heavy impact, and she coughed up blood.

Her body covered in wounds, then cast a healing spell on herself and turned back to the shark. The attack had clearly drained the creature, and seeing that its gamble had failed, it roared louder. Though now there was real fear threaded through the sound, because it understood it was facing death. Windsor was not about to grant it any reprieve.

So she unleashed light arrows, and Huawa, furious over Windsor's injury, hurled fireballs even harder than before. Then something astonishing happened. Huawa's whole body transformed in her rage, swelling until she stood the height of a young girl. Her form sheathed in red and gold mark armor, fiery hair streaming, a golden flame pattern blazing on her forehead. Even her fireballs changed.

No longer small red ones, but far larger golden spheres. And when her golden fireball struck the shark, it snapped the black horn clean off, kept right on going, and punched a bloody hole through the creature's skull, killing it outright. Windsor was stunned by the sight, and a moment later, she watched Huawa shrink back down and drop onto the deck.

So she abandoned the shark's loot and rushed over an alarm. Huawa's hair had dimmed, but her chest still rose and fell gently, which let Windsor breathe again since it seemed to be nothing more than exhaustion.

She gathered the little one up, and though she did not understand what had just happened, she was deeply moved because she knew the transformation had been triggered by her own injury, and her gaze softened as she looked at Huawa. Dark Moon arrived just then, asking with worry whether Windsor was all right.

And Windsor said she had taken no serious harm since after passing through five shields, the orbs force had not actually been that great and the healing had her mostly mended. She told Dark Moon to go retrieve the shark's drop and make sure no further accidents happened. And Dark Moon nodded, leapt off the ship and went after the item.

Windsor cast healing on Huawa, who was not wounded, but could still be soothed by it. Then she walked to the bow and stroked Little Blue's head because without that timely interception, the tower ship would surely have taken damage. And seeing the wounds across the whale's body, she quickly healed those two. Liruk came up, bowed slightly, and asked what they should do next.

And Windsor told him to hold position for now, that they needed to rest and recover. And he answered that he understood, "Lord," the group rested aboard until they had mostly recovered before moving on, and the rest of the dive passed quietly without incident. When the time came to surface, Lee piloted the ship up and out of the water, and Windsor carried Huawa with Dark Moon beside her into the core cabin.

She found me sitting on the sofa with my eyes shut and understood I was in the arena. So she settled by the bed with Huawa and spoke quietly with Dark Moon rather than disturb me. When I opened my eyes and noticed them, I asked whether everything had gone smoothly, mostly out of routine since nothing unusual had come up lately and I was not paying close attention.

As it turned out, something genuinely unexpected had happened this time because Windsor said they had run into a fairly strong sea beast and hit a small snag. I asked with some concern whether she was badly hurt and she said no serious injuries. So I asked what exactly had happened.

She recounted the whole encounter and when she reached the part about Huawa undergoing a huge transformation because she herself had been injured, she looked down at Huawa in her arms with open tenderness. I was genuinely surprised to hear of a special change like that. So I checked Huawa's information and confirmed it. Her readout now showed her as a fire elemental spirit in an evolving state.

Legendary level six. Her stats sitting in the low 40s across strength, agility, defense, and wisdom. Her mana over 400. Her profession listed as hero, fire element controller with a new line reading fire marshall god awakening. And her skills now ran fireball blazing body marked as advancing, firebinding and fire shield. Her loyalty and affinity both at 100.

First, her spirit being flagged as evolving told me Huawa had begun developing to a deeper level. And that new fire marshall god awakening line made me suspect that once she finished, she might become a fire marshall god rather than a simple elemental spirit.

Second, her blazing body skill showing as advancing was almost certainly the cause of her body's enormous shift since I had always been puzzled by that skill, thinking it did little more than act as a power bank, when in fact it carried enormous potential. Beyond being triggered by an external shock, I guessed her awakening had also been quietly fed by how constantly I had her use the skill.

It seemed this was the in-between state of an incomplete awakening where she could only shift forms by luck until she fully awakened. And there was no telling how long that would take.

My guess was that beyond drilling blazing body, she would also need a steady supply of energy, which frustrated me since I still had nothing fire element on hand. So for now, I could only let nature run its course and have her practice.

I laughed and ruffled her hair, asking whether she meant she would work hard, and she nodded seriously, then flapped her wings and flew out toward the deck to drill her blazing body, leaving me a little speechless that my off-hand encouragement had fired her up this much. Windsor gave me a look, and I just spread my hands to show I was as innocent as anyone.

With my mana recovered, I went back to making ice and listing it the moment it was done. And once my mana emptied again, I pulled out the items Windsor had hunted today to appraise. The first was a card, a small gem or vein resource card green, which on use spawned a small gem or vein in my territory.

And the appraisal cheerfully noted that gems were a precious resource in my luck was good. So, my plan was for her to stay folded into my shadow as a reserve. The knife I draw only when someone forced my hand. With the arrangement settled, I started my daily resource fishing. Windsor had pulled the line the last two days, and now I wanted to test my own luck.

The first thing that surfaced was a bridge construction blueprint, a green item that builds a bridge at a chosen spot to better connect a territory at a cost of 500 stone. I couldn't decide whether it was useful or clutter, so I set it aside to feed into 100 transformations that night and let the system roll it into something better.

The second pull was a luxury bathing set, also green, which promised to lift my mood with an actual bath. And that one I genuinely wanted since lately I'd been boiling water by hand and wiping myself down like a man living in a tent.

A real bathtub with an automatic heater sounded close to luxury, except it needed a power source and a great deal of fresh water, and I couldn't bathe in sea water. I made a note to solve the power problem and upgrade the seawater converter, then frowned because spending pure water on a bath felt wasteful enough that I half wanted to feed this into 100 transformations, too.

The third item appeared while I was still arguing with myself. So, I called the two commoners over to haul the first two pieces into the store room and looked at what was left in my hands. It was a cloth bag, and the description told me it held element gems, a special mineral good for construction, and more interestingly, for strengthening certain special beings.

I burst out laughing because that second use was exactly what I needed. I tore the bag open and counted 35 fire element gems, 26 gold element gems, 12 water element gems, and I decided on the spot that this one bag was worth more to me than the bathtub in the bridge put together.

I went looking for Huawa and found her at the stern, drilling her blazing body while Ha Rui sat perched on the railing, watching with the patience of a much older creature. The moment they saw me, both launched off and came to roost on my shoulders. And when I drew out the fire and gold gems, their eyes lit up like I'd offered them candy.

That reaction told me everything. It wasn't only spirit crystal and purple crystal that fed an elemental growth. Gems did the job, too, and that opened a whole new supply line in my head. I handed them one gem each, both no bigger than a thumb pad, and watched them eat in their wildly different ways. Huh. Breathed a thread of flame, melted the red gem to slag, and drank the molten light straight in.

Ha Rui was rougher, her small, pale hand flushing gold as she crushed the gem to powder in one squeeze, then licked the golden dust off her palm with a tiny tongue. Thoroughly pleased with herself, I pocketed the rest and decided to save them until after the competition because if they gorged now, they'd have to digest and a pet busy digesting in the middle of a war fights at half strength.

With that done, I told Huawa to keep grinding blazing body and set Harui to working her hardened gold body since I'd half decided to convert her into a hero eventually, and toughening her physique now might push her closer to an evolution.

Ever since Hui came to me, a quiet ambition had been taking shape to gather every type of elemental spirit I could find and raise each to evolve because something in my gut insisted the payoff would be enormous. I knew it was a long road, an absurdly difficult one, but I felt no rush, the kind of project a person chips away at for years whenever the chance appears.

So, I spent the rest of that stretch on the stern watching my two little spirits train until the goddess broke the calm with a global announcement. The systems voice rolled out across the whole world. The two domains competition was about to begin and participants should ready themselves. It would run as a two armies confrontation with starting positions assigned by rank.

The higher ranked lords placed further forward. Killing demon domain novice lords would grant merit points. The whole thing would end once participants dropped to 40% and it would start in 5 minutes with Chon Mo wishing everyone a victorious opening. I picked the announcements apart one at a time while the count ran down. The first line told me nothing useful.

The second mattered because it implied my own position would land somewhere far back, which for most people would be a blessing since the rear is the safe place to be. For me, it was the opposite of what I wanted. Further back meant fewer kills, fewer ship cores, and I badly needed ship cores to grow my fleet.

So, my only hope was that my teleport binding with Lee Kio keyed off her position instead of mine and dragged me up to the front row with her. There are always trade-offs. I hadn't ground the arena for points and white stone, and now I'd lost my forward slot. The bill simply coming due.

The third line reminded me I'd earned merit points, the same currency I'd seen last time I crossed into the demon domain. Except that thanks to a long talk with the gym mountain turtle, I now grasped how valuable they really were. Inside Shanmo City, merit points buy things whit stone can't touch. So this run, I intended an outright killing spree, harvesting demon domain lords for ship cores and merit both.

The fourth line was the crulest. An ending threshold of 40% meant that on average 6 and 10 people on both sides were going to die. Somewhere around 6,000 corpses, and I knew that number would scare many qualified lords into staying home, which the system permitted since Shano never forced anyone through the gate. I opened the chat channel, and the wall of reactions was exactly what I expected.

People called it a slaughter, a butchering. Somebody complained they'd assumed it would be a search and hunt format like before instead of a head-on collision. A few announced they were folding, declaring it too dangerous, and the Boulder ones jered at them for cowards, and chanted that fortune favors the brave, that whoever survived this would come out rich.

In the end, most chose to go in anyway because the 10,000 who'd clawed their way this far were the ambitious ones, the killers, and they weren't about to flinch now. I took out the binding rope Leakio had given me, tied it to my wrist, and settled in to wait for the teleport while I use the leftover minutes to check the strength of my own side, which I hadn't reviewed in days.

Windsor read back as a legendary hero, a light element messenger now at level seven with strength in the 60s once her bonus is stacked, agility and defense up there with it, wisdom holding at 60, and a mana pool past 600. Her skills covered healing, light arrow, light shield, plus the blue grade entangling spell and bewilder. And she carried the purple scepter of light and a green hairpin.

Her loyalty sat at a flat 100 and her affinity at 98, the kind that reads his open admiration. The detail that made me sit up was her attribute growth 10 points this level instead of the usual five. And the meaning wasn't subtle. If higher quality units start growing twice as fast at a certain threshold, the gap between a legendary and a lesser hero stops being a gap and becomes a canyon.

And I genuinely didn't know whether the growth would double again at the general stage. If it did, the math at the top of the ladder was going to get terrifying. Dark Moon checked in as a level five legendary hero, a knigh hunter with attributes noticeably padded by the rare spirit treasure she'd consumed. Enough that a level five Dark Moon already matched what a normal legendary wouldn't reach until level six.

Her kit ran darkness blade, darkness erosion, moon shadow, and the blue grade shadow stealth and flying sword technique. With a green shadow blade in hand, loyalty at 100, and affinity at 75, which the system labeled close.

I noted the way a merchant notes a depreciating asset that the treasure boost looked huge now, but would shrink to a rounding error once her base numbers climbed since one or two herbs mean a lot at level 5 and almost nothing at level 15.

Little blue rounded out the readout as a level seven deep sea giant whale, 30 m of purple grade muscle, carrying impact bite, thunders sword, and a freshly awakened giant wave. Pets, it seemed, just grow their own skills as they level, which made me wonder whether Huawa would keep awakening abilities on her own after I turned her into a hero.

Though, since she could still evolve unassisted, she'd probably keep learning, too. And only her next level up would prove it. I ran a quick eye over the commoners and found most had hit level five from fishing every single day, which suited me fine. Then, my ship began to change color, shifting to white, shot through with blue patterns, and a spatial gate opened ahead.

I gave the order without hesitating. Little Blue slid forward to take point. The tower ship following close in its wake and my vision blacked out blazed white and left my mind swimming.

When I came back to myself, I stood inside the two domains competition arena and the first thing in front of me was a transparent light barrier with rank upon rank of black ships bearing red patterns behind it so densely packed they stretched past where I could see. The ships at the very front were level six wooden craft.

I was still taking the measure of it all when a startled shout cut across the water. Somebody crying out that I was completely unfair. I turned my head and found Lee Kio on her own wooden boat. Gawking at me with her eyes stretched wide.

I smiled and told her I genuinely owed her thanks this time that I was riding her coattails up to the front and never would have landed such a good spot otherwise, which was the plain truth since without her item, I'd be stuck in the rear watching my profit shrink.

Her eyes went sly and she darted them around before proposing with theatrical sweetness that we just split the gains from any demon domain lord we killed together right down the middle. I smiled back and told her I wouldn't let her lose out, a deliberately empty thing to say because of course I wasn't agreeing to an even split.

I'd already counted the heads on her deck, and while she had more bodies than I did, they were plainly far weaker, so an equal share would have been a losing trade. When the time came, I'd hand her something I didn't care about and call the favor repaid. Leio pouted at my non-answer since she'd known I wouldn't take the even split, but had at least expected some gesture, not a polished, useless sentence.

Then she huffed and asked with the bluntness of real envy, how I'd managed to stack up so many highquality characters, half accusing me of cheating. Anyone who just laid eyes on a tower ship, two legendary heroes, and a golden pet would have asked the same.

And the funny part was that I was still hiding the better half of my hand with little blue lurking on the seabed and dark moon tucked into my shadow. I gave her an irritated look and told her there was no way to cheat with the goddess watching everything. That I was simply luckier than most. And that last part I meant.

It had started as a single wooden plank, and only because misfortune had hidden a fortune inside it. Only because the goddess's compensation had landed in my hands. Was I standing where I stood now. Le Kio believed me well enough. The question had only been her way of letting off a little of the imbalance churning in her chest.

Right then, a second wooden boat blinked into place beside Lie Kio, another level six craft, and both of us looked over at once. Leio greeted the newcomer as Bakes and ribbed her for being slow, and Bakes said something had come up and held her back. Then Leo turned to me with an exaggeratedly pitiful expression and asked whether I minded that she'd brought in another ally without clearing it with me.

I did mind because she'd acted on her own and quietly cast herself as the leader of our little group and that needled me more than I let show. But things were already set. The urgent business was killing demon domain lords for profit and everything else could wait until the war was over.

I decided privately that if I ever worked with Lee Kio again, I'd keep a card hidden in my sleeve because the girl was not an honest operator. Out loud, I just smiled and said, "One more person was. " And I watched the unease flicker across her face because if I'd snapped at her, she'd have felt forgiven.

Whereas a calm non-reaction told her the splinter was lodged deep, the kind that quietly sour future cooperation. So I took Huawa and crossed the plank she laid between the holes.

I went for two reasons. The first was that I trusted Lee Kio not to attack me while our interests were chained together. Lee Kio said my name needed no introduction and Bakeol smiled and called me the pride of the Joe Kingdom before offering her hand and hoping aloud for chances to work together. A small note of approval that I hadn't held the grip a beat too long or tried to take advantage. I was quietly amused. Of course, I hadn't when I already had Windsor and Dark Moon, and even otherwise, I wasn't about to tangle myself up with a fellow lord.

Since the relationship there began and ended at cooperation, Lee Kio decided we should all lay our cards on the table and went first, naming her profession as archer and her talent as Eagle-Eye, which let her lock onto a target more cleanly before introducing her epic shield warrior hero Guru.

Only now did it land that she couldn't beat even my subordinates. And the way they looked at me afterward like some kind of monster started to get genuinely uncomfortable when bake seal pressed reluctantly about my own strength.

They'd clearly wanted to ask whether I wasn't afraid of dying while underleveled. The muscle is right there if danger comes. I sat watching the two of them deflate under the weight of my introduction, privately smug and more than a little entertained. While we'd been talking, the field had been filling in, and when I looked around, I noticed something off and stood up, frowning.

Most of the ships arriving now were level three or four, some even level two, which made no sense among the supposed top 10,000. Helpless that they'd never lead this trio with me towering over it. Relieved that the trio had me in it at all and reassured after a real conversation that I was decent enough to partner with for the long haul. Back on my own deck, Windsor came to meet me, looking me over with open worry and only easing once she'd confirmed I wasn't hurt.

The transparent wall began to ripple, thinning by degrees until it simply wasn't there. And my eyes went hard. Ahead of us, the demon domain ships started to move. Black holes and red patterns rolling forward row on row, giving off a strange pressure, and as they closed, I could hear shouting carry across the water. The spirit domain lords answered with a roar that seemed to shake the sky.

Lie Kio took the lead, and her boat surged out. Bakes yol and I falling in close behind and the whole spirit domain front began to move. Tens of thousands of holes setting off at once on the sea. A long black and red line and a long white and blue line rushed at each other and met. An arrow flew.

Then two whole forests of arrows crossed in the air. Then magic of every color slammed together and then the fleets themselves crunched into one another. I'd layered myself in four shields before the first contact. A light shield, a fire shield, a golden shield, and my own. And I'd done the same for Windsor, Huawa, and Haoui. His death triggering one of the goddess's notifications in my ear that I had no spare attention for. Watching me tear through the fleet, a brown-haired, blue-eyed man on a level six demon domain wooden ship was in a foul mood.

Since the ceiling of any achievement is set by the ground beneath it, he pointed at my ship and ordered his people to attack, and the boat came about and bore down on me. Kajuneo had clearly noted my hull outranked his, but it didn't rattle him because a higher level ship isn't automatically a stronger one. And besides, he believed he could handle me and my group.

And on top of that, he held a trump card. After I had torn apart yet another ship, I caught his boat angling toward mine. And even though it was only a level six craft, I didn't relax because a level six in the demon domain meant a front runner, the kind of lord who might be carrying something strange.

And I had no intention of capsizing in a puddle. I thought for a moment, then sent little blue under. Little Blue Dove and swam toward the target. And Kajuneo, knowing nothing of its existence, took out an item and pressed it into the hand of a short, thin man beside him. He told the man to wait for the opening, strike at the enemy lord, and kill in a single blow. The man swore he wouldn't miss.

This was a hero of Kajuneo's named Mocha, a stealth hunter, an assassin by trade, and Kajuneo trusted him enough to nod once and look back at my tower ship with a cold little smile. " Bakes smiled because now there was a thread binding us, the kind that thickens into a real relationship through repeated dealings. All of it hammered home a warning that the waters of this world ran far deeper than I'd assumed and one careless step could drop me into a trap.

Before long, the demon domain lords in my stretch couldn't hold against the four of us and started to retreat. And naturally, we chased, watching the black and red ships fall back toward the neighboring battlefields in a ragged arc that crashed into the surrounding fighting. The retreating lords merged into the demon domain forces nearby. But I didn't care and kept attacking, and the two battlefields began folding into one.

I noticed that the spirit domain leader in this new battlefield commanded a level six wooden ship carrying as many as 50 commoners all in leather armor with bows. And it surprised me since I had no idea how anyone fed that many mouths. The surprise lasted only a moment because everyone has their own fortune. And I'm not arrogant enough to think I'm the only lucky man at sea.

Any more than bake seal's legendary hero was a fluke. The lord of that ship surprised me too because at the moment he looked like a lion tribe orc. A leather bag of meterlong javelins slung across his back, hurling them with such force that almost every throw took a life. An invincible figure in his corner of the fight.

He noticed us arrive, spared us a single glance, and went right back to slaughtering the demon domain lords in front of him. I had no intention of approaching just then, so I returned to my own hunting. Since out here, a single lord kill banked not only whit stone, but a ship core and a territory core, the bedrock of everything I meant to build.

With my group added to the line, the balance tipped and the side already losing crumbled faster. This time before they could even flee because spirit domain ships were already streaming toward them from the flank with demon domain holes in pursuit. The fleeing demon domain lord and the fleeing spirit domain lord collided. The two clusters grinding past one another until both sides shot clear and reformed with their main forces.

After which everyone paused to size up the new shape of things among the demon domain ships that had just given chase. Their bitterness sliding onto Leio. Losers always reached for an excuse before they reached for a lesson. So I cut in with a cold snort saying anyone who chose to enter the two domains competition should have come prepared to die and asking who walks into a slaughter expecting to lean on others. The crowd's faces shifted because none of them had counted on anyone else before entering. And the 10,000 who'd made it this far were no fools.

They all knew relying on yourself beats relying on anyone. They'd only been steered for a moment, and my words made them waver. I ignored the lord, whose expression was souring, and got ahead of him, telling them not to let themselves be used, that he was spending them like pawns to reach his own ends. He retorted exasperated, asking what goal he could possibly have and calling it an excuse.

But I smiled and pointed out he'd shifted the subject, then laid it bare, that he wanted to use them to accuse us and seize leadership of the fleet. His expression cracked wide open, and I knew I'd guessed right. And once I'd said it, the crowd reacted, their gazes turning wary on him instead of us, and he understood his plan was dead.

He'd wanted the leadership for the prestige and the future benefits, and now it was ruined. So he rounded on me, demanding what right I had to speak, and who I even was. He'd registered the unusual look of my towership, but irrationality had the better of him, and the words spilled out.

I laughed because the man was genuinely a bit slow, trying to use influence to suppress people without once weighing his own and daring to try it on me. I said my name softly, Ha Shin and asked whether I was qualified to speak now. The change in his face was immediate and the chatter erupted.

People muttering that it was actually the real Big Shot that Lee Kio had allied with Hashin and maybe their guesses had been right, wondering aloud how strong I was. Whereas if I let him live a while longer to go kill demon domain lords than killed him last everything of his would fall into my hands right now he was just a laborer working for me and I genuinely hoped he'd rack up a big body count since the more he harvested the richer my eventual take so I said magnanimously that now wasn't the time to settle personal scores and that we could talk after we dealt with the demon domain the others were stunned for a beat then caught my drift and smiled faintly since whatever the grudges this was no moment for infighting Hagawa understood my thinking too played dumb and smiled, leaning on whatever item he held, plainly unsure who'd come out on top.

" Lie Kio echoed that we'd still fight individually, but cover one another, and Hagawa put in quickly that no one was to stab an ally in the back while we fought the demon domain.

Windsor and the others snapped alert, and even Lee Kio and Bakes nearby grew, and barely had I finished speaking when the water heaved, and water arrows erupted across the whole sky, raining out in every direction. All sitting around 110, carrying floating water arrow technique, fire shield, fireball, water cage technique, and the area melting firecloud descent. Noon had crept up while we bled, so I sent everyone off to eat and rest before we resumed fishing. And soon, I was working through a meal my laid out. While I ate, I opened the chat channel to catch up on the fallout. The regional board was a wall of the usual worship. The boss is a freak.

The surplus coming from demon domain lords who had killed spirit domain lords and fed me the extra cores indirectly.

A real boon for someone raising two elemental spirits, even if the vein would surely be thin. That one caught my interest because if it could feed me electrical power, it might crack my ship's electricity problem.
