I sighed and put it away.

Out on the deck, a few lords were already fishing. So, I handed a stack of rods to one man and told him to distribute them. That was when I noticed Pan sitting off to the side, the rock race tank, who had surrendered to me, a hero I had collected but never appraised. I walked over and he scrambled up to salute the instant he saw me.

I told him there was no need for ceremony and asked whether his quarters had been sorted out and he confirmed the Lord had arranged it. I nodded and told him these new comeers would be his responsibility going forward. Then asked if that posed any problem since my plan was to hand the management of ordinary residents off entirely.

From here out, Windsor and the others and I would stop babysitting the rank and file and serve as the core leadership of the territory, parceling the day-to-day out to other heroes and concerning ourselves only with managing those heroes well. " And I gave a satisfied grunt and finally ran my spirit eye over him. The readout pleased me.

Siokpan came back at fine quality, a step above the ordinary I had assumed. Level six, strength 30, agility 29, defense 32 before gear bonuses, wisdom 30, 300 mana, a shield warrior hero carrying stone skin art, great shield art, and gigantification wielding a green lapis lassly shield. The unexpected quality was a small gift. But what really caught my eye was his loyalty at 83 with affinity not far behind.

The loyalty of any hero I had not summoned myself would never start at full, but 83 was a fine base and would climb with time.

As I kept conquering, I would keep subduing heroes this way since leaning on summoning alone was never going to scale and imperfect loyalty was no concern so long as they would not bolt at the first hard wind, provided I kept the high posts staffed by heroes I had summoned.

I waved Sock Pan off to his duties and turned to the three drops from the two purple sea beasts, hoping they paid out since I had nearly died earning them. The first was an incomplete magic eye skill stone, permanent dropped by the 10-armed demon octopus, damaged by some prior misfortune so that all it retained was the power to read information.

I was delighted regardless, even stripped of its offense, an appraisal stone solved a real headache since I could have Dark Moon and the others learn it. And Windsor had complained more than once that without an identification skill, her teams had a harder time hunting alone than when I rode along. With this stone, Windsor and the rest could lead their own crews from here on.

And since it was permanent, every hero under me could learn it in turn. I sprawled on the deck chair, laughing over the find. And that was when Windsor and Dark Moon wandered over. Huawa and Harui having already gone off to drill. Windsor settled into the chair beside mine and asked what had me so pleased. I gave them the short version and handed the stone across.

And the two, surprised and pleased, learned it without hesitation. I moved on to the second drop, a broken 10-earmed demon octopus beast core, blue, holding the residual energy of the beast and good for a wide spread of uses. I sat there a little stunned because this was effectively a magic core, the very thing I had hunted for after every seab beast kill early on and never found until I gave up looking.

The only frustration was the timing since I had spent two whole weeks gutting low-grade sea beasts and the drop rate had to be miserable for me to surface one only now. At least I had confirmed beast cores were real and obtainable. The third drop was a fire cloud descent skill stone purple granting the skill on use with the learning requirement of a fire element profession.

I was happy at first read and then immediately conflicted because Huawa could obviously learn it as it stood. Yet, if I ran my hundred transformations over it, I might bump it to gold with the risk that it would turn into a skill stone she could not use at all. It was a real dilemma, and I shelved it to decide that night.

I pushed the matter out of my head and turned to the ship, still battered with a snapped railing, which I could hardly die for the afternoon hunt. So, I upgraded it straight off, and the whole craft came back fresh as new. The readout showed it at level two now, 50 m x 15, three floors.

The first floor held the kitchen, a large living room, a restroom, the observation deck, the fishing corridor, the mast, and the helm. The second floor held the core cabin, two large living rooms, four small ones, a warehouse, and a restroom. The new third floor added a kitchen, two large living rooms, four small ones, water storage, a restaurant, its power coming from multi-or linkage propulsion.

The technology slate read ship drainage at level three, ship thermal energy at level two, ship automation at level one, multi-or linkage propulsion at level three, and ship diving at a weekend level three with diving as its special ability. The new floor had bought me the restaurant and the water storage, and I was glad of both. I opened the technology interface and got to work.

First, I pushed the diving technology to level four, then bumped the propulsion technology to level 5, and finally unlocked the complete version of the diving technology and built it as a level one complete setup. My white stone was flush this time, and after watching that octopus shred my railing, I had decided a diving system with a shield was no luxury.

The complete version held for only an hour at a stretch, but an hour of protected diving could swing a desperate moment my way. With that settled, I went down to the small living room where the upgradeable fixtures lived because my swollen headcount meant the seawater converter in the flower producer could no longer keep pace.

I took the seawater converter up to level 10 in one shot, lifting its output to,280 L a day, enough that I could finally take a bath. And a good thing the new water storage existed or hoarding that much would have been its own problem. Then I ran the flower producer up to level seven.

Reaching 200 kg a day, plenty for my whole crew with enough surplus that I might cook something fine and sell it since white flour was a rarity now. When the spending stopped, I had just under 13,000 white stone left. So I kept the change and dumped the rest into cloth and cotton wading because winter prep never sleeps.

Then I fired up the diving function and took the crew hunting beneath the waves, staying submerged 4 to 5 hours with the upgrades. An hour of it now under the full shield. Barely two hours into the hunt, the goddess Chiomo cut in with an announcement, declaring the weekly survival point settlement that everyone would draw a basic reward by their points, and that the top 10 should choose their bonus point rewards.

I left Windsor and the others hunting and ducked into a random room to claim mine. The basic reward was a packet of water and food I scarcely glanced at since neither meant much to me anymore, and I went straight to the bonus, which offered three items in a choice of one.

The first option was a Muroke Talisman, Purple, described flatly as an identification token of the Muroke and nothing more, which made me frown at how little it told me. The second was a large fertile soil resource card, purple, which on use grants my territory a stretch of rich earth, where anything planted yields far more than ordinary ground, and that one was genuinely strong.

The third was an Earth Origin Ginsing seed, purple, that grows into the rare spirit treasure Earth Origin green flower when planted. And my eyes went wide because I already held the seed of the Earth Origin Mother Gins and a second purple heavenly treasure herb to pair with it was no small thing.

The Earth Origin seed obviously raised raw stats, but a seed is a seed with no telling how long it would take to bear, so it might do nothing for a long stretch beyond patting my foundation. The fertile soil, by contrast, would pay off the instant I had ground a plant, multiplying every crop and feeding more mouths, and I knew well that a territory grows on bodies, and bodies grow on food.

Then there was the Muroke Talisman, which I simply could not read, and I sat there genuinely torn between the three. In the end, I chose the Muroke Talisman against my own instincts. I did not understand it, which was exactly why I wanted to gamble on it. And even a dud, I could run through hundred transformations and salvage as a gold item.

I pocketed it and hunted alongside Windsor and the others until our time ran out. The sky had dimmed to dusk when we surfaced, and I went straight inside. The level two core cabin had grown a third room, which I gave to Huawa and Hui. After dinner, I cleared the white stone off the money tree, dropped onto the sofa, and got ready for the day's 100 transformations.

But first, I skimmed the trading list Wang Mo had forwarded. The battle had pushed some higher grade items onto the market, though high-grade did not mean good things like fancy spoons and chopsticks, and I bought up nearly all of them anyway since they were cheap and made perfect fodder for transforming later. A glance at my accounts showed roughly 7,000 white stone in hand, having pulled over 5,000 from the afternoon's hunt alone.

I was about to begin transforming when a friend message stopped me and I opened it puzzled to find bake seol. The first thing in the thread was an image and beneath it a single line asking whether I needed this. I opened the image and read a talent quality enhancement orb blue which carries a chance to improve a talent depending on the user's existing grade.

And my heart jumped because I wanted that thing badly. I fired back that it was exactly what I needed and offered to trade her my wood element talent mutation orb for it since I already understood what she was after. Why she preferred my mutation orb to using this herself. Whether to deepen the tie or because she valued the mutation orb more, I neither knew nor cared.

So long as the trade went through, I listed the mutation orb on the trade channel earmarked for her and the swap closed in seconds, leaving the blue talent quality enhancement orb in my hand. I did not dare run my hundred transformations over this one because if it shifted into anything else, I would have to go find a corner and weep.

So, I used it straight, nerves prickling, praying it would not fail. Then, I pulled up my own information and the result knocked the breath out of me because my talent now read 100 transformations. Legendary, able to drive any item through a positive random change, able to specify the change type at will, with the resulting quality guaranteed to climb one full grade three times a day.

I laughed out loud at being a legendary grade hero in my own right now. The part about specifying the change type lit my eyes up because it meant I could take a humble cup and name equipment to get equipment or name blueprint to get a blueprint. The dice loaded in my favor at last.

I could not keep the grin off my face since the upgrade gutted most of the old randomness that had burned me so many times. The thought struck me that if my talent had climbed once, it could climb again. And if it kept climbing, it might one day let me transform anything into precisely what I wanted, which would make me flatly unbeatable.

The one catch was that specifying type was not the same as specifying item. And the talent quality enhancement oreb belonged to the special item category. So even if I named the special type, the odds of coughing one up would stay miserable since special items were too numerous.

Then I drew out the water cup I had bought today, the highest grade thing in my cart at blue quality and specified blueprint type before running the 100 transformations, choosing blueprints because they held real value. And a blueprint duly appeared in my hand to read. It was a 100 spirits pavilion construction blueprint.

Purple, a building that summons creatures of every race with a chance of pulling heroes and keeps a steady trickle of commoners flowing into the territory. Raising it called for 1,000 wood, 500 stone, 200 refined iron, 100 refined copper, and 10 gems each of fire, water, and earth. I felt mixed about it. Calling it useful, I could already pull commoners with civilian summoning cards, which were never hard to come by.

Calling it useless, it still carried a chance at heroes, though I knew full well that chance was thin as paper. It was the textbook chicken rib too bland to relish and too wasteful to toss. So I filed it to build later since a purple building was a purple building.

Next I took out the broken 10 armed demon octopus beast core blue specified the same type and transformed it and out came a 10 armed demon octopus beast core at purple. The same energy reservoir bumped a full grade which sent me into thought. Two lessons sat in that experiment. First beast cores were their own distinct category. So going forward I could specify the beast core type to fish for cores.

Though below high quality, it would never be worth a precious transformation. Second, even with a legendary talent, specifying the same type as the source made the result overwhelmingly likely to be a related item useful for steering future transforms.

Filing both away, I sent Windsor to call Huawa and Harui over, held the purple beast core out, and asked Huawa whether she could use it on the logic that since she and Harui had first shown up as pets, it ought to do something for one of them. But both elves shook their heads. So, I gave all the elemental gems I had on hand to the two of them.

Ran the rest of the off-p gems through the element converter into purple crystals and left the core cabin to test the beast core on Little Blue. Outside, the whole ship was cutting forward under Liuk's command with Little Blue swimming out ahead of the bow. I called my whale over, held up the 10-earmed demon octopus beast core, and asked whether it was any use to him.

Little Blue let out a happy cry, so I tossed the core across, and he opened his mouth and swallowed it whole. I patted his huge head and went back to the core cabin for the last item, the fire cloud descent skill stone I had been agonizing over. I specified its type and ran the hundred transformations and let out a long breath when it resolved into a fire meteor fall skill stone.

Gold learnable by anyone with a fire element or earth element profession. Mercifully, it had not warped into a skill stone nobody could touch. This one was going to Huawa, the first goldrade skill stone I had ever pulled. So once she learned it, slipped into her martial god form, and let it loose, the result would surely be devastating.

And I could not wait to watch it. She had not fully awakened yet, but her drilling had nudged the odds of triggering the form a little higher. The sort of thing that could not be forced, only worked at while nature took its course.

I got up and went to bed, and the one waiting was Dark Moon, since Winter had gone to rest in the new room, and I understood perfectly well what that arrangement was meant to convey. Morning came, and I opened my eyes to find Dark Moon already up.

I rose and not long after I had watched Mory had the meal laid out, I sat at the table and watched Dark Moon and Windsor come out of the inner room together. And when Dark Moon's eyes met mine, her deliberately cold face flushed Scarlet. I chuckled while Windsor shot me a mock glare, having clearly already been told everything.

After eating, I handed the resource fishing rod to Windsor for the day's three pulls. And once she had gone, I settled on the sofa to dig into technology. While Mory, with nothing to do, drifted over, knelt, and began kneading my legs. I let her be because I had a real problem to crack, the matter of electricity. So, I searched the keyword directly.

The result was the ship electrical technology, which once lit would give the tower ship the conditions to run on electrical power. I had stumbled onto it before and failed to light it. So, I tried again, and the prompt came up the same as last time, stating that a level three tower ship could light it.

The condition had not been met before, and it seemed the prompt only shifted its wording once the requirement was nearly within reach. I nodded since at my current rate of earning, it would take only a handful of days to push the ship to level three and unlock it. And I closed the panel.

I was on my way out when Shanmo broke in with a fresh announcement congratulating everyone on surviving two full weeks and promising that we would now come to understand this world more deeply with a wish for a pleasant journey. And that single paragraph left me genuinely puzzled. A bare congratulation was not Chamo's style. Every previous time the goddess had spoken, it had carried real weight.

So, I turned the line over and over, and the phrase about understanding this world more deeply was what snagged me. I remembered what the Jimson Mountain Turtle had told me long ago, that Shawn Mo restricted the movements of the various races for the sole purpose of slowly tempering novice lords like us into something stronger.

The natural reading now was that Shan Mo had lifted those restrictions, though probably only a partial lifting, which meant that from here on, my journey would likely start running me into people of other races. The thought left me uneasy and beneath the unease, eager, because I genuinely wanted to see this world's true face for once, and the prospect made my chest tighten with something close to excitement.

I calmed myself and walked out onto the deck where the day had turned gloomy, no sun reaching through. Dark clouds packed thick overhead, the sea stretching boundless beneath them in a way that pressed down on the chest.

I went to the deck chair cluster and patted Dark Moon who rose to make room, then leaned back and pulled her down onto my lap, my hand finding her cat ears and her body shivered before she sank into me. Windsor watched the whole performance, said nothing, and merely rolled her eyes, and I chuckled and started in on a glass of juice, savoring the rare quiet.

It did not hold long. Little Blue let out a cry not far off, and I turned to see his whole body heaving, the skin splitting open as fresh wounds tore wide and blood streamed out. The three of us were on our feet at once, certain some enemy had ambushed him. But after a tense stretch, no other sea beast surfaced, and my mind clicked into place.

The beast core I had fed him the night before had to be the cause. So I told Windsor and Dark Moon it was nothing. That little blue was simply evolving, and the reassurance settled them. Though Windsor still wo a steady healing over the whale to dull the pain.

The wound slowly knitted shut as the crying tapered off, and where the bloody gashes had been, there now ran lines of red pattern across his hide, lending him a faintly demonic cast. I opened his information. Little blue now a deep sea demon whale at purple 30 meters long level seven strength 52 agility 48 defense 48. His skills now ram tear thunders sword giant wave and a new one transformation.

The name had shifted from deep sea giant whale to deep sea demon whale. His stats had ticked up and the beast core had plainly done him good. I waited until the evolution had settled before I asked what transformation did.

Little Blue swayed side to side, and under our startled eyes, his body shrank fast until he was barely a meter or two long, then darted around the water, leaping clear of the surface, looking thoroughly pleased with himself, which struck me as both funny and exasperating since it told me his mental age was not running especially high.

I asked whether shrinking was the whole of it, since at that scale the skill looked pointless. And little blue stopped, swayed again, and this time his body swelled instead, growing until he hit a full 60 m, double his natural length, longer now than my own towers ship, which widened my eyes. That was a real card after all, since sheer size carries plenty of advantages when the moment calls for it.

I patted his head and sat back down. And just then, a commoner handed me a blueprint, which turned out to be for yet another reclining chair. A little deflating, but I built it. And Dark Moon promptly stretched out on it. At that same moment, Windsor reeled something up and handed me three items she had caught that morning.

The first was a Starlight magic staff at Green Grade that lifts the caster's mana by 10%. And I decided to run my 100 transformations over it that night, and if it stayed a staff, pass it to Huawa, my key training project and current Trump in the team.

The second was a diving respirator blueprint at green grade for a respirator that lets a person move freely through the ocean, requiring five wood, 10 plastic, three iron blocks, and two copper blocks. It did not look especially useful, and the recipe calling for wood of all things confused me. So, I decided to transform it that night under the blueprint type, and see whether anything better fell out.

The last item was a medium magic horn horse herd at bluegrade, a resource card that yields magic horn horses on use, good for hauling or even as cavalry. And I sat straight up since bluegrade meant I could pull a purple item out of it. And I tucked it away, pleased.

With nothing else pressing before the afternoon hunt, I opened the chat channel, glancing first at the regional board, where the chatter was thin and the headcount had dropped under 3,000. That stopped me cold. It meant that in 2 weeks 70% of the people in my region had already died, brutal beyond what I had assumed.

And if other regions had bled at the same rate, then the total across this world had to be astronomical. Some part of me hoped the slaughter elsewhere had run gentler than mine. I switched to the World Channel where someone had asked what this week's disaster would be. That drew the usual bravado about blocking soldiers with soldiers and damning water with earth.

A jab about brains being decorative organs and a nonsequitular craving for hot pot pig brain that made me smile despite myself. The whole exchange a sign that plenty of people could no longer stomach the monotony of the food. Then a single message dropped into the channel like a stone in distilled water and threw up a thousand ripples.

Someone shouting that he thought he had spotted an island. The board exploded. Half told him he was hallucinating a giant sea beast into a land mass. Half were desperate to know if it was real. And one confessed he had half believed Shanmo was lying about islands existing at all.

The spotter announced he was going in for a closer look and asked the brothers to wish him luck, which they did in a flood. The replies kept refreshing with no further word, so he had clearly set off to investigate. The news startled me, too, because I had assumed I would be first to make landfall, only for someone else to stumble onto an island ahead of me.

By my reckoning, the first person to land and found a territory would draw a massive reward and open a gap over everyone who founded theirs later. So, a thread of anxiety wound through me as I sat glued to the channel. After a stretch, the spotter came back crowing that it really was an island and that he was the luckiest man alive, which set off a fresh chorus of envy.

People demanding to know why no island had ever crossed their path. And my own heart sank a little watching it. They pressed him to found his territory and described what one even looked like. Several noting he had beaten even me to it, while another cut in that this came down to pure luck.

The man promised he would found it right then, and the board lit up with people clamoring for a description, asking whether founding took this long, accusing him of hoarding the news. After 2 or 3 minutes of silence, the channel grew restless, and I was restless along with them because I urgently wanted that founding information myself.

When the spotter finally spoke again, it was to whale that he was crying, that he could not found a territory, which the board met with open disbelief, accusing him of inventing an excuse to keep the details to himself, demanding he stop stalling and tell them. He swore he was not lying and the doubt kept pouring in. I had assumed at first too that he was angling to hide the information.

But after watching so long, his words rang sincere, not the cadence of a man spinning a lie. And it really did seem he simply could not found a territory there. So I sent my own message into the flood asking plainly what the situation was and why he could not build an island. And although my line got buried under the churn, almost the moment I sent it, a few sharpeyed people caught it.

Word spread fast when calling for everyone to stop sending messages because the boss was speaking. Another asking why he had missed it. A third snapping at the rest to quit shoving the message down. Ever since the two domain competition closed, my standing among the spirit domain lords had climbed to something close to towering. And within a minute, the traffic thinned.

I sent my question a second time and added a line asking everyone to hold off and let the man speak. Somewhere out there, a young lord in a long robe, five companions sitting around him on the grass, shot to his feet. So suddenly he startled the people beside him, blurting that the boss was talking to him and scrambled to reply.

He insisted everything he had said was true, sounding wronged. And I told him I believed him and only wanted the specifics. So he laid it out. He had taken out his territory core and declared his intent to found a territory and gotten no response beyond Shanmo's prompt that the conditions were not met. And wandering the island to try several spots had drawn the same prompt every time, which he could not explain.

My mind raced over it, not meeting the conditions to found a territory what would produce that. One possibility was that the island was already occupied since only one territory could exist per island. Another was that the island was missing some necessary element, leaving it short of the requirements. Beyond those two, I could not guess.

I laid out both for him and told him to explore carefully and stay alert since he might turn up something unexpected. And he answered that he understood and would post the moment he had news. Rather than reply, I sent him a friend request. And Yupong, as his name read, accepted instantly in obvious excitement.

I told him I suspected this island was exactly the deeper understanding of the world Chiommo had hinted at that morning, that he should roam it and might find something, but to be careful, and he promised to notify me the second anything turned up. He never raised the matter of a reward, and I understood the calculation.

Asking for one would reduce us to a single transaction, whereas staying quiet might let him build a standing connection with me, and the long-term payoff beat any one-time bounty. Still, I told him plainly that he should notify me if he found anything and I would give him a fitting reward because if you want the horse to run, you have to feed it grass.

He shot back that I was looking down on him, which puzzled me, then declared that working for me was his honor and that I should never mention rewards again because it insulted him. Delivered with a shameless earnestness that left me dumbfounded since this uppong was plainly his own breed of oddball. I had no idea how to handle that sort of person.

So, I told him to go explore quickly, and he swore he would guarantee the mission, then turned to his companions and told him to start sweeping the island and stay sharp for traps.

Yupeong and his crew hacked through the undergrowth and pressed inward, taking the occasional attack from monster beasts native to the island, though those were weak enough that even Yupang at level 5 could handle one alone, let alone with five at his side, one an ordinary hero. I closed the channel and turned the situation over.

More and more convinced this island was Chion Mo's chosen method of making us understand the world and that people would keep hitting the same scenario from here on that nobody had yet founded a territory steadied me a little since I wanted to be the first. A threat of worry stayed with me though.

The fear that the island I had been steering toward by the map might turn out just as unfoundable and I sighed at finding myself completely helpless since all I could do was hold my course and deal with it if and when I hit one. When Windsor heard the sigh and asked what was wrong, Dark Moon glancing over two, I just shook my head and said it was nothing.

I stopped chewing on it, drank a glass of something, lay back, and let my eyes close, drifting off without meaning to, until Windsor woke me at noon. The gloomy weather fooling me into thinking it was night before I registered the hour. I ate lunch, fired up the diving technology, and started the day's hunt.

Out on the island, meanwhile, Yupyang had spent the whole morning exploring and turned up nothing beyond a few wild fruits, so the six of them sat down to eat. A wild boar roasted on the ground beside some fruit and bread. And the sight of the sizzling oily meat had them swallowing hard since they had choked down nothing but fish for so long they were sick of it.

They wolfed the boar down and resumed the search. Two hours later, one of his men burst out of the tall grass, stared ahead, and yelled for the lord because they had found something. Yupyang shoved through the grass and stopped short at the scene before him. A dilapidated little city stood there, part stone wall and part wooden wall, a dried riverbed circling it like a moat.

Small animals scattering through the empty channel at the shouting. The ground inside packed hard from old foot traffic, so the weeds grew short and sparse. He nearly came apart with excitement, hollering that he was rich, since a small city like that had to be packed with good things, then rained himself in and told his people to move out and watch the surroundings, and the six started toward it.

Yupang crossed the broken bridge over the dry channel and entered the city, greeted first by ruined residential buildings and weeds everywhere. After clearing a path and killing a few low-level monsters, he reached a second city wall, mostly intact and built entirely of blue stone, and passed through its gate.

The buildings inside stood in better shape, and though those lining the road were damaged, you could still faintly read the prosperity this place had once held. They ducked into several at random and turned up nothing of value. So he pushed on to the center where the buildings rose taller and abandoned their uniform style for distinct character a piece.

The castle at the heart pulling all their eyes. Yupang walked straight in and the castle proved enormous. Its courtyard planted with all manner of trees including some bearing fruit. Though he left the fruit alone since it was not going anywhere, and made a circuit of the grounds first. In the southeast corner, he found a cluster of buildings, most already ruined, but two still standing whole, and he hurried over.

One was made of blue stone with bows and arrows hung around the walls. And as he neared it, a prompt surface, telling him he had discovered a complete troop nest, asking whether he wished to destroy it, and noting a chance to obtain the nest afterward. He burst out laughing because until now he had owned exactly one white militia troop nest.

And here was another, no, two, since a second stood right beside it, and even at only a chance, he was hardly going to leave them. He ordered his men to tear the first building down. And once it was demolished, a green light flashed, and an archer troop nest at Green Grade floated up, which delighted him, so his grin nearly split his face.

He threw his people at the second building. And this time, the nest came a blue, a lancer troop nest that summons lancers, a powerful cavalry unit. He stood there stunned, rubbing his eyes, gabbling that he could not be dreaming as he snatched it up, gritting so foolishly that his subordinates half wondered if their lord had taken ill, until one asked whether they were going to keep exploring, and snapped him back.

He pocketed the nest and told them to miss nothing and leave only when every building had been picked over since two nests had already dropped before he had even seen the heart of the castle. And he split the six into two groups. Meanwhile, I had wrapped my own days hunting without a mishap.

Pulling close to 6,000 white stone along with a clutch of white grade building blueprints I gave no thought to and simply collected. Stretched on the lounge chair, I pulled up my own information and found the afternoon of fighting had carried me to level six. Each basic attribute climbing five points, which meant I no longer needed to throttle my leveling now that I sat at legendary quality.

I read my line over level six with two of 600 experience banked, strength 32 before bonuses, agility 32, defense 31, wisdom 30, and 300 mana. I was an ice element messenger hero carrying spirit eye, water arrow technique, shield, and freezing spell equipped with a green staff, a green ring, and green sea star armor. My talent now 100 transformations at legendary and my title still empty.

Beyond my own gains, Ha Rui had leveled two. A gold elemental spirit at level six, strength 55, agility 52, defense 56. Her skills were the hardened gold body, golden shield, sharp arrow technique, golden cage technique, and golden rain. Her loyalty and affinity both at a perfect 100. Surely the fruit of the element gems and purple crystal I have fed her.

What disappointed me was that Huawa had not leveled at all despite holding more gems. And the only reason I could land on was that her ongoing awakening was burning through energy faster than the gems could supply. That evening, I ate dinner up on the deck, barbecue, and cracked two bottles of red wine. And as I ate, I messaged Yupang to ask whether he had found anything.

He was at his own dinner, working through some edible meat his people had scavenged out of the Lord Castle's underground cold storage, cooked into a fine meal by the chef among his subordinates.

Though one seat at that table sat empty, the thought of it weighed on him because once they had pushed into the castle proper, they had met two puppets he had taken for ordinary statues, and a single careless moment had cost him a man. After putting the puppets down, he had recovered the castle's territory core and some gems from the warehouse.

Though he was unsure what any of it was good for, and when my message landed, he answered quickly that there was an abandoned lord territory on the island. That surprised me, genuinely unexpected, and I told him to lay it all out. So, he recounted the whole of it from the broken bridge to the warehouse. When he had finished, I found myself puzzled.

By his own account, the inner city wall still stood intact. So, how had the place fallen into such ruin? And even if some attacker had cracked it, why leave the territory core behind? And why had the warehouse gone entirely untouched, which meant whoever struck it had come not to occupy the place, but only to destroy? A thought surfaced then and crowded everything else out.

The question of whether disasters might come even after a territory was founded, whether it was only when Shanmo herself moved to test the lords that you would see a lord killed, but his goods left unplundered. The longer I turned it over, the more likely it felt, though, even if I had it exactly right, there was nothing I could do, and I could only meet whatever came as it came.

I set the tangle aside and kept talking with Yupe, asking how many element gems he had and whether he was selling, since those gems were near useless to most people right now, and I had two elemental spirits to feed.

After a hero dies, he can condense into a heroic spirit inside the hall, keeping every bit of the strength he held before death. Able to fight in the territo's main city, though his power can never be raised any higher. The construction cost ran to a thousand wood and stacks of ghost wood and underworld pattern stone and dark element gem and soul cleansing water.

I stared, then swore, rubbed my eyes, and used spirit eye to read it again. And it hadn't changed. It really was a gold quality blueprint. I laughed from somewhere deep in my chest because I had pulled a gold item without ever finding the resource fishing rod. The first true gold thing I had ever obtained. Every gold piece before this having been my own talents doing.

I badly wanted to know whether 100 transformations which guaranteed at least a one grade lift could push something already gold even higher. I couldn't wait for night, specified the category, and ran the talent, and nothing changed at all, which had me wondering whether gold was simply the ceiling of this whole world until a prompt surfaced telling me my talent grade was insufficient to use 100 transformations on it.

That stopped me, because Chion Mo had actually volunteered a hint, and after a moment's frown, I understood it meant gold was not the ceiling, that grades above it existed, and only my present talent fell short of forcing a gold item to budge. I smiled at that because better qualities waiting above me was good news. And if my talent grade was the bottleneck, then I could simply find ways to raise it.

Once it climbed high enough to transform gold, I would transform gold. And I genuinely looked forward to the day. My talent was clearly powerful and nowhere near fully unfolded. And I put the Hall of Valor away in high spirits, humming a little tune to myself.

When hunting time came around, I dove, the tower ship sinking under, and for the first 4 hours, I met no real trouble, dealing with another sea beast and pushing a bit farther on until Windsor suddenly called out and pointed ahead. My eyes went wide the instant I looked. There in front rose a mountain with its peak sheared flat.

And on that smooth crown sat a black city ringed with arrow towers. The whole thing sealed under a white light shield curving over the walls like an upturned bull. I cursed without any real heat because I had finally found a lord's territory and it was sitting underwater.

The irony stung since I couldn't tell whether I would have found a territory at all without my diving technology or whether activating that technology was the very reason I had drawn an underwater one. It didn't matter either way because a territory was a territory. So I ordered the advance and sent little blue ahead to test the shield and she tapped it cautiously with her tail and was blocked.

Then slammed it hard several times and the shield rippled and held. My face went grim and I ordered everyone to strike together. And even under all of us at once, the shield refused to break. My mood was foul and the fear that I couldn't even get inside started to set in until my eyes lit up on a memory.

The Muroke Talisman purple proof of identity for the Muroke was something I had chosen as my first place survival reward the week before purely because the thing struck me as strange. As a last resort, I brought the ship in close and held the talisman to the light shield. The instant they touched, a small opening bloomed and spread outward until a great gateway stood open in the shield.

I was overjoyed it had worked, grateful I had chosen the talisman back then, and grateful I had never run it through hundred transformations because if I had, I would be weeping now. We steered the tower ship through the barrier and came to rest beneath the city wall, which stood completely intact. The main gate under its arch entirely undamaged.

That puzzled me. With no breach in the outer defenses, how had this territory ever fallen empty? And what had its lord faced that he could be wiped out behind walls that still stood whole? Carrying that question, I passed through the main gate into streets laid out neat and orderly with rows of houses. And the whole place was a ghost town.

Not a single sign of life anywhere. I drove the ship onward with everyone aboard on high alert since no one could say what danger waited. I didn't stop because Yupeong's experience taught me a territo's key buildings and best loot usually sat at the center. Soon we reached the inner city wall where 10 sea creatures flanked the gate arch.

All of them shark-headed, each gripping a spear in its right hand and a shield in its left. I read them with spirit eye and learned they were hungry shark puppets. Blue things alchemists and mechanics build together and pack with great strength. I eyed them wearily, unsure whether they still worked after all this time.

But I refused to drop my guard because puppets had cost you Pyong a subordinate once, a lesson I had no intention of repeating. Windsor threw a binding spell and Huawa and Harui struck at the same moment.

The four of us pinning four puppets while the rest came alive and advanced behind shield and spear toward the ship. Even the one I had frozen was already shaking free. My own water arrows beside Windsor's light arrows and Hui's sharp arrow technique and Hawwa's fireball and the commoner's arrows. Under that storm, six puppets were torn into scattered parts on the ground before they reached us. Then I activated the diving technology and a thin film of light wrapped Windsor and Huawa and Harui and Sokpan and me, the five of us stepping off the ship while Dark Moon and the others stayed aboard. Setting my boots on the stone slab gave me a strange unreality deepened by the sight of Little Blue drifting beside me. The shark head startlingly lifelike with scarlet eyes and sharpointed teeth showing through its gaping mouth. And Hui went and stopped the other two. I swam up a little to see what sat at top the shark's head and found a button with Huawa standing squarely on it.

So, I praised her for being clever and patted her head. She beamed and pointed at Harui to make clear they had found it together. So I praised Harui, too. Then I had the four puppets loaded onto the ship, and we rode Little Blue with the tower ship trailing behind into the inner city, where the buildings turned plainly more lavish.

Everyone a crystal house in some different color. I sighed and kept searching, turning up a profession temple, a muroke altar, a sawtooth sea shark troop layer, all purple and all dead. Then I hit a crimson striped python troop layer, blue, a complete layer, offering a chance of the troop layer itself if destroyed. And my eyes lit up at finally finding something I could take. I destroyed it.

My luck good this time, and the troop layer came to me. I circled the area again and found several more incomplete layers, guessing this was where the old lord stockpiled them, then split everyone up to search. We turned up four more intact layers, though destroying them yielded only two. A blue-eyed water leopard troop layer, green, and a bewildering tree troop layer, blue, which honestly disappointed me a little.

Leaving the layer district behind, I came to a stretch of varied buildings and past several damaged ones, hit something that set my heart fluttering. It was a troop enhancement pool, purple, a complete building whose blueprint dropped on destruction with no mention of the word chance anywhere in the description. I checked it several times to be sure, then destroyed it and claimed the blueprint outright.

A troop enhancement pool that strengthens troops to a degree scaling with their quality. Building it demanded stacks of stone and energy stone and manavine and water and light element gem. Two of those materials being ones I had never even heard of, though that hardly mattered since I could gather them in time. I put it away, meaning to push on, and discovered my diving time for the day had run out.

It was a disappointment, but there was nothing for it. So, I chose to surface and pick the exploration up again the next day, unworked that the place would vanish since Yupang had spent a whole night on the island he was exploring without losing it. The tower ship broke the surface and I shivered hard because the cold had been down brutally, far worse than it had felt in the sea.

And I knew I had to solve the heating fast. I went back to the core cabin, sat and started cranking out cotton quilts and selling them at the market rate. And within an hour, I had turned every raw material into quilts and moved the lot. When I checked my assets afterward, my eyes went wide. 13 gold stone, 32 purple stone, and 82 white stone, 133,282 white stone, all told.

Even counting the white stone I had hunted that afternoon. I had cleared at least 120,000 and I burst out laughing that I was wretched. Then got straight to upgrading without another word. First was the tower ship itself, which wanted 8,000 wood and 16,000 iron nails and copper blocks and a ship core. So I bought everything for 40,000 white stone and triggered the upgrade.

The level three tower ship now ran 60 meters x 15 with a fourth floor added carrying its own kitchen and dining room and warehouse and a brand new conference room alongside more living quarters, a water store, and the rest. Its technology list stretched out across drainage and thermal energy and automation in the multi-ore linkage propulsion and three flavors of diving plus the underwater breathing.

The conference room was useless to me for now since I had fewer than 50 people under me and hardly needed a dedicated hall to hold meetings and even the extra living quarter sat half empty. After the ship, I clicked ship electrical technology, which wanted 4,000 white stone to unlock, and I startled because the ship thermal energy had only cost 500 back in the day.

Money was no longer the problem. So, I unlocked it, built it for another thousand, then poured 2,000 more into raising it to level two. As the electrical system came online, fine silver lines threaded along the walls of my room with blue circles flickering at intervals down their length, and the whole place took on an inexplicable high-tech feel.

When I pulled out a heated blanket and flipped its switch, it warmed in my hands, the electricity carrying across the air without any wire at all. I called Mory in and told her to arrange heating for everyone. And while she was at it to have someone bring my luxury bathing set since the level three core cabin had its own bathing room.

Now, with that settled, I went back to upgrading. First, the power technology 16,000 whitest stone to push it to level six, then 6,000 to lift the ship thermal energy to level four, then 3,000 to carry the automation to level three. The weekend diving wanted 24,000 to crawl from level four to 5, while the complete version only wanted 10,000 to go from 1 to two. Then, underwater breathing, 4,000 to reach level two and 8,000 to reach level three, 12,000 total.

And at level three, it would let seven people breathe at once for three hours. When everything was done, I had 19,282 white stone left, having burned a full 114,000, most of it on technology. It didn't hurt at all because money only has value once it is spent, and otherwise, it is just a number that means nothing.

After that, I opened my friend list and found Wang Mock asking what the special offer was in his personal store this week because I meant to grab another teleportation array. Wang Mock, who was eating dinner, opened his store the moment he saw my message and told me it was a teleportation array, and I felt the knot in my chest loosen since I had worried other players might see something different from me.

I told him to list one wood for sale at 5,000 white stone, and then buy that array on my behalf. And he followed my instructions without hesitation, bought the array, then relisted it on the trading channel at one white stone, and specified the trade to me.

I didn't snap it up and instead told him to change the price to 100 white stone because he was deliberately refusing payment and I couldn't allow that knowing that over time letting it slide would breed resentment in him whether he meant it to or not.

He resisted at first but my firmness went out and he accepted and that small insistence only hardened his determination to follow me because he figured a future was only possible at the sight of a man with that kind of perspective. With the array in hand, I opened Yupe's chat next.

I went straight to the point and asked him to do me a favor and buy the special from his personal mall and he answered it once with no problem. Big shot. Only that he didn't have that kind of money himself. So, I had him list some wood on the trading channel and bought it for 5100 whitest stone.

Yukong stared at the 5100 now sitting in his hands, feeling almost dizzy, wondering whether the big shot wasn't afraid he might simply pocket the money and run since 5,000 whit stone was a fortune to most people. He marveled at how much money I must be holding to trust him so easily.

Reasoning that since I had asked him to buy an array, I had clearly already bought my own, which meant this was an extra, which meant I had at least 10,000 white stone and spare funds. He found the scale shocking, wondering when he would ever see numbers like that.

Had he known I had just spent over a h 100,000, the figure would have terrified him outright, being completely beyond anything his imagination could hold. Soon, his array arrived, and I had chosen him precisely because I had traded with him before and judged him reliable. Once it was done, I checked and still had over 9,000 white stone.

So, I found Lie Chong and squeezed a third array out of him, which left me under 4,000 and unable to buy more, and I had no need to anyway since four arrays was plenty. With those errands closed out, I ran the day's 100 transformations this time on the items I had bought from Wang Mock, specifying all of them as equipment, and pulled three pieces.

There was a flowing fire staff, blue, lifting the user strength by a fifth. An auspicious cloud armor, blue, lifting defense by the same, and a flowing wind bow, blue, lifting agility, though nobody under me could use a bow yet for lack of any archer profession.

I gave the flowing fire staff to Huawa and the auspicious cloud armor to Windsor, and by then my dinner ready, so I ate while idly opening the chat channel. Someone bragged about clearing an island for two green troop nests. Another topped him with a blue one, and a third claimed outright that he had gotten a hero.

The channel demanded proof and he posted a picture and when I clicked it, the thing really was a hero. An orc warrior of fine quality already at level seven. The rest of the channel erupted in envy, wailing that his good fortune was harder to bear than their own losses. And somebody begged to know how it had been done.

What actually caught my attention wasn't the hero, but the method, because here was proof of another path to gaining one. The first path was the summon cards. The second was the way I had taken So Pan by crushing a hostile force and subduing the enemy hero. And this was a third, stumbling on a wandering masterless hero and finding a way to win him over.

There was even a fourth, my own hundred spirits pavilion, whose description promised a chance to summon a hero outright. There were plainly many roads to a hero, and most people would likely have one before long, though most of those would be lowquality with limited growth. Never fit to anchor a territory's high-end core strength.

That was only my own read on it, of course, since not everyone had the chance to draw a legendary hero, and among the people I knew, only Bake Seal held even one, never mind me with several. A thing common sense couldn't really measure.

The channel pressed him to explain how he had subdued the hero, whether he had beaten him into yielding, and the man answered that he had simply been lucky, that some time ago, a bottle of immortal drunk wine had dropped from an attacker he killed.

The story took the usual detour, half the channel hooting at the man to get to the point, while the other half hooted at the first half to stop interrupting. Only after some wrangling did it come back around. The man explained that he had cursed the useless wine for ages until today.

When he drank it with his meal while exploring the island and the hero came looking for him on his own except at a drink and asked to follow him.

The channel howled that without the picture they would have called it a tall tale that it read like a scene straight out of a novel and the man admitted it felt like a dream to him too except now he was nearly out of wine. That set off a stampede of people offering to sell him their own at a discount.

Peerless True Brew, fresh stock, whatever they had and somebody asking whether the wine was any good got told he could drink it and it wouldn't kill him. I smiled at all of it, feeling that the internet had polluted me, too, since I used to be a pure little lamb before I ever touched it. After dinner, I went to bed, where my pure little lamb went to keep company with the little cat.

The night unfolded in a way better left to the imagination, where genuine affection holds a thousand virtues, but none of them quite matched the soft gasp of a half-sung sigh. The fifth day arrived, and I woke groggy, moving Dark Moon's cattail away from somewhere it had no business being. Baffled by when exactly she had picked up that peculiar habit.

My shifting woke her too, and she looked at me, snorted through her nose a few times, her ears twitching, and rubbed her little face against my chest, every trace of her usual aloofness gone. I told her gently it was time to get up, that we still had yesterday's territory left to explore.

The night before, after surfacing, I had left the ship exactly where it sat, hedging against not being able to find the territory again come morning, and Dark Moon's mind cleared slowly while her cheeks colored.

I smiled at that because Dark Moon was always this way, utterly given over in the moment and bashful the instant it ended, which I found endearing in its own way, and I had no wish to change her since everyone carries their own temperament.

After breakfast, I went up to the deck and Huawa and Harui flew over and the whole group looked at them in surprise because the two little ones had grown again, their old height of 40 cm now stretched to 60, like a pair of exquisite dolls. I scooped them up and checked their stats. Huawa, formerly Ha, a fire elemental spirit in her awakening state and legendary, had hit level seven.

Her strength and agility and defense all up near the high 40s. Her mana at 450, her profession listed as hero and fire element controller under fire marshall god Awakening. Her skills read hardened gold body and golden shield and sharp arrow technique and golden cage technique and golden rain. Her loyalty and affinity at 100 as well.

I had braced myself for Hawwa reaching level seven, but Ha Rui doing the same caught me off guard since she had leveled up not long ago and I had assumed she would need more time. Huawa was still an awakening with no visible change and no progress bar to track which dragged up the memory of chasing girls back in the day since there was no progress bar then either.

And after a while of getting nowhere, I would lose interest and quit. Huawa though was one thing I would never quit having decided to raise her myself. I set the two little ones down and they hovered there beating their small wings while I patted their heads and told them they had done well and she keep eating and keep working to level up.

I had plenty of purple crystal anyway and they both nodded eagerly. Once there, I activated the underwater breathing technology, and with Dark Moon added to yesterday's group, the six of us set out while Little Blue stood guard near the ship. Soon, I made a discovery. A cluster of mermaid growth pools, some blue and some green. Each a complete building offering a chance at its blueprint on destruction. All of them circular pools sheathed in a light shield with green or blue liquid inside.
