I frowned at being human, wondering whether I could even use the thing, though it hardly mattered since worst case I could simply turn it on myself with hundred transformations and waste nothing. I put the three blueprints away and pushed on and around a corner met another complete building.

Its base was a truncated pyramid of cyan stone shot through with gold and blue patterns, steps climbing all four sides to a rectangular blue crystal stale up top. An incense table set before it holding only a few empty vessels. It read as a sacrificial altar, purple, complete with a chance at its blueprint on destruction, and my eyes lit, and the group set on it at once.

Luck refused me this time, and no blueprint dropped, which gutted me, though I studied myself fast since I was hardly Chan Mo's illegitimate son and couldn't expect everything to fall in my way. I kept hunting complete buildings, and over the next 2 hours, I combed everywhere but the Lord's castle, and found nothing but incomplete ones.

My mood souring until all I had left was the hope that the castle held something worth the trip. The six of us entered while Little Blue and the ship stayed outside since the castle's own shield only opened a person-sized gap under the Muroke talisman, and they were far too large to fit. I pushed the gate, and we stepped in carefully.

The first sight, a pebbled path lined with fruit trees I had never seen before, marked as fish heart fruit, an ordinary tree the Muroke apparently treasured as a delicacy. I picked one and tasted it, found it genuinely unpleasant, and figured Muroke and human pallet simply didn't agree. Then I picked a second one and checked it in the mall, where it fetched five white stone, surprisingly steep.

So, I filed that away for our departure and turned to look for things worth more. We followed the path straight into the castle's core and started working room by room, most of them holding nothing of value until we hit a warehouse. I opened it to a wide space heaped with bags on the floor. The contents of the first one I checked long since rotted to nothing.

I told everyone to look for anything still worth taking, and Dark Moon and Windsor and soap pan spread out to search while Huawa and Harui flew off toward the nearby wall, sniffed it with their tiny noses, then turned back to me and pointed. When Huawa and Ha Rui both stopped at the wall and started sniffing, I figured the two of them had caught wind of something I had walked right past.

The two little spirits pressed their noses to the stone, then turned and pointed at the wall together, looking back at me as if to ask what I planned to do about it. I asked whether there was something hidden behind the panel, and they nodded in perfect unison, which told me they were sure.

So, I started feeling along the surface for a mechanism, because I would rather find the catch than smash the whole thing open and risk ruining whatever was tucked inside. Breaking it by force was the kind of last resort I keep in my back pocket, never the first move since a careless swing in a place like this tends to cost more than it saves.

It took a little patience, but I found a button set into the corner, painted the exact gray of the surrounding stone so anyone in a hurry would miss it. I pressed it, and a section of the wall slid down until it sat flush with the floor, and what waited inside was a jade shelf lined with several boxes.

The first held a single token, round and silver, stamped with the pattern of a walled city. And the moment I lifted it, my appraisal named it a silver pass. For the auction, the Yong Jong Trading Company ran out of Chian Mo City. Holding it meant I could walk into that auction as a recognized buyer. The kind of credential you do not stumble across by hunting sea beasts.

I had no idea how rare it actually was, but if some dead lord had sealed it behind a hidden wall, I was inclined to believe it was worth more than nothing. The second box was jade and a good deal larger. And when I cracked the lid, it turned out to be packed with element gems, well over 200 of them in an assortment of types.

I scooped the whole lot into my storage without bothering to count properly because gems are gems and you never sniff at free ones. The third box was metal made from some material I could not name, and the scroll resting inside it was black as ink.

My appraisal read it back as the ice seal of a thousand miles, a goldrade magic scroll that cast a sweeping area attacked the instant you tear it open. I genuinely could not believe what I was looking at because a single-use scroll at that grade is practically a spare life folded into a sheet of paper and finding one in a forgotten warehouse felt almost obscene.

That gold scroll raised the question that had nagged me since we boarded this dead territory. If the owner had been staring down the loss of his home, why sit on a weapon like the ice seal of a thousand miles instead of burning it to save himself?

Either the place had fallen to something a scroll could not stop, or it had not fall into combat at all, which the intact city walls supported even while the wrecked interior argued the opposite. I turned the contradiction over for a few seconds, decided it was costing me more than it was worth, and let it drop.

The previous owner had chosen not to use the thing, and I had no intention of refusing it on his behalf. The next two boxes each gave up another scroll, Thousand Stone Thorns, and Magic Vine Entanglement, both blue grade, one for raw damage and one for locking an enemy down hard.

Ever since the two domain competition wrapped up, I had been quietly hunting for any life-saving item, and the market had given me nothing. Partly because Hundred Transformations refuses to make that sort of thing from nothing.

Now, three had landed in my lap inside one afternoon, and my sense of safety climbed noticeably, I opened another box, expecting a fourth scroll, half wondering whether this lord had collected them like a hobby, and instead, my appraisal pulled up a general level participation scroll, blue grade, usable only by a lord who had already reached general level strength. I needed a moment to parse what that scroll offered.

The spirit demon war reference was the great opening battle we had all been thrown into on arrival. And the second engagement, the one limited to the top 10,000, had been its own special affair. Then I noticed the word additional, and it clicked.

This scroll let a qualified lord drop back into the general level battlefield one extra time after the main war, which made it genuinely valuable because the war hands out ship cores in a flood of spoils, and ship cores are the bottleneck on every fleet expansion there is. A lord who hides on his island and builds behind closed walls never grows and growth means ships and ships mean course.

So I tucked the scroll away with real care. I kept opening boxes half enjoying the blind draw rhythm of it until one lid came up on nothing. The next was empty too and then several more until I half wondered whether some petty dead lord had stacked hollow boxes purely to mock whoever found the place.

I kept opening them anyway out of pure stubbornness and the persistence paid off because near the bottom I found a shape I knew on site. A skill stone named by my appraisal as the mystic ice puppet. Learning it let me summon ice puppets whose strength ran a full third above my own. Each lasting 5 minutes, the only cap being how much mana I poured into the summoning.

I learned it on the spot, grinning at how unfair it read on paper, but wanted my mana reserve intact with unknown rooms still ahead, so I held off on testing it. The rest of the boxes were hollow, and I called the hall done. Windsor came over and reported flatly that everything on her side had been Muroke Food Stores and other Sea Provisions.

All of it long since rotted, which left her a little deflated at the wasted search. I told her not to worry since my own corner had paid out handsomely, and the six of us filed out to work through the remaining rooms.

We cleared a string of empty chambers before we reached a door that did not match the rest, calling it a room under sold it, because it read more like a small palace built mostly from black crystal with a golden gate worked over in Muroke patterns.

Six puppets stood guard in front of it, two female and four male, and it was the first proper look I had gotten at what Merur folk were shaped like, even rendered in lifeless craft.

The males were broad and heavily muscled through the chest, square jawed and frankly handsome, gripping steel trident and shields, while the females were slender and graceful, faces lovely enough to turn heads, their upper bodies draped in seaweed for modesty, and their hands wrapped around slim staffs. My appraisal tagged the men as Muroke warrior puppets and the women as Muroke mage puppets.

Both blue grade described as joint work by alchemists and engineers. I checked the palace itself and was almost offended when it read as an ordinary building because nobody decorates an ordinary building this lavishly and posts six guards outside it. If anything, that tag made me more eager since plain packaging over armed centuries usually means the contents are what matters.

I called the order to move and Silk Pan stepped to the front before the word left my mouth. 9 m stretching to a full three. The lapis lassly shield ballooning alongside him as gigantification and great shield art took hold. Behind that wall of him, I felt the kind of security that lets you fight without flinching, which is exactly what a front line is for.

I started chanting the mystic ice puppet, genuinely curious to see the thing work, and the ice elements gathered and condensed until a figure took shape, 2 m tall, carved entirely from blue ice crystal, hauling a two-handed greatsword, summoning that one puppet ate a clean third of my mana, which I logged and accepted. Then I called up a second before the fighting opened.

By then, Windsor and the others had already landed their first blows on the warrior puppets, and our usual control skills barely stuck this time. The puppets shrugging free almost the instant they were caught. These Muro constructs were bluegrade like the hungry shark puppets we had handled before, but visibly a notch stronger, which quietly killed any plan I had of capturing them intact.

Three of the warriors charged us while a fourth held its ground to guard the two mages. And watching puppets run actual tactics gave me a fresh and slightly unsettling respect for whatever alchemy built them. While the Muroke mages were still mid- incantation, Windsor threw bewilder at each of them. Her casting speed leaving the puppets in the dust.

By the time the two mages got their first skill off, she'd already finished both her castings. And what they released were a pair of soul-shaped muroke apparitions that drifted onto the two charging warriors. Where those soul forms touched, the warrior puppets swelled a size larger and grew noticeably more menacing, which told me plainly that Bewilder did nothing at all against constructs.

My two ice puppets and Siopan each squared off against a warrior. The six of them slamming together at once and my side took the worst of the opening clash and got driven back a step.

Dark Moon moved in then, the windbreaking blade in her hand lengthening and going black as Darkness blade ran along the edge and she slid over towards Sock Pan to gang up on the one warrior that had not been buffed. Her first strike knocked that puppet back outright and she stayed glued to it. Her second slash opening a deep gash down its arm.

She kept the pressure on without letting up, faster than the puppet could answer. And once it was clear she could hold the thing alone, Seakpan peeled off to reinforce my struggling ice puppets. I tried a water arrow technique into the fight, watched it accomplish almost nothing against that hide and stopped wasting effort, spending the leftover mana on a third mystic ice puppet instead.

Around the same time, Hui and Huawa threw golden cage technique and firebinding onto the warrior shielding the mages, pinning it just long enough to matter. Windsor seized the window and put a light arrow straight into one of the mage puppets, catching it before it could swap skills, and the bolt took it in the head and dropped it cold.

The surviving mage got a skill away regardless. Blue water bubbles blooming into the air one after another and floating toward Dark Moon and my ice puppets. Huawa answered with fireball and Ha Rui let loose Golden Rain, the two of them meeting the bubbles headon and bursting most of them, though two slipped the net and settled onto Seakpan and me.

The instant the bubbles touched down, they spread into a thin clinging film, and I came through it fine, well outside the melee with a shield already up. But Sock Pan was in real trouble. His whole body went sluggish, and a warrior puppet steel trident drove straight into him. The strike only stopped because the Lapis lassly shield ate the force since a clean hit there would have ended him.

In the same breath, one of my ice puppets exploited the opening and beheaded the warrior it was facing, then wheeled off to join the others. Silkpan pulled out of the line and fell back to me, his movement still locked up from the film, so I kept him behind cover while the rest pressed on.

Windsor sent another light arrow into the standing mage puppet, only for it to splash against a layer of light patterned ward the thing had raised. By then, Dark Moon had already sheared both arms off her warrior and was driving her blade down through its skull. And the moment it dropped, she ripped the windbreaking blade free and lunged at the puppet that had just torn loose from Huawa and Harui's binds.

Huawa's fireball and Harui's sharp arrow technique came down on the last mage together. And this time, no ward saved it. The puppet losing its head. With everyone's hands suddenly free. The remaining warriors lost any chance they had, and the rest of the fight went exactly the way a numbers advantage should with the team boxing them in while Dark Moon and my ice puppets took them apart.

By the time it ended, Sock Pan and I had both shaken off the film. To be safe, I sent the three ice puppets in first to push the gate open, and nothing jumped out since the interior was nearly bare. The only feature was a circular pit in the center of the hole wrapped in a thin barrier of light.

I walked over and found it half full of red liquid gone almost entirely dry and floating in the dregs was an oval membrane about 60 cm long, a muroke shape faintly visible inside it.

My appraisal stopped me where I stood because the membrane read as a muroke hero of legendary grade, a cub dying, starved for nutrition, and the pit itself came back as a mermaid growth pool, a complete purple grade building with a chance of dropping its blueprint if I destroyed it.

I stared at that reading and asked myself whether I had misread it because the thing had not even been born and the system already called it a hero of legendary quality. Heroes are not usually born. So I worked the logic instead of gaping. The guards out front were merely blueg grade and no lord protecting a legendary hero postsecond raid centuries if he has anything better.

So the cub almost certainly was not a born hero. A born legendary hero would have parents formidable enough that a lord at this level would never have laid hands on the offspring. And this one had not even spent a general level participation scroll yet.

The sensible read was that the cub was the lord's own child, lucky enough to roll legendary at birth, and that the lord had spent a hero class change card to turn it heroic. Whatever the truth of it, the cub was now my prize, and its immediate problem was that the dried out pool had stopped feeding it.

The territory had been abandoned long enough that the nutrient solution had nearly run dry, leaving the cub clinging to the last of it. I pulled out a handful of water element gems and a few purple crystals to test a theory, dropping the water gems in first, and the solution rippled and rose a touch. Encouraged, I fed in every water gem I held, and the level climbed.

Then a single purple crystal drew a far more violent reaction. The solution surging much higher than the gems had managed. So I kept feeding purple crystal until the liquid rose enough to submerge the cub. And when I checked again, the reading, it shifted from dying to growing, which meant I had saved it.

After that, I took several of the jade boxes from the warehouse and spent more purple crystal to brew fresh nutrient solution, storing it so the cub would not go hungry on the trip back. Then lifted the little muroke into one of them. With the cub secure, I ordered the mermaid growth pool destroyed. And this time, my luck ran dry since it gave up no blueprint when it shattered.

It stung only a little since I already had several mermaid growth pool blueprints in storage. We circled the rest of the palace and turned up nothing more. So, I led the group to the heart of the place, a final chamber raised entirely from purple crystal.

We pushed through into an empty hall, climbed the stairs, and stepped into a luxurious room dominated by a wide bed dressed in a brocade quilt of some material I could not name, embroidered with muroke patterns. A bookshelf stood against one wall, but none of that held my attention because the thing in the corner did. A blue cylinder with an ocahedron suspended above it.

A shape I knew at a glance. The territory core. The instant I reached it, the system offered the choice to destroy the unowned core, which would hand it to me and erase the territory. I said, "Destroy," and the cylinder dissolved into scattered starlight while the core floated free into my hand.

And then the whole territory began to shake, the architecture coming apart, until it all winked out and left the six of us standing on a bare mountaintop. Little Blue and the ship were already racing toward us. And once the ship pulled alongside, we boarded. The core still cuped in my palm.

There was time left on the dive, so Windsor and the others and I hunted together, and I made a point of leveling actively rather than coasting. When the timer ran out, we surfaced, and the cold hit so hard the whole crew shivered the moment we broke through. I sent everyone inside to rest and called off work for the day since nobody lasts long in weather like that.

And the commoners scattered toward their quarters, looking thoroughly relieved. I lingered though since I had not used the resource fishing rod yet and handed it to Windsor when she came to help since Huawa doubles nicely as a hand warmer anyway. I took Hui and Dark Moon back to the core cabin while Mory went to start lunch. Dark Moon settled onto the sofa.

I stretched out with my head in her lap and Harui tucked against me and I pulled out the territory core I had just won to study it properly. It was the first core I had ever taken off a true territory rather than off a novice lord. And I was curious whether the two even worked the same.

My appraisal named it a Muroke territory core usable to found an underwater territory and raise Muroke exclusive buildings. And that single line set my mind racing. If a Muro core builds underwater, then cores of different races must build different territories entirely, which meant my own human race corps would only ever raise a land territory full of human buildings, while this one opened the door to a base beneath the sea someday.

I was not about to use it now since it was not my foundational core, and I had no spare energy for an undersea outpost on top of everything else. I am a human and a human needs his ground first. So a sea territory only made sense as a later supplement. I put it away. Then I pulled out the incomplete map and traced the voyage.

And at my current speed, the destination sat another 5 or 6 days off, which left me both eager and on edge. The eagerness was obvious since soon I could finally plant my own territory. And the unease was simple, too, since there was always the chance of arriving at an island where no territory could be raised at all.

I sat there frowning over a thing I could not control until Dark Moon reached up and smoothed the creases out of my forehead. The door opened on Windsor coming back in and I shook the worry loose. Caught the concern on Dark Moon's face and gave her a smile, telling her it was nothing, just some things on my mind.

Fretting over what I could not steer was a waste. So I decided to take it as it came and keep building my strength the honest way. Meanwhile, Windsor crossed to the other side of the sofa, sat, lifted my legs across her lap, and handed me the item she had pulled with the rod while she went to work on my calf.

I asked where Huawa had gone, and Windsor told me the little spirit had wandered off to drill her skills, which made me smile because Huawa really was the diligent sort. I said as much aloud, and Hui sprawled across my stomach, flipped upright, and shot me a fierce look while jabbing a thumb at her own chest and clenching her little fists.

I told her yes, she was very diligent, too, and poked her forehead, which she read as me brushing her off because she flew up in a huff and stomped off to grind her hardened gold body skill. Windsor rolled her eyes and asked why I had to needle her, and I said a little extra training never hurt anyone.

Then I turned to the items Windsor had fished up. The first was a fish tank blueprint, white grade, for raising a few showy fish to lift the mood, requiring 25 units of glass. White or not, it was exactly what I needed since I could not leave the Muroke Cub crammed in a jade box indefinitely.

I bought the glass and built it, and a tank appeared on the living room floor, 3 m long, two wide, and 1 and 1/2 tall. Windsor and I shifted it into a corner, then moved the cub in all its nutrient solution and where it had room to grow.

The second item was spirit wheat seeds, green grade, a high nutrition grain that strengthens the body of anyone who eats it regularly and makes talents more likely to emerge among the residents. The same effect family as the spirit rice seeds I already kept. The last item was a Moonwell blueprint.

Blue Grade, a building that produces moon water with healing properties and a special potency toward the spirit race, costing a 100 moonstone along with stacks of wood, light, and water element gems. That one I liked since moon water is useful to me directly and sells well to the spirit race. Though it occurred to me I could run 100 transformations on it later and aim for a purple upgrade.

I decided to sleep on which way to go because by then I was genuinely tired and I shut my eyes. Dark Moon worked her fingers gently over my scalp while Windsor kept at my calf, and somewhere in there, I drifted off without meaning to. By the time I woke, Mory had lunch on the table, and the few of us ate and talked and laughed our way through it.

Windsor coaxed Harui out to join us, and the little spirit was far too proud to glance my way, right up until I produced a purple crystal as a peace offering, at which point she laughed and folded into my arms. She had reached 60 cm now, big enough that holding her meant actually holding her, so I kept an arm around her while she ate.

After the meal, we settled in to lays around since cold weather is made for staying in bed and Harui amused herself bouncing on the quilt while I ignored her and opened the chat channel. I posted that I had run across an underwater territory while hunting, sealed off behind a light shield I could not get past, which set the channel off at once.

Somebody demanded to know whether there really was such a thing as an underwater territory. Certain I was making it up. A second voice accused me of showing off that I had diving technology. A third decided that was the real flex and the channel piled on. I admitted half honest that I could only die for an hour and it was nothing to brag about.

Then attached a photo of the underwater territory to prove the find. People lost their minds confirming it, marveling that out of everyone only I had stumbled onto one while somebody sourly pointed out I had not even gotten inside and another mourned that being able to look but not touch was its own torment. I grinned and posted two more photos, the ones from when I cleared the Muroke territory.

One from outside the light shield and one of the lord's castle within just to stir the pot. The channel detonated, half of it shouting that they had caught a living Big Shot in the wild. The rest begging for friend slots and gawking that the Big Shot had actually made it inside. It dissolved into the usual rowdy nonsense.

Players angling to befriend the prettiest avatar. Others getting told off for being gross. The whole thread descending into cheerful chaos. I read it and burst out laughing because a crowd of lively troublemakers turned out to be its own entertainment. And I made a note to drop in more often and pick up some of their banter. Windsor asked what I was laughing about and Dark Moon looked over too puzzled.

So, I told them, deliberately vague, that I just wanted to join in the fun. Windsor swatted me, having spent enough time around me to read exactly what I was angling at, and Dark Moon went red and pulled the quilt over her head.

Their shyness, after everything, honestly earned my admiration, and I tugged Dark Moon back out from under the quilt, while Windsor's whole face flushed scarlet, and Harui, midbite on a purple crystal, felt the quilt shift under her and looked down in total confusion. Afterward, my whole body wound tight and then loosed all at once, and I let out a long breath.

Dark Moon poked her head back out, glancing at me a little bashfully, and Windsor reminded her about her mouth, so she wiped herself clean in a hurry.

Watching the two of them, I felt thoroughly ready to go again, and Windsor read the look on my face, pinched me hard enough to stab me back to my senses, and I gave them an awkward grin and blamed the whole thing on the pair of them being too charming for their own good. Then I remembered I had set up the luxury bathing equipment the day before and never used it.

So, I suggested a bath, and neither of them refused, even though they both knew exactly what I had in mind. The bathroom held a wide tub already filled from the water storage tank. So I heated the water and we settled in and Ha Rui playing nearby kept casting confused looks toward the bathroom door, plainly wondering what all the noise was about.

That evening after dinner and lights out, I lay alone to run my hundred transformations for the day. I pulled the first item, a hungry shark puppet blue grade, an alchemist and mechanic construct with real power behind it. I thought a moment about what to aim for, specified the blueprint type, and triggered the transformation.

And it came back as an alchemy workshop blueprint, purple grade, a facility where alchemists and mechanics research and build puppets and other machinery with a hefty bill and stone, refined steel, and fire and water element gems. I nodded, pleased, because it was a blueprint I did not own yet, and a genuinely important one for the territo's long-term growth.

I put it away and moved to the second item. The second was the moon well blueprint I had fished up that day. And since moon water amounted to a healing potion in liquid form, useful but ordinary, I wanted the purple version. I specified the blueprint type again and ran the transformation and out came a well of life blueprint.

Purple grade, a well that produces well of life water, a far stronger heal whose long-term consumption actually raises strength. The kind of thing the spirit race goes feverish over. I let out a small shout, surprised both by the strength boosting line and by the fact that the result was a second copy of a blueprint I already held.

And when I checked, it confirmed an identical well of life blueprint too now in hand. I could not help laughing because the run had upgraded the quality and doubled the quantity at once, which had never happened before. And the only thing I had to say about it was the more the better. I struck while the luck held and pulled the third item, a Muroke talisman, purple grade, a Muroke identification token.

Figuring it would not do me much good as is, I specified a special item and transformed it. And out came a Muroke heart crystal, special grade, a noble symbol of the Muroke that wins their favor, and even lets the holder command some of them.

I turned the blue crystal over, wondering how it was used, and the thought had barely formed when it lifted on its own and floated to the point between my brows, leaving a cool sensation, and then nothing. A mirror showed a blue romboid mark there, flat as a tattoo with no rise to it. And I had to admit, it lent me a slightly otherworldly air.

I was a touch let down that nothing more obvious happened, but it was clearly an upgraded Muroke talisman, and I would learn its real worth the next time I met the Muroke, which mattered because if it worked, I would have effectively befriended an entire race. After that, I cleaned out a few decent, if impractical, items from Wangmok's listings and called it a night.

The next morning, after breakfast, Windsor and the others and I carried lounge chairs up to the deck, Windsor and Dark Moon, each cradling an elemental spirit while I read. The book was called Spirit Adventures, the tale of a spirit race traveler crossing the sea and running into one island and one race after another.

And I read it with real interest because even if I could not vouch for how much was true, it still handed me useful glimpses of the world. It cataloged races I now had names for. The dragon race, the goblin, the dwarf, the beast race, the muroke, the lava race, and more. The weather had turned mild. A soft breeze carrying the ocean scent across the deck.

And when Dark Moon, in charge of the resource fishing rod today, handed me the first pull, my spirit. That first item was a small surprise, a blue grade summon rather than the usual white.

The second pull was a small spirit crystal or vein resource card, green, which would hand me a small spirit crystal vein on use, and I raised an eyebrow because spirit crystals feed elemental spirits, and my two could always use more. The catch was the small in the name since a minor vein would not produce much. So I filed it away to run through hundred transformations that night.

The last item read as a tailor shop blueprint, white grade, a shop of tailor who sew clothes, costing only wooden stone, white as it was, I took it as a small improvement to the territory and went back to my book. Not long after, Chano's notification chimed, and I was not surprised because it was the last day of the week.

The first announcement told me the week had ended and that weekly survival point rewards were ready to claim. And the second told the top 10 to choose their additional rewards. I pulled up the survival point rankings first. I sat at the top with 4,370, well ahead of Bake Seol in second with 2,220. Lee Kio held third at 2,110.

Ebake Pung fourth and James fifth with Elizabeth Kaidi Isabelle Cook and Jinmo Songing out the bottom of the 10. I was not surprised to find the gap between me and second place had only widened because the 100,000 odd white stone I had poured in had not been spent for nothing.

Then I turned to my first place reward and what caught me off guard was that there were five items on offer this time and I could pick two of them. I asked Chan Mo and learned the extra pick was a bonus for holding first three weeks running. A welcome surprise that made me wonder idly what the reward would look like if I held first an entire month.

I set the speculation aside and read through the five. The first was a hero class change card, special grade, which grants a hero class and the open-ended advancement that comes with it. Breaking past the level cap's quality alone would impose. That one delighted me at once because I could spend it to turn Hui into a hero. And there was no question I was taking it.

The second was a clear wind staff, purple grade, raising the wielder strength by 30% and inscribed with sharp wind technique, a group buff that lifts agility by 20%. I was not much moved by the staff since I could take any blue item, specify the equipment type through 100 transformations and reliably produce a purple staff of my own, which was already my plan for raising my strength.

The third item was a magic energy cannon blueprint. Purple grade, a city defense weapon that charges off spirit crystal or purple crystal for heavy damage. Built from refined steel, magic pattern stone, and general level beast blood. Fine, but only a production blueprint good for a single cannon. So, it went no further with me.

The fourth was a pet summon card, purple grade, an extra helper in card form, whose value I could see even though I felt no shortage of pets. The last item stopped me cold. An item quality enhancement card, purple grade, which raises an item's quality at a success rate that drops the higher the starting grade climbs. I did not deliberate.

I took the hero class change card and the item quality enhancement card, passing on a pet summon card since I was not hurting for pets. I had been wanting to push the grade of the dragon blood I held for a long while, and an enhancement card was exactly the tool.

With both in hand, I used the hero class change card on Hui first, silently apologizing to Little Blue that his turn would have to wait and promising myself I would make it up to him. When the change finished and I checked her, Hui read as a gold elemental spirit of legendary grade, level seven, every core attribute at 60 or near it with 600 mana.

Her class was now hero and gold elemental envoy. Her skills were hardened gold body, golden shield, sharp arrow technique, golden cage technique, and golden rain. And her loyalty and affinity both sat pinned at 100. The shift mirrored almost exactly what had happened with Wawwa. So I handed Harui a magic eye skill stone and had her learn the appraisal skill too.

Then I pulled out the undersea demon dragon blood purple grade which grants the bloodline of the undersea demon dragon race once consumed, provided every basic attribute already sits at 50 or higher. I used the item quality enhancement card on it without dragging my feet, praying it would not fail, and the purple light hung a moment before fading.

When I checked, the bottle read as Undersea Demon Dragon Emperor blood gold grade, granting the bloodline of the undersea demon dragon emperor the usage requirement unchanged, and I burst out laughing because it had worked. Riding the high, I poured the Azure Sea Dragon Emperor blood into the bottle, expecting the two to merge, only for them to sit stubbornly separate.

I could not make sense of it since both were now the same grade, and started to wonder whether my whole premise about fusing them had been wrong, or whether they were simply meant to be drunk and left to merge inside the body. I decided to just ask and silently put the question to Chonu.

To my surprise, she answered, telling me different blood types require a blood fusion agent to merge, which gave me a path forward. When I asked where to find one, I waited a long stretch and got nothing, which meant Chama had decided she was done loving me for the day. I pouted, put both bottles away, and slumped back into the lounge chair, feeling deflated.

All that anticipation collapsing into a flat result. Still, at least I knew the method now and could chase down a blood fusion agent later, so it was not a total loss. I was about to pick my book back up when Chiao<unk>'s voice returned and I braced for a reminder, but it was an announcement. It said the friends and family fun event was beginning and anyone could pay to invite friends to visit.

After which, 3 hours on, they could enter the treasure hunt space together. I sat there baffled because a treasure hunt sounded pleasant enough until you remember that the moment treasure appeared, everyone present would be at one another's throats over it. Chano really did seem determined to keep us fighting around the clock, never granting a true day of rest.

And I figured even the greediest capitalist alive would tip his hat and ask how she ran a thing this ruthless. For me though, the event was nothing but good since my strength already ranked near the top among the lords in play. The name itself was the puzzle because if the thing were really about loot, it would be called a treasure hunt event, not friends and family fun.

So, the wording clearly favored team play, which put a lone wolf at a real disadvantage. While I was chewing on that, a message landed, and sure enough, it was Lee Kio asking whether I wanted to come over and run the event together, noting that Bake Seal would be there, too.

She waited on my answer more nervously than the message let on because during our last collaboration she had quietly lined up other allies without telling me and she feared that slip had cracked our trust. Wang Mock and Yupong nodding hard. I let it drop rather than dig the hypocrisy hole deeper, told them to eat their fill and that I would make more if it ran short, and turned to business. So, we opened our sheets and I was as curious about the four as they were about me. Yupang read as level six, a hero and mad blade warrior with danger perception at excellent quality. Carrying a single combat skill and mad blade dance along with a green warb blade and earth rock shield. Everyone had spirit eye so I set that aside as standard.
