Every attribute above 40, a hero and ice element messenger carrying water arrow technique, shield, freezing spell, and mystic ice puppet. The last two at purple grade, plus a green staff ring, and sea star armor.

My talent listed as 100 transformations at legendary grade. Once a lord crosses level seven, each level up doubles the attribute gain set by talent quality, which for me meant 10 points per attribute every level. So, it was no wonder Wang Mock and the others were stunned to find me a single level above them, yet nearly double in raw stats.

Lee Kio and Bake Seiel were just as taken aback because while they had known my overall strength ran high, they had assumed my personal strength sat close to theirs. Partly because my last arena showing had been unimpressive. Only now did the gap come clear. Four skills with two purple, three pieces of equipment, and above all, a legendary grade talent.

They knew what that talent meant, that as our levels rose, the gulf would only stretch wider, and all four silently resolved to chase every path to raising their own talent quality. Wang Mach said as much aloud, half despairing, that with his and Yupong<unk>s merely excellent quality talents, he felt almost unworthy to stand beside me.

I privately agreed their grades were low, only Bake Seol's respectable, and in my view, nothing below epic quality really had a future since a hero can climb levels regardless, but a low grade leaves the strength gap yawning no matter how high you climb. I kept that to myself, trusting my talent would push them harder than any warning.

Lee Kio took it as a blow of her own. Her talent only excellent quality, admitting she had assumed I was epic at best and never imagined legendary. And being squeezed between bake's epic and my legendary left her genuinely deflated.

I had a fair guess at what leak was feeling, so I softened it by explaining I had simply gotten lucky that I had traded a talent quality enhancement orb with baked seal last time and used it to lift my talent from epic to legendary.

Bake Seal, startled at being named, then nodded when the others turned to her, confirming the trade and adding she had handed over the orb for an item I held. Reading the unspoken question why she had not used it herself, she explained that the orb improves talent too, that her own had originally been wood element affinity at excellent quality, and that she had used it to raise it to epic.

Lie Kio and the men were openly envious, and I told them not to be discouraged, that they would get chances to improve their grades in time. Lee Kio, still pessimistic, countered that improving talent later was practically too late since you bleed attribute points the whole while you wait. Bakeiel agreed that the earlier you raise your grade, the higher you climb in the end.

And even with her epic grade talent, she was clearly not satisfied. Wang Mach and Yupang traded a glance at Lie Kio<unk>'s dejection and Bakeiel's sighing and read the same despair in each other's eyes. Both wondering if these two felt this crushed with excellent and epic talents, what hope was there for a pair stuck at mere excellent?

Reading the souring mood, Bakes change the subject and asked whether anything new came with reaching level seven. I hesitated and my reluctance made Bakeiel's stomach drop, though she kept her composure and told me to just say it since they would all learn it eventually. So, I stopped stalling and asked whether they all understood that every six levels marked a stage.

Bakeol and Lie Kio both nodded while Wang Mo and Yupang looked lost. So, Bake Seiel laid it out for them that the 30 levels split into five stages, soldier, knight, general, king, and emperor. Each corresponding to one of the five talent quality grades. Yupang jolted at that and asked alarmed whether his excellent quality therefore capped him at level 12.

Lie Kio answered that no. The hero class breaks past the quality ceiling and keeps leveling regardless which let Wang Mach and Yupang breathe again since hearing your end point was that low was the kind of thing that made a person want to lie down and quit. I was not surprised the two women knew this since Chono had told me the same.

So I nodded and dropped the part that actually mattered. That once a lord reaches the night stage, every level up raises each attribute by the talent quality grade multiplied by two. The table erupted, Lie Kio crying out, Wang Moch and Yupang<unk>s mouths falling open. Bakesials brows knitting tight. Bakesial voiced the obvious conclusion that the general king and emperor stages would all scale the same way.

And I told her I suspected exactly that. She said that meant even a single grade of talent difference would translate into an enormous strength gap down the line. And then she felt quiet since she had assumed she would only trail a legendary talent by a little each level. I did not want them fully demoralized.

So I reminded them we were only 3 weeks into this world with chances ahead to raise talent quality. Reading the doubt still on their faces, I added the real comfort that this world holds rare spirit treasures that raise your attributes once eaten. A way to patch the deficit. Leo seized on that at once, asking whether it was true.

And Bake Seol leaned in just as keenly. I told them of course it was true that I had taken some myself. And when Lee Ko asked where to find them, I said I had run across mine while hunting at sea. That what I had found were fruit trees and that there were almost certainly other kinds, though I could not be sure.

I was in fact certain there were other kinds since the well of life blueprint in my storage spelled out that long-term consumption of its water raises attributes, too. But I kept that to myself since saying it served no purpose. Leo took the lesson and resolved aloud to prioritize upgrading her diving technology and hunt the sea harder, the other three nodding along.

Then I steered them back to what mattered most, telling them that finding an island and founding a territory came before all of it, since only a territory gives you a true foundation, and without one, nothing lasts. The four nodded, sobered from the excitement, and clear again on priorities. And I reassured them there was plenty of time and no cause to panic.

We had been in this world only 3 weeks, none of us with a territory yet, none of us truly understanding the place. The only reason they had wobbled at all was that the gap between me and them had hit harder than they were ready for. To the point that they had briefly imagined everyone out here was the same kind of freak I was.

Thinking it through calmly, I was the number one newcomer in the spirit domain by a dominating margin. Very possibly the number one newcomer across both the spirit and demon domains combined. So measuring themselves against me had only been asking for grief. Lie Kio recovered and said with a giggle that since I was strong and well-connected, I had better not forget them when good things came my way.

Yupeong piled on that the big shot would eat the meat and they would happily take the soup and I told them with a smile not to worry that I would think of them whenever something good turned up. Big Seiel then raised the idea of naming the team now that we had formed one.

And every eye swung to me because in all four of their minds, the boss of this team was plainly me. So they wanted to hear what I thought. I felt a name was worth having since a shared name would help cohesion and a sense of belonging now that we had decided to ally. But I am genuinely terrible at naming things.

So, I tossed the question back and asked what they thought we should be called. Wang Mock offered something built around my own name, which I shot down at once is far too personal. And Lee Kio giggled out something built around hers, which I told her was no better than Wang Mocks.

Bio suggested a name whose first part stood for Chian Mo, the ruler of this world, and the second for the sea, which drew a weak protest from Yupang that it sounded grand, brushed aside by Wang Mach, pointing out that nobody would know the meaning unless we told them. I found it acceptable, but decided a mere team felt too plain.

So, I floated Chon Hay Pavilion instead and every face lit up because a pavilion sounded far loftier than a team. Lee Kio declared that from this moment we were the Chonhi Pavilion. The others nodded and I smiled and said that since everyone agreed, that was the name.

Lee Kio never won to let a moment pass unceelebrated, insisted on a drink to mark it, and I caught Mory's eye at which she unccorked a bottle of red wine and poured a glass each. I raised mine and said we would have just the one since we still had the event ahead and could not lose sight of the bigger picture.

We stood, clinkedked our glasses, called out together for the Shian Pavilion, and tipped our heads back to drink as the sea breeze moved over us, the sun bright. Our five shadows thrown long across the deck. When the glasses came down, everyone caught one another's eye and broke into laughter. And I laughed because I was genuinely glad to have a team friends drawn from the same world I had come from.

Windsor and the others were beyond trustworthy. Yet, I had always carried a vague sense that something was missing. And only now did I understand it, that I had never truly settled into this world, and some part of me had stayed an outsider the whole time. Maybe that would soften with the days, but for the moment it had been an unavoidable loneliness, and these friends had finally pried that feeling loose.

Lie Ko and Bake Seol, for their part, felt some of their own weight ease. The burden each had carried alone now sharable. And with someone taller to lean on, they relaxed without quite meaning to.

The two most thrilled were Wang Mach and Yupyang, laughing from the bottom of their hearts because they understood that walking into the Chonhi Pavilion had genuinely turned a corner in their lives, a real shift in fate that left them buzzing. Once we sat back down, I told everyone to eat, and they tucked in without ceremony, praising the food between mouthfuls.

Beexol, in good spirits, teased that after a meal like this, she would never choke down her old rations again. Lee Kio shouted that I had better take responsibility for ruining them. Wang Mock and Yupang staring at me hopefully. So, I looked at Beaol helplessly since the whole thing was her doing.

She watched me squirm with open enjoyment, having felt stifled under the weight of my numbers, and lobbed me a difficult question on purpose. So, I simply turned to Mory beside me and asked whether after meeting our own needs, we had any flour and vegetables to spare. The four of them blinked, caught off guard, because they had only been joking and had not expected me to take it as a real request.

But a beat later, every one of them looked to Mory with frank hope since nobody alive turns down the promise of a good meal. My answered that the territory turned out 200 kg of flour a day and had built up a fair store of it by now, that we had harvested several batches of vegetables, and that our current stock was enough to feed everyone for 2 weeks.

The other four stared at me the moment I let it slip that I could know my own flower and grow my own vegetables. Lie Kio called it outrageous and the rest of them agreed that a lord who fed himself off his own land was frankly a little unfair. I let the thought turn over in my head.

I had inventory now and I could always make more so parting with a small share cost me almost nothing. We had only just formed this team and as the boss of it I could spend a trifle to look generous and buy their goodwill. Killing several birds with one stone had always been my favorite kind of math.

So, I told Mory to keep our own supplies topped up first, then hand each of them one portion of the surplus. She nodded, already knowing exactly what to do. When the food reached their hands, the four went awkward since none of them had earned a scrap of it. Bake shiel told me at once that there was no need, that they had only been talking and I shouldn't take it so seriously.

The others nodded along with her, insisting the whole thing had been a joke. Of course, I understood exactly what they were thinking. I told them not to refuse, that these things meant little to me, and that on the day our team was born, I ought to set an example. Lee Kia looked ready to argue again, but I got there first and said to just take them.

I would not be handing out free gifts in the future, and this was the only time. After that, nobody kept refusing, and both Bakes and Leo looked more at ease than before. If I had kept giving them things, they would have felt like my subordinates rather than equals, and that was not the arrangement any of them wanted.

My one guarantee had settled the whole matter. Everyone took what Mory carried over and thanked me again, and we ate and drank until Chano<unk>'s voice broke across the sky. She announced that this treasure hunt was a team event, that points would be tallied from the whole team's hall, and that the top 10 teams would earn point rewards.

The full rules had already been posted to the world channel, she added, and the event would begin in 10 minutes. All five of us opened our panels to read. The rules were short enough to grasp at a glance. Killing a monster earned points equal to its level, multiplied by its quality tier.

Any item we found would be converted into points once the event closed, and the more valuable the item, the larger the hall. Each team followed the person who had started it and that leader collected an extra reward once everything was scored. This was a lord event which meant no subordinates could set foot inside.

The third rule was the one that made me quietly delighted because the leader's bonus meant my little bit of generosity had not been spent in vain. Bake seol and the others clearly caught the same detail and the looks they turned on me went a shade more complicated. Lee Kio asked outright whether I had known about all this in advance since she had originally meant to gather her own team.

I would hardly admit it, so I only gave her a ry smile and asked how I could possibly have known the rules before they were posted. I added that at the time, I had simply wanted Wang Mo and Yupong along. Lie Kio thought it over and decided I could not have known and chocked it up to luck.

Bake Sio gave me a long knowing look and thought better of me than ever. She had already worked out that this was no accident, but a bet I had placed by reading between the lines of Chan Mo<unk>s announcements, and to her that gamble was itself a kind of talent.

If I had known she could see straight through me like that, then I would have said she was the worm living in my gut. We waited and before long, a portal opened on the deck. I studied it with some surprise because it seemed we would not be sailing anywhere this time. The fight was going to happen on solid ground.

None of the five of us hesitated, and one after another, we stepped through. Windsor and Dark Moon watched me go with worry all over their faces, right until the portal winked out behind me. They knew I was no weakling, yet they still dreaded some accident, and there was nothing either of them could do but pray. I never felt the weight of their fear.

By then, the five of us had already stepped out of a second portal onto a mountainside, and every one of us stopped dead at the sight below. The mountain rose straight out of the water, its foot meeting the surface of a vast lake. Two long stone bridge corridors crossed that water, one reaching toward a distant palace, and the other toward a tiny island.

Both the palace and the island sent out further corridors of their own toward places I could not yet name. A faint mist lay over everything and blurred the far shapes into guesswork. Islands and structures were scattered all across the lake. All of them strung together by those pale stone bridges, and the sheer grander of it was enough to freeze five grown lords in their tracks.

I recovered first and asked whether we were making for the palace or the little island. My question snapped the others out of it, and they realized we did indeed have to pick a direction before anything else. This was clearly not a place built for turning back, and once we chose a road, the only way forward would be to keep choosing new ones.

Lee Kio spoke up first and said the palace, because she had a feeling the good things waited inside. The others nodded along, so I made the call for the bridge that led to the palace, which suited my own instinct. Anyway, we came down the mountain toward the corridor with Yupang out front, a mad blade warrior who still carried his Earth Rock shield.

I brought up the rear while Lee Kio and Bio held the middle, and Wang Mo slipped ahead under his stealth talent to scout. This event was not only about demon beasts, after all, and there were plenty of other players scattered through the lake. In more than a few situations, a human could turn out far deadlier than any beast.

We descended fast and careful. A handful of small demon beasts crossed our path, white quality at the third or fourth level, and Wongmok cut them down himself without breaking stride. The going stayed smooth until we neared the base of the mountain where he finally hit something he could not manage alone. He came slinking back and reported a green quality beast waiting ahead at the knife level, well past what he could take.

Lee Kio volunteered at once since she had not gotten to lift a finger yet. We reached the spot Wang Mock had marked, and I found a black ape better than 3 m tall. My spirit eye readed out as a blackbacked ape of green quality at level 9ine. Its strength, agility, and defense all hovering around 30. Its skills listed as sharp claws, roar, and earth spike.

Middling stats meant it was no real threat. Though I made a note to watch for that earth spike so nobody got caught flat-footed. I wasted no time and threw my freezing spell straight at it, and layers of ice climbed from its feet and spread up its body.

The moment it took the hit, its eyes went red, and it loosed a roar that left all of us dizzy for an instant, so that my freezing spell nearly guttered out. Seeing the ice keep climbing anyway, the ape flew into a rage and snatched up loose stones to hurl at us.

Before it could throw, vines came writhing out of the ground and lashed around its arms because baked seal had triggered her entanglement. A yellowish brown glow gathered over the beast as it wound up for earth spike. I was already reaching for my shield when Lee Kio moved beside me. She drew her flowing wind bow and an arrow bloomed out of thin air along the string.

When she loosed it, the shaft flew clean into one of the ape's eyes, and the brown glow around its body snuffed out in the same heartbeat as its shriek. Its hands and feet were both pinned, so it could not so much as flinch, and Leio put a second arrow through its other eye. The struggling faded fast after that, and soon the ape stopped moving entirely.

Wang Mock drifted over in stealth and jabbed its skull a few times, and only once he confirmed it was truly dead the rest of us relax. My eyes caught on a peach tree standing about 10 m behind the corpse, and I walked straight over to read it.

The fruit came up as origin honey peach of white quality, a heaven and earth treasure that would randomly raise a person's attributes by one or two points once eaten. And the tree carried exactly five. This was real quality. I told everyone we would each take one since it was the lowest grade of treasure and the count matched the team.

The four of them were quietly thrilled because none had ever touched a heaven and earth treasure before. Each of us bit in and the peach dissolved into a ribbon of light that poured down into our stomachs. I had done this before and barely reacted, but the others were startled half out of their skins. And Lee Kio blurted out a question about what was happening.

So I explained, passing along everything the gym mountain turtle had once told me about these treasures. And the whole team marveled that such things could even exist. When we checked our own panels, the readouts showed Yupang up two points along with Wong Mo and Bio while Lie Kio and I picked up only one a piece.

Lie Kio shook off her small disappointment fast and declared she would be hunting far more of these from now on. We pressed on and reached the foot of the mountain. Stepping out onto the stone bridge corridor itself. Halfway along the span, a demon beast blocked the road, thick limbmed and 2 m tall and four long.

A single horn rose from its head and black scales ran down its body with red lava seething in the seams and dripping to the stone. My spirit eye named it a lava beast of blue quality at level seven. Its strength 35, its defense 36. Its skills lava armor and fire stream.

Lee, Kio, and the others went solemn at the readout since the beast outmatched their raw stats, though their skill buffs kept them from real worry. And I was standing right there with every base attribute of mine already above 40. I gave the order to stop talking and simply attack. Bake seal threw out her entanglement while Lie Kio knocked an arrow.

Wang Mock melted into stealth to wait for his moment, and Yupe planted himself in front of me with his shield. The vines rushed the lava beast, but the lava on its hide scorched them to withered strings almost as fast as they landed, and Bake Seiel's face changed. Le Kio loosed three arrows in a chain with her triple shot.

So the beast dropped its head. Its black scales flared and swelled until they fused with the molten seams into a suit of dark red full body armor. That was lava armor. All three arrows struck the same point and only the third one bit through. Lie Kio<unk>'s expression turned grim. But by then my own skill had finished casting, and a mystic ice puppet took shape before us all.

It stood 2 m tall with a great sword in its grip, and it charged straight at the lava beast the instant it appeared. The others gaped at the sheer bearing of the thing, none of them sure yet how strong it really was. Then it showed them. The puppet sprinted right across the molten trail the beast had spilled with its fire stream and reached the lava beast in a breath.

Then it swung the great sword down through its skull, splitting the head open and burying the blade deep in the body. Wangmok popped out of his stealth and hollered that it was outrageous. The rest of them watched the whole thing in shock and then swung their eyes to me, and I wore a perfectly calm face.

I knew what they were thinking, that even with better stats, I had no business ending the beast so easily. I felt quietly pleased and having Lee Ko and Big Seal on hand for it. Both of them riding the top three of the points ranking only made the feeling sweeter. The math held no surprise for me at all.

My base strength sat at 43 with another 10 off my gear. The mystic ice puppet was a purple skill that carried 130% of my strength, which put its own figure near 69. That was roughly double the lava beast, so of course the fight had been no contest.

I watched the beast dissolve into a shaft of light and vanish the moment it died, leaving nothing at all behind, and I frowned, guessing this was some special rule of the event. I told everyone to hurry and keep moving since mystic ice puppet only lasted 10 minutes and I had no wish to waste any of them.

The four of them shook off their days and fell in behind me, and we pushed forward at speed with the puppet leading the way. Behind us, where the beast had died, a fresh copy of it flickered back into being. A weaker team of three stumbled into it not long after and fled in a panic once one of their own fell.

We knew none of that because we had already reached the palace doors. We shoved the doors open and walked into the main hall where a long corridor ran on with rooms lining both sides and every door shut tight. I had the puppet push the first one and a black shadow shot out the instant it cracked only to be hald by the greatsword before it cleared the frame.

It was a jet black civet cat white quality at the fifth level. We searched the room top to bottom and turned up absolutely nothing which left everyone deflated. The next room held a giant black rat of white quality at level six. And after the puppet dealt with it, we came up empty a second time.

The mood went grim, and the same worry crossed all our minds that the whole hall might be nothing but a demon beast den. Killing beasts earned experience and points. Sure, but what we truly wanted were items. The third room finally broke the pattern, holding no beast at all, only a table with a single white porcelain bottle set on it.

I walked over and read it as an experience pill of white quality, worth a random 10 to 20 points of experience each. And when I tipped the bottle out, there were 10 pills inside. I said we would split them evenly, two a piece, since I honestly did not prize the thing much.

Wang Mach waved it off and said he and Yupon would take one each and be glad of it, feeling they had been riding my coattails and contributing little. Bake seol spoke up next and told me that she and Lee Kia would take two each and I should keep the rest. I did not argue because I had genuinely done the heavier lifting.

Once the pills were down, we started moving again. The rooms after that ran much the same. Some hiding beasts and some hiding loot. None of it anything to write home about. Most was white grade odds and ends like experience pills, commoner summon cards, and wall blueprints. In one room, after the puppet cleared the beast, our habit of searching finally paid off.

I turned up a small pouch and shook white crystals the size of a thumb pad into my palm. My spirit eye called them spirit crystals, a material with broad uses, good for strengthening special creatures, raising structures, and now and then serving as currency and trade. I suggested we divide them by need once the event ended, and nobody objected.

When that puppet melted back into light, I let it stay gone since it drained too much mana. The beast we were meeting now, all white and green quality, were nothing we could not handle ourselves. We threw up a few shields for safety and kept exploring. And the deeper we went, the finer the loot grew, though the beast grew right alongside it.

At last, we walked out of the corridor into a grand hall held up by nine golden pillars beneath a half-d ceiling. In the center stood a circular platform 3 meters high with a purple box on top. A single staircase climbing to it and a demon beast lying across the foot of those stairs. The beast was small, only half a meter tall and a meter and a half long.

Its fur a mix of red and blue. Its shape all fox. My spirit eye read it as an illusion demon fox of purple quality at level 8. Its agility is startling 57. Its skills reality break illusion weakening and swiftness. Everyone sobered at that, myself included. Wang Mach whispered whether we might simply slip up and steal the box.

And Yupong<unk>s eyes lit at the idea. I shook my head and told them the fox's reality breaks stood a good chance of seeing straight through Wang Mock's talent, so the odds were poor. He nodded since the risk was real enough. Then I told him to still watch for an opening to go for the theft while the rest of us fought the thing.

A two-pronged press. We layered on a few shields for the small comfort of it and began to close on the illusion demon fox. Yupang took the front while Bake Seiel and Lee Kio held the middle and I brought up the back with the whole plan leaning on my puppet. I said the word softly and Bake Seiel let entanglement fly first.

But the moment her vines appeared, the fox burst into red and blue light and slid clean away. It was simply too fast. A pale blue shine woken Lee Kio<unk>'s eyes. Then her eagle-eyed talent and red light streamed along her flowing wind bow and folded into a crimson arrow. Her decisive strike, she loosed it, and the fox's blue eyes surged.

And the arrow racing at it actually slowed in the air while its body dodged with ease. Her face went grim because the finest bow in the world means nothing when it cannot land. Just then, my first mystic ice puppet took shape. It bore down on the illusion demon fox with its great sword and the two locked together.

For all the fox's power, its illusions meant nothing against a puppet, so it was forced back on raw strength. I cast a second mystic ice puppet, and with both of them in the fray, the fox was already pinned. Bake Seol wrapped it in entanglement while I froze it in place, and Lee Kio poured arrows into it. Before long, the fox was a mess of wounds and could not hold on.

Dying at last under a single stroke of a puppet's blade, Wang Mock chose that moment to reveal himself. Standing already on the high platform in front of the treasure box, I climbed up and asked why he had never made his move. He answered with a mournful face that the box simply would not open, that I had clearly stayed sealed until the illusion demon fox was dead.

That stunned all of us. The event gave no room at all for opportunists. I told him it hardly mattered since we had put the fox down anyway. And then I reached out with some anticipation and lifted the lid. Five items waited inside and I read them one at a time. There was a talent quality enhancement orb of green quality with a chance to raise talent grade but only for green.

And below there was a yak puppet blueprint of green quality for a big strong backed holler good for moving supplies. There was a mystic origin purple crystal flower of blue quality. A heaven and earth treasure worth 5 to 10 random attribute points. There was a barbarian warrior troop layer of blue quality. Finally, there was an abyss holy wall construction blueprint of purple quality.

A wall that at maximum level could turn aside the ruin of a king level creature costing 4,000 stone to raise. I noticed the item seemed pegged to our own talent grades. Since my talent and baked seals ran legendary and epic, while the box itself was only purple, our share got knocked down a level. Two of the five strengthened a person and the other three strengthened a territory.

So, I took the abyss holy wall blueprint without hesitation. The best of the lot. The rest I told them to split among themselves and nobody minded me claiming the top prize. Wang Mach took the talent orb and Yupon the yak blueprint while Bake Seol swallowed the purple crystal flower on the spot and the barbarian warrior troop layer fell to Lee Kio.

We did not linger in that palace once the finest loot was in hand since chasing the leftovers would only burn time better spent moving on. We picked a stone bridge corridor at random and crossed it. About halfway along, a blue quality fire jackal at level six tried to bar us, but baked seal all tangled it and my puppet cut it down in a single stroke.

On the far side, we came out to a huge circular pool ringed by more of the pale bridges. Lotus flowers floated across it, each one cradling a small red brocade pouch at its heart, and a handful of people were already out there in single seat boats, plucking the pouches one by one. We checked and found no readable information on any of it.

" And nobody argued. Each of us took a small boat and began paddling out to gather pouches. I rode inward a while and lifted my first pouch from the center of a lotus. Inside was a small cloud pattern stone mine resource card of green quality, which would seed my territory with a small stone mine. Decent enough.

My second pouch held a pet quality enhancement card of purple quality, a high chance to raise a pet a grade, and my face lit because now little blue could finally climb, too. He had been the very first to follow me, and I still felt a pain that I had never spent either hero card on him. This was exactly the way to make it up to him.

The third pouch gave me a medium purple crystal mine resource card of blue quality, and I could not help a small grin at another fine hall. The fourth was a letown, only a white quality wall blueprint I no longer needed now that I held the abyss holy wall. The fifth, though, was an alchemist summoning card of purple quality, and that pleased me a great deal.

I would be founding a territory soon, and a real domain has to grow on every front rather than lean on one leg. I would not summon the alchemist yet, but there would be no rush once the territory stood. I rode to a sixth lotus and when I reached for its pouch, a film of fluorescent light rose and blocked my hand.

I tried a few others and met the same wall every time and though I did not know the reason, it was plain I had reached my limit. Greedy as I was for more, there was nothing to do but climb out. Lie Kio and the rest were already waiting on the corridor when I stepped ashore and I asked with a smile how they had done.

Lie Kio had managed only three and lamented it. Bake sale had picked four and Wangmoke three while Yupe admitted weakly that he had pulled just two before the barrier stopped him. Bake seal asked half to herself whether I had picked five. I had already guessed the trick of the place, so I told her, "Yes, five pouches. " Everyone chewed that over and found it made sense.

It sat least well with Yupong, the only one of us still stuck on a green talent since even Wang Mo had just bumped himself to blue on the loot he had won. Wang Mo shrugged the whole pool off as a welfare zone. No danger anywhere in it, everything down to luck, and the rest of us nodded. Bake seol said there was nothing left here worth staying for.

So I picked one of the three bridges at random and led us across. To my surprise, no guardian beast waited on this span at all. Instead, we ran into a small party of six. My four and I traded a glance and the same playful edge showed in every pair of eyes because we were about to block the road and rob the robbers.

I was not surprised the other side failed to recognize us since we had hidden our information and there were far too many faces in this world for anyone to know them all. Their leader was a lord named Jyn Wool, who had pulled coin with six others to form a team and had already shaken down several weaker groups on numbers alone.

He eyed my five and judged the risk worth it. He reasoned that greater danger meant greater reward, that six was strong among lords, and that five of us meant at least five brocade pouches ripe for the taking. He called across the bridge that we should leave behind whatever we had won at the Lotus Pond, or we could forget about passing.

I could not help laughing. I only smiled, but Lee Kio giggled outright, and Wang Mock and Yupong laughed harder the moment they heard her. Jin Wool and his six froze, baffled about what could possibly be so funny in the middle of a robbery.

One of them stepped up with an ugly face and shouted at us to be serious, which only set everyone laughing louder until even I lost the fight to keep a straight face. Jyn Wool studied us and beneath his sour look, I could see unease creeping in. He softened his terms and said, "We needed only leave two items, and his own people erupted.

Half of them barked his name in protest, and none followed why their boss had suddenly gone soft. He waved them down and told them to trust him. And though they seethed, they held their tongues and glared at us with murder in their eyes. The whole thing had gone from ridiculous to tiresome.

So I let my smile fall and my face turned cold and told my team to finish this fast and get back on the road. Wang Mock and Yupyong answered and charged with Wang Mach vanishing into stealth as he went. Jin Wool and his crew pald plainly caught off guard, having never met anyone who simply attacked first.

Yupong reached them before they could think, and one sweep of his battleblade knocked a man clean off his feet. Wang Mach was cruer still, surfacing just long enough to drive the thorn in his hand through a throat and finish the man before slipping back into stealth. Jin Wool<unk>s people understood then that they had kicked a steel plate.

Their leader shouted that we should talk, that he would let us pass, and I ignored him completely. You threatened to rob us and then begged to wave us through. Enemies do not earn mercy. I froze one man mid swing at Yupang and Yupang took the opening and killed him with a single cut. Jyn Wool saw I had no intention of stopping.

So he dropped the talk and his last four rushed my front line. I threw freezing spell again and baked seol through threw entanglement and we locked down one a piece while Wang Mog flitted in and out to harry them. Lie Kio seized the moment and put three arrows into the knot of them, killing two and wounding the last.

Jin Wool was shaken to see his six shredded so easily and just as he turned to run my water arrow technique caught him square in the head and he dropped where he stood. Wang mocked mopped up the wounded man. I gathered the six lord cores off the bodies and laid out the split.

I would keep four while Le Kio and Biola took one each with the rest of the spoils going to Wang Mach and Yupong to divide. Bake Sio agreed without fuss and Leo nodded since I had carried the fight and nobody begrudged me the lion share. The cores being worth far more than the scraps these six had carried.

Wang Mach agreed with him. Leio cut it off and told them to quit stalling. She wanted none of it, she said. And the two of them should just divide it since the best piece was a green blueprint she thought little of. She wondered aloud what these six had even been robbing people for. Loneliness. Wang Mock caught my nod and stopped arguing.

Past the corridor's guardian beast, we reached the next place, and it was a strange one. A great tree hung all over with treasure chests. Every chest black so that no value showed through. 20 or 30 people were already there hammering at the chests. I found a fellow who stood waiting on his teammates and asked what the setup was.

And he looked by five over, smiled, and pointed up at the tree. He said to just attack the chests that once you knocked one down, its contents dropped straight into your spatial grid. I nodded and asked, a little puzzled, whether that meant we could keep cracking chests forever. " I thanked him and he waved it off and said I would figure the rest out fast enough once I started.

I turned to my four and told him to get to it and Wangmok wailed that none of them carried a ranged skill. That gave me pause since it was true and I had no quick fix. The helpful fellow from before cut in and said they could just climb the trees and Yupang wore a look that plainly called him a liar.

The man insisted saying he had come down out of one himself not long ago and at that Wang Mulk and Yupang went off toward a trunk with something like admiration on their faces. I paid them no mind since there was no danger here at all when everyone was busy spending mana on chests. I lined up a chest and cast water arrow technique.

The dark film around it shattered. The chest burst into a puff of black mist, and something new settled into my inventory. It was an origin apple seed of white quality, the seed of a heaven and earth treasure that would grow me an origin apple once planted. My face stayed calm even as I cheered inside because the grade was low, but the item itself was fine.

I loosed another water arrow at the next chest, and this time the film held and nothing dropped, so the protection ran stronger on some chests than others. And maybe the tougher the shell, the better the prize inside. I hit the same chest again with two arrows and finally cracked it.

The item that fell was a holy light guard skill stone of blue quality, a group defense skill covering a 100 meters, learnable only by a light element profession. Good stuff, and better still, it proved my hunch. I thought a moment and set the water arrow aside since it was only a green skill, weak and single use.

The mystic ice puppet was a purple one that carried 130% of my strength and lingered a full 10 minutes. This place could not have been more perfectly built for it. I chanted the incantation and called up a mystic ice puppet 2 meters tall with a great sword and every eye nearby swung to it.

I drove the puppet up the great tree and set it swinging at the chests and it did not disappoint, most of them dropping to a single stroke. The loot ran mostly white and green, so I spent my last mana to raise a second puppet. The two of them went to work together, some chests taking one sword and some taking two.

The onlookers watched the pair rampage across the tree and stared at me as if I were some celestial being. Someone muttered that this big shot looked familiar and his companion agreed he had seen the face somewhere before. Then a man let out a yell that startled the woman beside him.

He ignored her scolding entirely and came running at me, shouting the whole way to ask whether I was really the hashin who topped the survival ranking. The crowd erupted, half in disbelief that they were seeing me in the flesh. A few of them scrambling to take back careless jokes the moment they realized whose attention they might draw.

I had been busy sorting my hall and half thought it was Wang Mock calling. So, I turned to find a stranger barreling toward me, and I honestly had no idea who he was. The stranger reached me and announced, glowing, that he was my idol. His name was Gian Bong, and my face fell flat since I doubted he had heard his own words.

His friend, Jupyang, stood frozen with shock. The man who had asked him for directions a moment ago, had turned out to be the Hashin who topped the survival ranking. Gian Bong, claiming to be my idol, only stunned him further. Gian Bong caught his slip fast and corrected himself, insisting instead that I was his idol. You do not slap a smiling face.

I could not just chase him off. So, I asked mildly whether he needed something, mostly hoping to send him on his way so I could get back to my treasures, he said a touch bashful that he only wanted a photo, and I told him to go right ahead since there was no reason to refuse.

He fell to snapping pictures in a fever of excitement, and Joe Pyong edged over to ask for one of his own, which I granted easily enough after he had helped me earlier. But more people were drifting in now, and it was giving me a headache. So, I told Jon Bong to keep order and send them up one at a time.

Then, I cast a shield on myself in case someone thought to use the chaos for a cheap shot. Jon Bong threw himself into crowd control with delight. I stopped paying the crowd any mind because my inventory slots had jammed full. Sorting through them, my eyes lit and a smile pulled at my mouth.

I had turned up a spatial ring, a special item holding 30 cubic meters of space for storing materials, though nothing living. This was a pillow arriving right as I got drowsy. I slid the ring onto my finger and poured everything from my crowded slots into it. The two mystic ice puppets kept knocking chests loose while I worked through my grid and fed things into the ring.

To my disappointment, most of the hall was white and green quality tools. A great pile of them commoner summon cards with pitifully few even reaching blue. Then my eyes lit because I had finally struck something worthwhile. A holy temple construction blueprint. This was a special building that could house different statues for different bonuses, up to 20 of them.

It wanted a thousand stone, 50 holy crystal, and 100 each of fire, earth, and light element gems. A fine thing indeed. With it, I could gather statues down the line and layer new buffs onto my territory and my troops. Not long after, another item lit my eyes, a talent quality enhancement orb of green quality.

It was useless to me, but perfect to trade to Yupang, though I did not yet know what he might have worth swapping. A while later, I pulled a night troop layer of purple quality, another purple troop type for the collection. Then came a long, dry stretch where nothing caught my eye at all. It was not until the 10 minutes had nearly run out that something appeared.

And when it did, it did not merely light my eyes. It nearly blinded them. The thing was a goddess of harvest statue of gold quality which set in a building like the holy temple would raise my territo's food output by 50%. This was staggering. The first statue I had ever pulled came up gold and a goddess of harvest at that and I felt my luck had gone almost unreasonable.

Military strength matters to a territory but food matters more because it is the very foundation and whether you are growing your population or drilling your troops none of it happens on an empty stomach. Only on solid ground of grain does a domain rise tall and grand. I put the statue away.

By then, the puppets had faded and the crowd around me had thinned, most of it having drifted off to swarm Lee Kio and Bixiel for photos. Lee Kio was in her element, all smiles and easy poses, while Bakesio wore the fixed standard smile of someone forcing it so cleanly, I half suspected professional training in her past. I walked over and told her I was done and it was time to go.

She fell in behind me at once and called the others over, so the five of us slipped off toward a stone bridge corridor. A little way down it, she let out a long breath and said it was finally quiet. And everyone laughed. Past another set of guardian monsters, we reached a new place. A flat expanse dotted with items set at intervals across the ground.

All of them readable this time. No one had arrived here yet, so with no one to question, we simply had to feel it out ourselves. The instant we stepped in, a message surfaced in every mind, telling us each person could claim only a single item here. And in the same moment, our bodies turned heavy, as if we had shouldered a great load, the weight climbing with every step I took inward.

It came to me quickly that the deeper you went, the harder the gravity pressed, and that the deeper you went, the richer and rarer the prizes. I told the four of them that what they walked away with depended on their own strength, and wished them luck. Then set my feet moving toward the center.

I did not bother reading the outer items since their grades ran low, and checking would only waste time in a place where every heartbeat added weight to my frame. I walked until I hit the limit of what I could bear, and stopped close enough to the core circle that five more steps would have carried me in.

But I knew full well I could not manage it, that another pace would crush me flat against the stone. So this was where I would search. The first item read is a commoner summon card of purple quality, a guaranteed professional, which struck me as ordinary. So I moved on. Next came a large forest resource card of purple quality that would solve my wood.

Then a large fish resource card of purple quality that would solve my food. I passed on both. Disappointed, my whole body aching now and worn thin. The items after that ran the same resource cards and commoner summon cards. There was the odd purple wall blueprint. I no longer needed with the abyss holy wall in hand and purple troop layers I already owned.

What I wanted was something I had never held before. The more I walked, the more my hope finned until I half decided to simply grab a purple troop layer and be done. Then, just as I made to turn back, the very thing I wanted appeared, a lord's manner style selection card.

It was a special item used at a territo's founding to choose the shape of the manor itself, castle or palace, and the like. This one was genuinely special. I was not naive enough to think the different manner styles amounted to nothing more than decoration. I knew perfectly well that different structures meant a world of difference in defense and in comfort.

So the moment I read it, I made up my mind, chose it without a shred of hesitation, and turned to leave. I was nearly at the end of my strength, my whole body sore and screaming, and I felt that a little longer in that extreme position would have crushed me outright.

The pressure eased with every step back, and by the time I cleared the area, Leio and the other four were already waiting. Leo pounced at once and asked whether I had gotten anything good. I told her it was not bad, an interesting item without saying what it was, and she deflated a little, but let it lie. I glanced around and saw seven or eight newcomers already pushing into the flat expanse.

So, I said this place was of no more use to us and started toward the Stonebridge corridor. The five of us had not walked far, had not even met the corridor's guardian beast. When the announcement rolled out, "The treasure hunt had ended," it said. "Everyone was to leave at once, and stragglers stayed at their own peril.

Points would be tallied after the close, it added, and rewards claimed once the settling was done. The five of us stopped mid-stride, and though none of us felt we had gotten enough, there was nothing to do but halt. A teleportation portal opened beside us. I said we would catch up another time and see one another again if the chance came, and that I would send everyone their share once I got home.

Bake all said her goodbyes, and Lee Kio chimed a sweet farewell, while Wong Mo and Yupang called out to their boss as they went. They stepped into the portal one after another, and I followed once they had gone. The moment I came out the far side, I saw Windsor and Dark Moon.

The two of them rushed up, looked me over head to foot, and let out their held breath once they found me unheard. Their worry warmed something in me, so I pulled them both into a hug and told them not to fret, that I was fine and had done very well besides. Then I crossed to the recliner, dropped into it, and blew out a long, stale breath.

I reached for the drink on the side table and downed it in one go. Feeling myself come back to life. I took the holy light guard skill stone out of my ring, the blue one, a group defense skill covering a 100 meters that only a light element profession could learn. I had settled on giving it to Windsor from the instant I won it.

So, I told her to take it and learn the skill. I chose not to feed it to 100 transformations because even though the talent could now specify a type, there was still a risk. If it came out no longer a skill stone or one nobody here could use, it would simply be a loss. On top of that, I had picked up enough blue items in the event to keep the talent fed.

Windsor took the stone from my hand and used it on the spot. Next, I drew out the pet quality enhancement card, the purple one with its high chance to raise a pet, a grade for anything purple and below. I called little blue over and used it on him and with real anticipation, I read his information.

He came up as Little Blue, a deep sea demon whale of gold quality, now 30 m long and at level seven. His strength read 52. His skills a spread of impact, bite, transformation, and the heavier thunders sword and giant wave. The grade behind his name had shifted from purple to gold, and I laughed out loud, delighted.

He had followed me since the very first day, starting at blue, and now here he was, gold. I could not say why, but raising him gave me a real sense of accomplishment. All he lacked now was a hero class change card, and then I could turn little blue into a hero, too. Once I had sorted my hall, I sent Lee Kio and the other four their shares down the trading channel.

Then checked my assets and found them up better than 10,000 again. That came from my subordinates fishing all day from Windsor and the others hunting through the afternoon. And from what I had held before, 10,000 and change did not move me much because upgrading anything cost a small fortune these days. Taking the tower ship from the third level to the fourth wanted a full 80,000 white stone.

That was eight gold stone. I wondered when I would ever scrape that together. Mory came over just as I was lamenting it and told me dinner was ready. I got up off the recliner and the three of us went back to the core cabin and sat down to eat.

Over the meal, I asked whether Ha Rui was still drilling skills, and Windsor smiled and said yes, that the two little ones were nothing if not diligent. I nodded and chatted a while with my getting a general feel for how things stood on the ship. When dinner was done, I stretched out on the sofa, my head across Dark Moon's legs and my feet across Windsor's.

Then I opened the chat channel and told everyone they truly should have joined the event since the hall had been enormous. The channel was a riot. One man swore he had landed on a small island and never once beaten the quarter's guardian beast, stuck there the whole time with nothing to show. Others had scraped up a troop nest or two or simply hidden in a cave until the end.

The whole feed kept circling back to the great tree since three of the top ranked lords had turned up there together and that made for a sensation. Windsor and Dark Moon worked the ache out of my shoulders while I lay back easy and watch the channel goof off. Then Chonmo announced that the tallying was finished and individual rewards have been dropped into our space grids.

So I opened mine at once to find two new items.
