I raised an eyebrow at a piece of gear and equipped it. Handing my old starfish armor over to Dark Moon.

The second item stopped me cold. It was a Chano City property deed, a special item that served as proof of ownership to claim a property inside Chano City itself. I sat bolt upright, stunned, because this was as good as being handed a home, and a home in Chion Mo City, no less. I had talked with the Gyson Mountain Turtle before, and knew exactly what such property was worth.

That even the cheapest, most far-flung lot would cost a fortune well past anything I could touch. It took me a long while to swallow the shock, and a grin stayed pasted to my face the whole time. I hauled Windsor and Dark Moon off toward the bath without waiting for an answer, saying the whole afternoon had rung me out.

Their faces went red, and though they meant to protest, the words never came, so they were tubbed along, dragging their heels. A bath calls for a proper scrubbing of backs, and to show my diligence, I volunteered to wash the two of them myself. Perhaps I scrubbed a shade too hard, since it drew the occasional startled little cry.

Afterward, the three of us lay worn out from all that scrubbing, and I, having done most of the labor, least felt like moving at all. I opened my message window with Yupe and got straight to it, telling him I had a green talent quality enhancement orb and asking what he had to trade.

He had only just lained down, and the sight of my message lit his eyes since he was the last of us still saddled with an excellent talent, which frankly embarrassed him a little. Items to upgrade talent were hard to come by, so this was perfect, and the only question was what he could put up in return.

He turned over his event loot, even a blue quality piece, and guessed I would want none of it, which left the one purple item he had earned for leading the top scoring team. Trading a purple for a green gave him pause since the orb only had a chance to work. Then his eyes lit again and he offered me a ship core, something he had won back during the cross region competition.

I did not hesitate and told him it was a deal and the trade went through his orb for my new ship core. Another ship core meant another level of upgrade for my ship somewhere down the road. I took out the next thing, a commoner summon card of blue quality with a high chance of a professional which Dark Moon had reeled in with a resource fishing rod just that morning.

With it in hand, I told 100 transformations to designate an equipment type. In a flash of light, it became a pair of Streamlight boots of purple quality that raised the wearer's agility by 30%. I gave the boots to Dark Moon since they suited an assassin perfectly.

Then I pulled a Thornwall blueprint of blue quality, an item from the great tree I no longer cared for now that I had the abyss holy wall. I told 100 transformations to designate an equipment type again, and it turned into a thorn armor of purple quality worth 30% more defense. I kept the thorn armor for myself and passed the star pattern armor to Windsor.

Setting the leftover starfish armor aside for subpan in the morning. I took out one more, a pet summon card of blue quality, also off the great tree. This time I did not designate an equipment type since I could not turn all three of the day's uses into gear and wanted to gamble on something else.

I told 100 transformations to designate the same type and it lifted the card to a pet summon card of gold quality. And I cursed out of pure surprise. It had jumped two full grades at once, and better yet, it was still a pet summon card rather than some other item. Without a second thought, I used it right away.

Golden light spilled across the floor, and as it faded, I saw the pet at last. She was a fox of pure white fur with blue eyes and twitching ears. A long snow white tail without a single stray hair curled around most of her body. " Windsor and Dark Moon both looked at me puzzled, and Windsor asked whether I was truly naming her that.

" And asking what she thought. Windsor allowed it was an odd name, but let it go if I liked it. Right then, the little fox sprang off the floor onto the bed and pressed her fluffy head to my face, nuzzling without pause. I had braced for a foxy stink, but instead of anything foul, a sweet fragrance drifted up my nose as she came near.

I ran my hand from the crown of her head down her back to the tip of her tail, and I had to admit the fur was impossibly smooth. I cuped her small head gently and told her that her name would be Dye from now on and asked if that was all right. Her eyes at once narrowed into a smile almost human and seeing it I smiled too.

Windsor and Dark Moon melted at the site and reached out to pet her all over. All that petting from the two of them left the little fox uneasy and she let out the odd small cry. I used the moment to redow these information. She came up as a nine-tailed charming heart fox of gold quality at level four.

Her strength 39, her agility 43, her defense 38. Her skills only charm and heart linking. It was a simple sheet. Level four and two abilities. No more charm I could guess at from the name, but heart linking left me uncertain. Though I was in no hurry and could tested out hunting at sea the next day. I stroked her head and set her down to rest on the sofa.

With Dvi gone, I could not stand the silky looks from Windsor and Dark Moon. So, I coaxed them carefully. I told them they could tease the fox again tomorrow, that it was late and we could hardly keep her here all the while. They gave me their reluctant eyes, but I only spread my hands, and Windsor said softly that she understood, calling me a shin and swearing she was not being unreasonable.

Dark Moon nodded along with her. I knew neither of them was truly difficult, that they had merely been going with the moment, and that I had no wish for a little fox at my side while I rested. The three of us talked and laughed a while, and the closer we spoke, the closer our hearts drew, a subtle fragrance drifting through the quiet of the moonlight room.

Out on the sofa, the little fox listened to the sounds within, wore a look of confusion, and buried her head beneath her big fluffy tail. I woke after dawn, and told the two of them to rest a little longer since nothing pressing waited that day, and pulled all my clothes.

What I wore now were no longer the rags I had arrived in, which had long since fallen past wearing, but a black brocade robe I had bought off the trading channel. I laughed lightly, climbed out of bed, and walked out without waiting for a reply. At the sofa, I found Dowi with her head tucked under that great tail.

And at the sound of me, she lifted it and watched me with clever eyes. I scooped her up as she hopped free, held her against my chest and rubbed her two fox ears. Just then, Mory came out of the kitchen and I told her we would take breakfast on the deck today and to arrange it. She lowered her head, agreed obediently, and called me Lord, and I nodded and walked out.

The first thing I saw on the deck was the row of my people out on the fishing platform. I only gave it a glance and said nothing since I left all of that to soak Pan. I called him over, having remembered the starfish armor from the night before. He answered respectfully, his voice muffled, and asked whether I had orders.

I took out the starfish armor and handed it to him, telling him to use it for now and promising to replace it if I turned up something better. He took the armor, looked it over, and brightened, thanking me warmly. I nodded and paid no mind to the little prompt about his favorability and loyalty climbing since that was only what I had expected.

I told him to go about his work, then went to the lounge chair as always. I took out the resource fishing rod and started the day's fishing. Leaning back while the seab breeze blew gentle and cool. I turned over what sort of disaster the week might bring so I could ready myself early.

But no matter how carefully I combed back through the recent days, I could not find a single threat of one. So I sighed and drew out the incomplete map to check it. Reckoning that at my current pace, I would likely reach my destination by the week's end. Survive whatever disaster this week held, and I would arrive at the end point and found my territory at last.

The rod dipped, I lifted my arm and drew up the first item of the day, a frenzy potion recipe of green quality. It was for a brew that briefly doubled the drinker's combat power before dropping them into a weakened state afterward. A recipe and a surprising one, more valuable still for the power it lent. So I put it away, pleased.

Windsor and Dark Moon came over with Dark Moon settling straight onto the lounge chair while Windsor lifted Dowi from my arms, and I only smiled and said nothing, content to enjoy the quiet. Before long, the second item came up, a set of Starburst magic scroll production instructions of green quality that taught the making of Starburst magic scrolls.

I was overjoyed reading it since I could turn out scrolls in the future and a real production line would be an enormous help. The catch was that the instructions demanded a magic pattern master profession, one I had never so much as heard of before. It seemed I would need to gather all sorts of professional talents down the road.

A territory that means to grow and thrive has to develop on every front rather than pour everything into war. Leaning on one leg alone never stands for long. The third item followed quickly. A small sakeworm resource card of green quality. It was a worm you set among your grain so it bruised sake on its own. Sweet and mellow and beloved by common folk.

I was thoroughly surprised since this amounted to a cash crop. Once my territo's population grew, every corner of its little society would need to develop, and drink was hardly a small part of that. With sake worms in hand, my territory could brew its own in time, and selling it to the residents would line my pockets while enriching their lives.

Two birds with one stone. I put the card away. After a spell of rest, I found Mory had already set her people to preparing the food, and we sat down to breakfast. When the meal was over, I found myself with nothing much to do. So, I told the others not to butcher the fish they had caught, but to save them for Windsor and me to level on.

I was legendary quality now and needed to push my level higher. And I leveled only the two of us for a reason. I wanted a high tier fighting force raised fast so that I would not be left helpless and at a loss the day I ran into a highle sea beast or demon beast.

Noon came and went, and once lunch was cleared away, we took the tower ship down for the afternoon hunt. The ship sank beneath the surface. Whenever we crossed paths with seab beast, we killed them quickly and moved on. Already scanning the water for the next batch of prey, I decided this was a good chance to see what DIY could really do.

So, I let her join a fighting. She lay stretched along the ship's rail with her back to me, her blue eyes fixed on the beasts drifting past. A green level seven sea otter came gliding toward the hull. Di turned her gaze on it, and a ring of soft light wheeled through her eyes. The moment the otter met her stare, it went dazed and strangely fond, its guard melting all the way down.

Then it seemed to recall a companion that had once fought it over food and ambushed it from behind. That memory convinced the otter its neighbor was a deadly threat it had to strike first or die. It whipped around and slammed into the otter beside it. The neighbor, caught flat-footed, died on the spot from one blow to the skull.

The sudden madness rattled the rest of the pack, and they swung weary eyes onto the killer. By then, the maddened otter's own eyes had already cleared. It stared back at its companions in plain confusion, with no idea why they were bristling at it. A heartbeat later, a second otter lost its mind the same way and lunged at the beast next to it.

The whole pack churned into chaos like that, tearing one another apart. By the time they had bled themselves thin, we mopped up what was left with almost no effort. None of it surprised me. It was exactly what Di's two skills were built to do. She only carried the pair of them, but in the right situation, they were vicious.

She would open with bewilder to soften an enemy's will to resist. Then she threaded heartstring through the gap and steered the creature however she pleased against the crowd that made her genuinely frightening because with Da in play, you never knew which of your own allies would turn on your throat. Used well, she was a strategic piece all on her own.

The kind of asset that could tip a losing fight the other way. When the otter were finished, Dvi spun around and dropped into my arms, worn out from the effort, I stroked her smooth fur and told her to rest while we pushed on to the next spot. The rest of the hunt ran smooth with no real danger in a solid hall.

By the time the tower ship broke the surface, the sky had already gone dark. I went straight back to the core cabin to check where everyone stood. Dark Moon had climbed to level six. Her strength and defense both sat at 55. Her agility a little ahead at 57, her intelligence at 54, and her mana past 540.

As a night hunter, she wielded the darkness blade, the darkness erosion, and the moon shadow, backed by a blue shadow stealth, a blue flying sword technique, and her magic eye. Her new purple streamlight boots joined a blue windbreaking blade and a blue auspicious cloud armor with loyalty and affinity both at 100. Beside her level five self, she was a different fighter now, that lone purple piece telling the whole story.

I looked over Windsor's sheet next. She was still level seven. Her raw strength at 60 before gear, her agility and defense both at 67, her intelligence at 60, and her mana well past 600. As a light element messenger, she carried the healing, the light arrow, and the light shield backed by a blue entangling spell, a blue bewilder, a blue holy light guard, and her magic eye.

Her gear ran rich, a purple authority light, and a purple star pattern armor paired with a green hairpin. Her loyalty and affinity at 100. She said she should reach level eight tomorrow and her strength would climb sharply again when she did. I checked my own line last. I was still level seven, my strength at 43 and the rest of my numbers down in the low 40s.

My 400 mana fed an ice element messenger kit built on spirit eye, a green water arrow technique and a green shield, a purple freezing spell and a purple mystic ice puppet. My gear ran a green staff and a green ring and a purple thorn armor. My legendary 100 transformations under it all and no title yet. My experience sat at 73 out of 800, close enough to cross into level 8 tomorrow, too.

I closed the panel, dropped onto the sofa, and opened the chat channel to see whether anything worth reading had turned up. The whole feed was people hunting the sea for the first time, the tone swinging from giddy to grim. One warned a friend who had gone under that morning and sent back nothing, while another swore he had met a golden level 18 python big enough to stop his heart.

A few dreamed aloud about carving territory out of the sea like the Muroke until someone reminded them that a land core only builds on land. Then the talk circled to me to whether the big shot with a Muroke core would rule the water, which the rest waved off as more trouble than any human would want. I closed the feed.

3 weeks in, the survivors had adapted to this world and were pushing out of pure survival into real development. Survival points would climb across the board this week, and just as surely, plenty of them would die on the seabed before it ended. I knew better than anyone how deadly that water was since I had already met the Azure Sea Dragon King and monsters on the order of the gym mountain turtle.

The sea was boundless and out there you could run into anything, so a good hunt leaned on luck as much as strength. I lingered on the channel a while longer, quietly filing away every scrap of it. The strange sea creatures, especially their traits and their quirks, went one by one into the back of my mind.

If I met any of them later, I wanted to keep my footing instead of capsizing in a shallow ditch. By then, Mory had dinner on the table, and we all sat down to eat. Afterward, I went to look in on the mermaid cub. The membrane around it had swelled to about a meter across and thinned enough that I could make out the shape inside.

The lower body was a silver white fish tail. The upper body was smooth snow white skin over a human frame, the small face so delicate, and the eyes so tightly shut that whole thing looked like a piece of art. I studied the figure and given how flat it was up top, wondered whether the mer folk was male, though the body was slender enough to leave me unsure.

Windsor caught me frowning at the cub and thinking something had gone wrong, asked whether the mermaid was in trouble. I told her my doubt plainly. Windsor let out a chuckle, then looked at me and burst into open laughter, while even Mory pressed her lips together to hold back a smile. Dark Moon gave me a strange sidelong look of her own.

I asked the room what on earth had gotten into all of them. Windsor tried to say I was being ridiculously dense, but she was laughing far too hard to finish. And now Dark Moon was laughing, too. I was annoyed and a little embarrassed, so I turned to Mory and told her to be the one to explain.

Mory startled under my stare, stammered that the mermaid was still a child, and dropped her head. My silence was deafening. I said nothing, turned on my heel, and walked to the bedroom without a glance at the laughter behind me. I swallowed the embarrassment, undressed, and got into bed. Then I pulled out a batch of the better items I had been collecting off Wang Mock, the same as any other night.

It was time for my hundred transformations. The first thing I took out was one of Wang Mock's finds, and its description alone made my face go slack. It was an automatic flavoring cup of blue quality. You poured in water and picked whatever taste you wanted, from sour and sweet to spicy and salty and stinky, with one final entry on the list that I could only stare at.

Whatever genius dreamed up that cup had me stumped about what water was supposed to taste like to earn that last label. I grumbled to myself, specified the equipment type and triggered hundred transformations. A golden light flashed off the cup and a staff settled into my hand. It was built from three vines braided around one another. At the top, the three curled outward into a cuped shape that cradled a silver white orb.

The readout named it a heavenly dragon staff of gold quality that raised its wielder's mana by 40% and it came engraved with a skill called second form. That skill let the staff switch into a long spear, trading the mana boost for a 40% lift to strength once per day. The thing had actually paid out gold, the ridiculous little cup, handing me an equally ridiculous prize.

I triggered second form at once and watched the staff reshape itself. The vines cradling the orb stretched into a spearhead while the orb flattened into a guard. The lower vines drawing out into the shaft. The finished weapon was dark gold, a faint dragon pattern coiled along its length. The readout confirmed the heavenly dragon spear with the same 40% strength bonus.

I waited in my hand and found it well balanced and thoroughly to my liking. For now, it was locked in spear form until morning. So, I equipped it and decided to hand my old staff down to Hui. The second item was where things got ridiculous again. It was a stretch fit undergarment of blue quality that reshaped itself to the wearer's whim.

Sold apparently to add a little spice to someone's evening. I rolled my eyes. I was faintly curious what it did, but next to a piece of purple gear, it was easy to throw away. I specified the type and ran 100 transformations and a purple light left a very thin upper body vest in my hands. The readout called it heavenly silk armor of purple quality, 30% to defense, noted as unusually stretchy.

That stretch made it perfect for Hwa, who shifted forms these days. The last item was a man-eating flower seed of blue quality, one I had pulled off the big tree during the treasure hunt. Its flower could stand up to a level 18 creature at full strength. This time, I left the type unspecified and let the transformation run wild.

Random rolls were unreliable, but every so often they surprised me. A purple light passed and I looked down at four gold green seeds where one purplish black seed had been. They were guardian seeds of purple quality now. Each able to raise a guardian that could hold off a level 24 creature. I was a touch let down.

The guardian was the same sort of thing as the maneing flower, only stronger, and no special item had shown up when special items were what I wanted. Still, one seed becoming four purple seeds was a nice burst, and I would take it. I packed everything away and called for my. She came into the bedroom, glanced at my bare shoulders, colored faintly, and lowered her head as she asked whether I needed something.

I had no clue what was running through her mind. I handed her the heavenly silk armor and told her to take it to Hwa. She answered that she understood with a small note of disappointment I did not catch. She had known nothing would happen, but confirming it still left her a little deflated. After my left, I checked my assets, which had crept up close to 20,000.

I had no plans to upgrade anything yet. The island was coming up soon, and I wanted a cushion of funds on hand so I would not be caught scrambling when it mattered. My thoughts were racing ahead toward all of that when I felt something soft against my arm. I turned and found Windsor in a silk night gown, holding my arm with her head resting on my chest.

I said nothing either, only smoothed her lovely hair and pressed a light kiss to it. And for that moment, a real quiet settled over me. I closed my eyes, let the piece stretch out, and drifted off to sleep. The next morning, I stepped onto the deck and ran straight into Huawa and Harui.

Huawa dropped into my arms first, rubbing her little head against my chest without pause, and Harui came right behind and landed there, too. I gathered up the two of them and settled into a lounge chair, then took out my resource fishing rod and cast a line while I teased the little ones.

The day was clear, though I felt none of the usual heat, and the sea breeze had picked up a good deal of strength. That stopped me cold. What if this week's disaster was strong wind? The more I turned it over, the more likely it seemed. The rod trembled in my hand and cut the thought short.

I hauled up my first item of the day, a well- construction blueprint of white quality that would solve a territo's water problem for 50 stone. It was better than nothing, so I kept fishing. I was not too worried about the disaster. My tower ship was no small thing anymore, and the odds of the wind flipping it were low.

I opened my personal store to look at the week's discounts, only to find they had not refreshed yet, which was a shame. A little later, the second item came up, and this one made me sit forward. It was a merit point increase card, a special item that granted a thousand merit points on use.

According to the Jimson Mountain Turtle, merit points were worth far more than white stone or gold stone since Chano City would trade them for genuinely precious things. I used the card without a second thought. Windsor came over to tell me it was time for breakfast. So, I got up and ate with her since a little more fishing could wait.

Once we finished, I went back to the line and pulled a third item, a patch of fertile soil of green quality that sped plant growth 10fold, and I tucked it away with the rest. That was the day's fishing done. I spent the rest of the morning talking with Windsor and watching Huawa and the others drill their skills.

And after lunch, I ran the usual hunt. I checked everyone afterward. Windsor and I had both hit level eight, and Dji had leveled as well, though Dark Moon had not climbed yet. After dinner, I ran 100 transformations on the same blue items, and the rolls handed me two purple pieces of gear and one purple resource card.

There was a black pattern armor for 30% defense and an Azure cloud staff for 30% mana. The last was a large element gem mine in the territory. I handed the armor to Dark Moon and the staff to Harui. Spent a quiet while with Windsor and Dark Moon after that, and turned in. On the third day, the wind was stronger the moment I woke and reached the deck.

The sea churning up the occasional white cap. I called the navigator Lee Ruk over and asked whether today's breeze was hurting or sailing. He answered honestly that it was not troubling the tower ship much yet, though it would become a serious problem if the wind climbed any higher.

When I asked whether the rising waves put the ship in danger of capsizing, he assured me at once with real weight that our safety was not at risk. I nodded, waved him back to his work, and left it there. After Lee Ruk went off, I cast my line, but three tries yielded nothing worth keeping. So, I gave up and returned to the core cabin.

Once breakfast was done, I scooped up Dalgi and checked the personal store again. And this time, my eyes lit up because the discounts had finally refreshed. The item on offer was a candidate qualification, a special item whose entire description promised only that I would receive a mysterious candidate qualification. That told me almost nothing, and the price told me even less that I liked.

It ran one gold stone which came to 10,000 white stone more than the teleportation array itself which struck me as faintly outrageous. Then again, there was no telling what discount each item carried. And maybe the array had simply been marked down harder. I bought it for the gold stone anyway. Whatever the thing was and whatever it did, in my experience, the discounted items had always turned out to be good things.

I was not short on funds. And if this one turned out worth having, letting it slip past was something I would truly regret. I put it away, found nothing else to do, and pulled a deck of cards from the treasure hunt hall to play with Windsor and Dark Moon. After the hunt and dinner, I carried Dark Moon to bed and held her close through the quiet of the night.

I opened the chat channel for a look. The whole feat had turned into one long cry against the wind. Some said their upgraded ships could still take it, while others on wooden holes were tossed at the mercy of the waves. At least one screamed that a wave taller than a house was coming for him before the goodbyes and funeral flowers rolled in.

I closed it. The wind was genuinely dangerous this time, and the waves riding in on it only made things worse. If my tower ship had been any lower a level, I would have been panicking with the rest of them right now. I was quietly grateful I had never once stopped upgrading the ship. Pouring everything into technology back then might well have finished me by now.

I lay there and pictured all those people fighting for their lives across the vast sea. A handful like me riding it out safe and dry. I found myself unexpectedly moved. Then I shook my head. When did I turn so soft? And what did any of it have to do with me? I kept one hand on Dark Moon and ran 100 transformations with the other.

The first was a specified role that gave me a demon shadow bow of purple quality for 30% agility. It was a bow and none of my people used one. But I did not fret since purple gear never goes to waste. Even if nobody here ever drew it, selling it or trading it later would be easy once the newbie lord stopped being so broke.

For all I knew, I might yet pick up a hero who fought with a bow. I set it aside. The second roll, another specified one, turned up cloud treading boots of purple quality for 30% agility, and those I equipped on myself without hesitation. For the last, I let the transformation run random again, and it handed me a ranch construction blueprint of purple quality.

A ranch would let me raise all sorts of demon beasts and sea beasts, whether to feed my residents or to train cavalry mounts, which made it a genuinely excellent thing to own. Its build requirements were another matter. I read them and could not help exclaiming because the list was enormous. It called for 2,000 wood and 1,000 stone, 50 wind element gems and 50 light element gems.

On top of that came 100 each of earth and water and fire element gems, plus 500 spirit crystals. Even wind element gems and spirit crystals of all things. A demand like that told me exactly how precious the ranch was, since the more numerous and rare the materials a building swallows, the greater its value in use. I stored the blueprint, spent a warm while with Dark Moon, and gathered her into sleep.

On the fourth day of the week, I had barely stepped out of the core cabin, heading for the deck when Lee Ru came hurrying toward me. The sight of him moving that fast made me ask at once what was wrong. He stopped and bowed, and I waved the formality off and told him to just tell me what had happened.

His face was grave as he reported that today's sea wind was far stronger than yesterday's and that sailing was now impossible. I was mildly surprised, though it was not really unexpected. Every week's disaster entered its elimination period on the fourth day, and that stretch was always the crulest, so I had braced for something like this, and his words only confirmed it.

I told him not to worry, that as long as the ship was in no danger of capsizing, we were fine. Then I caught the troubled look on his face, the way he seemed to want to speak, and kept swallowing it, and a bad feeling took root in my chest. I told him sharply to say whatever it was.

Lee flinched at the rebuke in my tone, then steadied himself. He said that while the tower ship was in no danger of overturning, the wind was too strong now and the waves too high. He forced out the rest with difficulty, that the ship had grown hard to control and was starting to drift with the waves. And when he finished, he watched me with worry.

I demanded whether he was certain, my expression breaking hard, and when he nodded, I felt my face go grim. I understood perfectly what that meant. If the tower ship truly began drifting with the waves, it would be completely beyond our control. If that lasted two full days, there was no telling where it would carry us.

Worse still, if it drifted clear past the range of my incomplete map, how was I supposed to find an island in all that endless sea? Nearly four weeks of effort could be swept away with it, and the thought set my mind spinning after a way out. The simplest, most direct fix was to upgrade either the tower ship or the diving technology.

A larger ship would resist the wind better, and stronger diving would let us wait it out below. Neither was in reach right now because I did not have that kind of wealth on hand. My stripped down diving technology sat at level four and pushing it to level five ran 24,000 white stone. What I held was barely enough for one level in a single extra hour underwater, which solved nothing.

The full version was worse. 40,000 white stone just to climb from level three to level four, and I could not even cover one step of that. Taking the tower ship from level three to level four demanded a full 80,000, flatly impossible to gather inside a week. Lee Ruk stood there watching the gloom shift across my face, uneasy himself because he understood just as well what a situation like this meant.

Then he saw my expression suddenly freeze. He saw me go blank for a long moment and then settle back into my ordinary self, and he was left puzzled, wondering whether his lord had found an answer. I had. I told Leuk not to trouble himself over any of it and just to hold the ship steady as best he could.

He was clearly still doubtful, but he only nodded and promised to do everything he could to keep the tower ship on course. I reminded him to put the ship's safety above the course itself, and he swore there would be no danger. Then I told him to let everyone know we would hunt this morning and to have them eat quickly, and he bowed and went.

I headed back to the core cabin to eat and get moving, because there was a reason to hunt early. If we waited until the ship had already lost control before going under, we would only be diving along the path the wind had blown us off to, which was worthless. Better to cover some real distance underwater first.

I was about to ask my how long breakfast would take when she carried the food straight out, so I said no more and called Windsor and the others to eat. I told them we would start the hunt the instant we finished, and Windsor and Dark Moon both turned to me in surprise since my schedule almost never moved unless something had happened.

Windsor asked what was wrong, and Dark Moon watched me with a trace of worry, and even Dye curled in Windsor's arms, tipped her little fox head at me with a nearly human curiosity. I laid out everything I had just learned and their faces clouded over together. Windsor told me not to worry and said things might not be as bad as we feared.

All to keep me from carrying too much weight on it. Dark Moon agreed and told me the same and my nodded along beside them. I knew they were only comforting me and it warned me all the same. But I told them honestly that I was not worried because I had already found a solution. That was the plain truth and not just words to ease them.

Windsor's face lit with hope and she asked at once what the plan was. The others looked to me just as eagerly, but I only said I was not sure it would work yet, so I would keep it to myself until the time came. Dark Moon and the rest were a little put out by the suspense, though they held their tongues.

We finished breakfast fast and came up onto the deck. The wind hit hard the moment I stepped out, waves slamming into the tower ship and rocking it, and the canvas sail on the mast had already been drawn down by Lee Ruk's crew. I spared it a glance, said nothing, triggered the diving technology, and took us under.

At first, even submerged, we could feel the surge of the water and hear the wind screaming above. As the ship sank deeper, the howling faded out of hearing, and the sea around us went still. In the span of a short descent, I passed from churning violence into perfect silence. It was a strange singular feeling, like stepping out of a storm swept courtyard into a house and pulling the door shut behind me.

Dark Moon told me to look over there. I came back to myself and followed where she pointed, and there was an underwater volcano. The red glow of its crater plainly visible. It was not bare either. Lush growth covered its slopes, most of it red in every shade from dark and fiery to pale and reddish brown. A genuine sea of red.

Patches of green were scattered through it here and there. I looked it over and found nothing remarkable. So I asked Dark Moon what she saw. Windsor eyed her with the same doubt since she had not spotted anything special either. A volcano with plants on it was nothing new to us, even if this one grew far thicker than the rest.

Windsor asked Dark Moon whether it was the sheer lushness of the growth that had caught her. And Huawa and Ha Rui looked over just as puzzled. Dark Moon shook her head gently and said it was not that. Then rather than string us along, she told us there were no animals on this mountain. That stopped everyone.

We looked hard at the dense vegetation again, and she was right, because growth this thick with not a single animal in it was genuinely unnatural. My eyes lit up at that since special places tended to yield valuable things, and the stranges only made me more eager. I told the group to move in closer and take stock, and the tower ship began drifting toward the volcano.

I ordered everyone to stay sharp and keep low, and we circled once above the canopy first. The nearer we came, the more careful I grew, since a place that promised rare rewards usually promised fresh dangers. The ship slid slowly over the tangled growth while we watched, and I brought up the full diving technology and layered a shield on top just in case.

I circled the whole slope once, and nothing attacked, and that quiet only sharpened my caution. I brought the ship down lower and sent little blue deep into the vegetation to scout ahead. And once he confirmed there was no threat, I activated the underwater breathing technology. Then I led Windsor and Dark Moon off the ship along with Syuk Pan and Huawa and Harui and Doggi, seven of us in all.

At level three, the breathing technology gave seven people 3 hours of open water. And with the shield up around the tower ship, it would be safe on its own for a good while. Six people and one little fox slipped into the jungle, and every one of us stayed on edge. The earlier scan had turned up no animals, but that was no reason to drop our guard.

We had run into plant type sea monsters before, and those were often more troublesome and far stranger in how they struck than any animal. We cast shield over ourselves and each other and pressed on, the tall trees crowding close and lending the place a deep closed in weight. We had gone maybe 6 or 700 m when a fiery red vine lashed out of the grass and whipped straight at Soak Pan.

He reacted fast and swung the lapis shield in his hand around to meet it. But the blow still hurled him backward into a thick trunk before he rolled off onto the grass. The strike startled us, though nobody panicked, and I locked the vine down at once with a freezing spell. It twisted and thrashed like a snake and could not tear free its strength plainly no match for mine.

I looked the thing over. A dark red length hung with black spots that writhed like a snake and read it with spirit eye. It was a black spotted vine of green quality at level 11. Its strength 40, its agility 38, and its defense 41. Its skills camouflage and toxin and whip. My mouth twitched at that list because it was the whole toolkit of a cheap shot artist.

Sopan was probably cursing the thing under his breath about now. No martial virtue at all in this one. It hid, it poisoned, it lashed from cover. I shook off the strange notion, put an arrow through it, and sent it off. Then something I did not expect happened. The black spotted vine dissolved into a cluster of red light moes.

I checked the ground for a drop and found nothing, and instead the moes drifted toward me and sank into my body, the shield offering no resistance at all. Everyone else stared and Windsor threw a healing my way on reflex. I was startled too, but a careful look inward turned up no problem. If it was not my imagination, my body felt the faintest shade stronger than before.

I told them I was fine and to stop worrying, and their faces eased. Silk Pan had made it back to the group by then. When I asked whether he was hurt, he said he was not. The sudden ambush had caught him unprepared, but the thing still could not break his defense, and my concern seemed to touch him.

I told him the red moes had gone into me and left me a little stronger, so the plants here were clearly no ordinary growth. Windsor asked whether I was certain it was not an illusion, and I could not blame her for doubting since that had been a plain sea monster and no heavenly treasure. I said we would know for sure once we killed another one, and we moved on.

It was not long before we met the second sea monster, or more precisely, the second wave, because this time it was a whole patch. It looked like ordinary little fiery red grass on the ground, the kind we had walked past so often we barely noticed it.

Then the moment we stepped into the middle of it, the grass shot up wildly into 2 m vines that lashed out like whips and bound us fast. I checked the ambushers without flinching. It was red blood grass of green quality at level 11. Its strength 35, its agility 34, and its defense 36. Its skills, camouflage and entanglement and paralysis and blood drain.

If the black spotted vine was a dirty fighter, this was a whole field of them. And I was starting to think the mountain was where the dirty fighters came home to roost. Right then, I felt a sting across my skin as a rash of sharp thorns rose on the red blood grass, sank into me, and actually began drinking my blood.

To my alarm, my shield did nothing to stop it. A glance at the others showed them all caught the same way. I wondered whether this was a quirk of the red blood grass alone or a trait shared by every plant on the mountain. If they all ignored a shield that meant a great opportunity waited up here. It could just as easily mean great terror.

I did not dwell on it. The grass drained blood painfully slowly, but I was not about to sit still for it. So I loosed a water arrow technique and cut its roots clean through. It broke into red moes and drifted into me the way the vine had, though these were noticeably fewer. The others went to work on the grass that had bound them.

And what drew my eye was Huawa. Red flame roared up all over her, burning the surrounding grass to ash that scattered into moes and flowed into her, which was simply her blazing body at work. On its own, that would not have caught my attention, since her strength made short work of anything like this.

What did catch it was that even the moes from grass she had never touched were pulled to her blazing body and drawn into her. A guest took shape in my mind. There had to be a fire attribute treasure on this mountain, and the plants had been shaped into what they were by it. Huawa was a fire elemental spirit and an awakened one.

So with her blazing body up, she naturally drew those moes in. It was only a guess I would need to confirm. If I was right, this strange volcano was Huawa's great chance. And she might even reach a full awakening here. When the absorbing was done, I asked whether they felt any stronger since I wanted to be sure, even though I no longer thought it an illusion.

They closed their eyes to feel for it and opened them again in surprise, and their faces gave me the answer before their words did. Windsor said, her voice a little unsteady, that I had been right, and the moes truly had strengthened their bodies. The others looked plainly excited because they understood what it meant. The plant-type sea monsters covering this whole mountain were weakened heavenly treasures.

Just then, I caught a wave of pure delight from Huawa, the kind that made me think she had leveled, and I could not believe she would have climbed again so fast. I checked her sheet. She read as Ha Huaw, a fire elemental spirit in her awakened state. Legendary, still level seven. Her strength stood at 48.

Her defense at 45, her intelligence at 45, and her 450 mana ran her fire element controller profession with its fire marshall god awakening. Her skills covered fireball and an advancing blazing body, a firebinding and a fire shield, a gold fire meteor fall and her magic eye. She wielded a blue flowing fire staff and the purple heavenly silk armor.

Her loyalty and affinity both at 100. There was no level up. Looking closer, I found instead that her agility had ticked from 47 to 48. I had assumed these modes could only strengthen the body, adding vitality and the like, and had not expected them to raise actual attributes. What was more, they seemed to do far more for Huawa than for me.

I had taken in roughly what she had, yet none of my attributes had moved while hers rose. That was a good thing, and I let it go. I told everyone to keep moving, understanding their excitement well enough, because I was every bit as excited myself.

What worried me was that someone might let it carry them off, especially Silk Pan, whose face had gone red with it, and even Huawa and Harui were looping through the water in circles. I reminded them not to lose their heads yet and to stay extra careful since the plants here could apparently walk right through our shields. That tightened everyone up, and their faces turned weary again.

So, I said no more and pressed on. The mountain was thick with plant type sea monsters, and we met one or several every little while, mostly poor green or white stock. No threat to us anymore. Green quality tops out at level 12 with average attributes around 44. I was level eight now with mine already over 50.

And if I had not been short on blood fusion agent, I could have taken those dragon bloods long ago. Windsor's average had climbed past 70. And with her gear on top of it, she was outright terrifying. Even so, I did not loosen my guard because I did not believe a mountain this size would hold no highquality monster.

And my caution proved right soon enough. I ducked a set of sweeping branches out of habit since the plants here were all tricky and I had long since learned the footwork. Then I stepped back to study a large tree with a reddish trunk and a snarling ghost face carved into it. Spirit eye named it a ghost face redwood of blue quality at level 10.

Its strength 55, its agility 53, and its defense 54. Its skills camouflage and shriek and poison mist and whip. The camouflage did not surprise me anymore since it was standard issue on this mountain. The ghostfaced redwood was furious that I had slipped its first swing and started lashing every other branch it had toward us. And we all fell back out of range.

These plant monsters shared one fatal flaw, which was that they could not move. So once we were clear, we opened up at range. A water arrow technique and a light arrow, a sharp arrow, and a fireball all rained in on it, and it could only swing its branches to block and take the hits, which plainly hurt.

Then the ghost face on its trunk writhed and let out a shriek so sharp it stabbed our eardrums and set our heads spinning. And for a moment we were disoriented and our skills stalled. In that gap, the ghost face spat a burst of fiery red mist that spread over us fast. Windsor, far stronger than the rest, shook it off first and threw up holy light guard without hesitation.

A milky half-dome of light that inverted over the ground and sealed us in. The poison mist rolled against it and could not push through, and we breathed easier since we had honestly feared the guard would not hold it either. It seemed only the plant's physical attacks could ignore a shield. We hit back the moment the mist failed.

I loosed a water arrow while Hui called down golden rain and every skill we had swarmed the tree at once. This time its flailing branches could not save it, and it took the killing blow square on the ghost face of its trunk. The whole tree broke apart into far more moes than any green monster had given, and all of them poured into Huawa since it was her strike that finished it.

Syuk Pan watched her enviously while Windsor and Dark Moon hardly reacted. All of them simply glad for her. Once she had drawn it in, I felt the report of her improvement again, and I said nothing because everyone's attributes had risen somewhere along the way. I called for us to keep fighting and pushed on in the same direction.

If the plant monsters here rose as treasures under this strange change, then the closer we drew to the source, the greater the gains and the higher the quality we would meet. So, heading toward the stronger plants was the right call. And since we were already here, there was no reason not to hunt the treasure with everything we had.

Not that I would throw my life away for it. And if the way ahead proved impossible, I would recognize the difficulty and pull back. But it was nowhere near time for that yet. For the next stretch, nearly every plant monster we met was blue quality, and we kept correcting our course toward the strongest of them.

Along the way, Silkpan gained a level, and so did Dark Moon and Dalgi, while Windsor and I banked a great deal of experience, and all of us grew stronger again. As we went, I noticed that for all our turning this way and that, we were climbing steadily upward overall, and it made me wonder whether the treasure sat inside the volcano itself.

If that was the case, only Huawa could go in after it. A fire elemental spirit is a born darling of flame at home in it like a fish in water, feeling not the least discomfort, but only ease. Then Windsor said, cautious that something was wrong ahead. And when I looked, I saw she was right.

Within a radius of 500 meters, there stood a single scarlet tree at the center and no other tree at all. Around that tree, halfmeter scarlet plants blanketed the ground, palmsized flowers blooming on them wreath in flame, small vines trailing from the stems. Spirit eye read them as flame bewilder flowers of blue quality at level 11. Their strength 60, their agility 55, their defense 58.

Their skills bind and burn and mist. Most of the others hovered around level 10. What surprised me was that they carried no camouflage and after nothing but sneaks the whole way up, running into something that fought out in the open left me oddly off balance. Then I turned my eye on the scarlet tree at the center.

It stood over 6 m tall, golden oval leaves on scarlet branches, black fruit hanging among the topmost boughs, and I read it too. It was a dragon blood flame leaf tree of purple quality at level 12. Its strength 88, its agility 80, and its defense 88. Its skills ran whip and burn and frenzy and flying leaf and dragon fruit.

That information rocked me. No wonder it did not bother with sneaking since at that strength it hardly needed to. With it average near 80, I was no match for it and only Windsor could kill it here. It had a deep bag of skills and a whole crowd of flame bewildered flower underlings at its side, which made it awkward to handle, though not impossible.

I told Windor to take the main target while the rest of us drew fire and cleared out that swarm of flowers, and she nodded. Then I gave the word, and our skills went out together and landed across the flowers in the tree, which stirred up a hornet's nest. The vines on the flower stems shot out, and the flowers sprayed sheets of flame that clung to the vines and drove them at us.

I froze the vines with a freezing spell while Windsor raised holy light guard, and the vines passed through the guard even as the flames on them died. Huawa and Dark Moon set to clearing the vines. In the opening, I chanted up mystic ice puppets while Hui loosed golden rain. A shower of gold drops fell into the flowers and cut them down in droves, which took the pressure off everyone.

The three mystic ice puppets took shape. Two hefting greats swords and charging the flowers while the third planted itself at Windsor's side. Windsor kept pouring light arrows into the dragon blood flame leaf tree. And though it blocked them, she still snapped off many of its branches. The tree shuddered in pain, and the leaves along one branch all caught scarlet fire, tore loose, and spun at us in a whirl.

Its flying leaf skill. The leaves slid through holy light guard with their flames snuffed but their speed unbroken and we scattered though plenty still caught us and opened bleeding cuts. Then Huawa swept her flowing fire staff up and chanted aloud and red points bloomed in the water overhead as the sea water around them boiled away. The points fell as huge fireballs her fire meteor fall.

They crashed down and all but wiped out the flame bewilder flowers, snapping off many of the trees branches and leaving it badly hurt. With the flowers gone, we all turned our full weight onto the tree. It kept whipping its branches to block and lash, throwing the odd flying leaf back at us. The two Mystic Ice puppets had already forced their way into its reach and tangled up its branches.

Little by little, those branches thinned out, and the trunk began taking wounds. Our side took hits, too. But Windsor's healing kept it from becoming a real problem, though it burned through her mana faster and faster. Then the whole tree blazed up red, and its branches turned savage, which meant it had thrown frenzy, and branches whipped, and burning leaves flew in a storm.

Water arrows and sharp arrows and light arrows, fireballs and golden rain and the ice puppets all crossed in the water. A battlefield gone colorful with a dangerous kind of beauty. Then I caught one of the trees branches flick and a black fruit tore loose and came sailing at us. And I shouted for everyone to watch out.

I knew it was the dragon fruit skill, though I had no idea yet what it did. The fruit dropped into the middle of us and simply exploded, its fragments trailing black flame, and we threw ourselves flat. Even so, some of us could not clear it in time. And soap pan was one hurled down badly wounded and screaming with black flames still crawling and burning over him.

It caught my arm too and the constant gnawing burn of it was agony. Windsor sent a healing over and still the flame would not go out and my face went hard because if this kept up my arm would be crippled. I had not expected the trees dragon fruit to be so vicious and this unknown black flame clung like a maggot to bone impossible to shake.

Then Huawa flew over, reached out a tender white hand and laid it on my wound. And I watched the black flame draw off my arm and into her. She crossed a seak pan next and pulled the flame off him the same way and there was an expression of pure enjoyment on her face. The look she wore when she ate purple crystal.

Flames surged around her and her blazing body flared. The red gradually shifting to gold as her body stretched taller and her fire marshall god form came out. Her scarlet hair streamed, golden fire boiled off her, red armor sheathed her, and a marking sat on her brow.

I noticed that unlike before, Huawa's pupils had turned into two small black flames rather than their usual gold, which I put down to the black fire she had swallowed. In that fire marshall god state, she beat her wings, lifted the flowing fire staff enchanted, and once more gold moes gathered in the sky overhead. A gentle sweep of the staff sent them falling as golden fireballs.

Her fire meteor fall again, but this time far larger and more numerous and carrying far more menace. When the meteors had fallen, a sword of flame formed in her hand, and holding it, she flew straight at the dragon blood flame leaf tree. The fireballs came down in a scene of sheer ruin, and the tree gathered its branches high to shield itself as it felt the enormity of the crisis.

I watched her charging silhouette against the falling fire, and the tree flailing its boughs, and it hung there like some vast soul-shaking painting. The fireballs fell one after another, breaking the treere's branches and slamming into its body, and some dropped among the flame bewilder flowers and simply erased them. The tree went berserk, tearing every flying leaf free and hurling the lot at Huawa.

But the leaves struck her armor and could not leave a mark on her or slow her by a hair. As she closed, the tree felt the crisis bearing down and flung every last dragon fruit at her past caring about us now. Her speed never dropped, and she flew straight to meet the fruits.

Our hearts clenched watching it until the golden fire on her gathered into a golden shield, and the dragon fruits burst against it, one after another without ever breaking through. More than that, the black flame that followed each blast was drawn into her instead. Her aura swelling with it, and the fire on her sword deepened from gold toward a dark gold.

Nothing stood in her path now. She reached the tree, and the flame on the sword pulsed and drew out into a 3 m blade, and she gripped it two-handed, raised it high, and cut down at an angle. The tree threw up what branches it had left to block. But that was only its death throw.

The flame sword went through like a knife through tofu, splitting the tree in two with a single stroke, so that it crashed to the ground. As it fell, it broke into a vast tide of energy modes that streamed up toward her where she hung in the water. And she closed her eyes and quietly took them in.

Then she opened them, came down to me, and landed, tilting her little head up as if to ask whether she was not magnificent. It was the first time I had watched her fire marshall god form so closely. She stood 1 m 70, slender and armored, her figure finally balanced, spirited, and bold as some heroic lady general.

In the past, she would drop back into a little spirit the instant a fight ended and fall into a weakened days. This time likely from swallowing so much black flame, she was not weakened at all and held the fire marshall god form with ease. I reached out and ruffled her lovely hair, and she narrowed her eyes, smiled, and turned all at once into an obedient good girl.

Dalgi and Harui kept circling her, plainly fascinated by this shape of hers. Then Huawa pointed a finger ahead and then at herself, I asked whether she meant there was something up there that could help her, and she nodded quickly, so I said not to waste time and to move on at once. After that, we met several purple quality plant monsters, all a match for the dragon blood flame leaf tree.

Waja dealt with everyone and gained a good stack of moes and a sharp rise in her attributes. At last, we broke out of the jungle into an open stretch with the volcano's crater another 100 meters up the slope. I looked at that bright red mouth and let out a breath, grateful we had Huawa in her fire marshall god state, because without her getting this far would have been genuinely hard.

Windsor was strong, but her skills were too few, and she had nothing for an area. Huawa was far more versatile. She had an area strike in fire meteor fall, a single target fireball, and a fire shield for defense. In the fire marshall god form, she could even close in and fight handto hand, which made her flexible in a way the rest of us were not.

I looked at the empty ground ahead and felt a little lost since there was no treasure in sight and it could not really be down inside the volcano. I asked Huawa whether she could sense where the thing that could help her was, and she nodded and ran for the crater with everyone trailing after. She reached its edge and pointed down, and we gathered around her and looked into the mouth.

Hot lava churned below, and the sight of it left me faintly dizzy, and I noticed a sheet of transparent film across the crater holding the lava apart from the seawater. I asked whether it was down there, and she nodded. I asked whether she would be all right going in, worried despite knowing she was a fire elemental spirit, and she shook her head to say she would.

I told her to go down then, but I made her promise something first. If there was danger below, she would run at once and not throw her life away for any treasure. And I was serious about it. She patted her chest, flashed her white teeth in a grin, and nodded, telling me plainly not to worry.

I smiled, smoothed her hair, and told her to go and come back as soon as she could. She wasted no more time, spread her wings, and dove for the lava below. When she had first touched down on the volcano, she had felt something tugging at her. The closer she drew to the crater, the stronger that pull became, until at its edge, it had nearly slipped past her control.

Now, the deeper she flew toward the lava, the harder it hauled at her, and she could feel that some change was waiting to work itself through her down here. She sank into the lava and felt only comfort, as if she had eased into a hot bath with every pore of her opening wide, and she swam fast toward the source of the pole.

A few lava sea monsters crossed her path along the way, though none were strong enough to matter, which spared her real trouble, and she went straight down until she reached the bottom. The sight below stunned her, her eyes going wide as she hung their frozen. Two beings lay motionless at the floor of the lava. One was a giant dragon over 300 m long, holy black, and shot through with fiery red patterns.

A pair of curved red horns sat on its head, and a pair of enormous wings spread from its back. It lay sprawled with its head thrust forward, its eyes wide in fury. The gaze still holding fear and ferocity even in death before the dragon knelt a woman 100 meters tall on one knee. A long spear in her hands.

She wore fiery red armor whose lower half fell into a red skirt that left her fair smooth-skinned legs bare. Her face carrying a hard majesty, her eyes resting calm on the dragon's corpse. Huawa looked at the two vast forms and thought that if not for the utter absence of any life force, she would have sworn they were still alive.

She used her magic eye to read them. The dragon came back as Chino Fen legendary at level 30 and nothing else. And she knew the emptiness was only because the gap between them was too wide. Even that scrap was enough to shake her. By everything her lord had told her, the very top of this world reached only level 30.

Yet here she stood before one, a member of the dragon race, no less, and a corpse at that. She turned the magic eye on the woman and Reggiraa legendary at level 29. That she was a top tier being one rung short of level 30 stunned Huawa further since a level 29 felling a level 30 dragon only underlined how strong she must have been.

Huawa did not linger. She moved in until she hovered before the woman since both the dragon and the woman tugged at her, but the woman's pull ran far stronger. This close, she could still feel a residue of that old might. The face was beautiful.

A red flame pattern set between the brows, and something in that pattern stirred a sense of kinship in her so that she could not help reaching out to touch it. She could feel the woman was a fire elemental spirit like herself, only far more human in seeming, carrying a human air, even in a human shape. Puawa knew perfectly well she herself was no human but a sprite.

A small envy rose in her at the thought. She wished she too could become a true human. The same race as her lord, and how wonderful that would be. As she drifted in that longing, the flame pattern under her fingers suddenly lit and gave off a red glow. Heat bloomed between her own brows as the pattern there began to shine and answer.

The two beams falling across each other. A day swept over her. And when her head cleared, a spirit body had appeared before her, identical to Yom Gira, but far smaller, only about 1 meter 70, roughly her own height. The sight of it startled Huawa, and she pulled back fast, watching the newcomer wearily. For her part, Yomjira came to in bewilderment.

She remembered only her long battle with the dragon Chino Finn across countless places, and how it had ended when she chased him into a volcano deep beneath the sea. There she had burned out her last potential to kill him and died of the exhaustion herself. So how did she have awareness again now?

She took in her surroundings, gathered her wits by degrees, and let her gaze settle at last on the little one in front of her. The girl's weary look drew a faint smile from Yomira. She understood already why she had consciousness again. Because she had met a fellow of her clan, and an awakened one at that.

The element sprite clan carried a special inheritance in which awakened kin could pass their legacy down through the patterns of the clan. She had died. Yet the clan pattern had kept a threat of her true spirit. And now, meeting an awakened clan sister. The patterns had sensed one another and roused that threat awake. She was fortunate indeed to run across one of her own down here.

Still, looking the girl over, she seemed not to have fully awakened, likely for one of enough energy, so the rousing could only be temporary. The girl's level was low, too, a mere level seven. For all that she was plainly a legendary hero, her attributes ran slightly under others of her tier, which struck Yongira as genuinely odd.

Yungira told the little one gently not to be afraid, that she meant no harm and was only a sliver of a true spirit that would scatter on its own before long. " Huawa chattered and gestured back at her since she could not yet speak a human tongue and feared the woman would not follow. Yumjira asked what she was being thanked for, then answered her own question.

Her true spirit had roused, she said, and her inheritance could be handed down now instead of lost. Huawa asked in surprise whether the woman could really understand her since until now only Harui had ever managed it. And this made the second. Yom Jira nodded and said that as clanqin, they naturally understood each other.

Huawa asked whether the inheritance she spoke of was the very thing that had been pulling at her, and Yomira nodded. As a fire elemental spirit, she held a huge draw over her own kind, even in a human body. Huawa was delighted and told her to hand it over quickly since her lord was still waiting for her up above.

At the word Lord Yong Gira frowned and her expression went cold. Because of their inborn closeness to the elements, most of the element sprite clan were born with great talent. All manner of races would steal their young and force them to acknowledge a lord, a thing the clan could never fully guard against.

It was precisely for that reason that whenever they came upon those who had stolen their clan's children, they answered with thunderous vengeance. This very chino fen she had hunted down was one such thief. He had plundered a group of the clans young and sold them off, which was why she had chased him to the ends of the sea.

She had never imagined that this young clan sister who had roused her would herself be one of the enslaved. This situation made her very uncomfortable. Deep under the volcano lay a chamber of old heat and settling ash. And there, Huawa faced a spirit body that was slowly coming apart. The spirit belonged to Yongiraa, an ancestral fire elemental spirit.

And a moment ago, she had been ready to hunt down the lord who owned Huawa and kill him herself. When Huawa spoke of that lord with such open warmth, the elers's face shifted, and Huawa read the shift as scorn that would not stand. In Huawa's heart, her lord was good to her, kind and strong both, and she said so without hesitation.

Yomira only grew more annoyed and told her plainly that the Lord was simply enslaving her. Huawa puffed her cheeks and refused the idea outright. The Lord always gave her good food, and everything she had become had come from him. Yung Gira knew the little one's name without being told. Since reading another's information was nothing for a spirit of her rank, a strength that had once stood near level 29.

Yet, the child would not stop defending her master. So, Huawa launched into the whole story instead. She recounted every moment between herself and her lord from the day they met, exaggerating shamelessly and praising him at every turn. Yumira was stunned by the tale. She had not expected Huawa to be a summoned spirit rather than one bought from a slave market.

She knew of novice lords the method Shian Mo used, though she had never lived it herself. Born as she was inside a territory and raised early for her talent, summoned elemental spirits she understood, gave themselves willingly and worked in concert with Chianmo. So her hostility toward Huawa's lord drained away. Still, the child was so young that the choice could only have been her parents, not her own.

By the child's own telling, the lord had treated her well, lifting the quality of her talent and letting her change profession into a hero. Two full transformations in barely half a month left even Yom Gira envious, for she herself had once been mere purple quality and had clawed her way up step by step through nothing but effort.

This lord was no small figure either, gathering so many fine things in only 3 weeks. Remarkable luck, she thought, and the last of her resentment turned to something closer to wonder. She admitted at last that she had misjudged the Lord. Huawa nodded eagerly, glad her defense had not been wasted. Then the elder spirit said she would pass on her inheritance and help the child fully awaken.

And she said it quickly because she could feel her true spirit beginning to fail. Huawa agreed at once, obedient, asking only what she needed to do. Yongiraa told her to hold still and leave everything to her. The little one floated motionless and ready, and the sight pulled a faint smile from the dying spirit.

The child was genuinely endearing, even if a face that sweet paired oddly with a body already near 1 and 3/4 m tall. Yumira folded her hands into a complex seal. One hand touched the clan pattern on the brow of her own corpse, and the other touched the matching pattern on Huawa's forehead.

A beam of light drew out of each pattern, and the two met before her spirit body and gathered into a golden sphere. Flame runes swimming and turning inside it. As the working went on, her spirit dimmed from solid to faint, the cost of it plain to see.

At last, she drew her hands back, and the golden sphere slid along the light toward Huawa, shrinking as it went until it sank into the child's forehead. Huawwa felt knowledge and skill flood her mind, though most of the skills set locked, as if something blocked them. The elder explained that the gift held her own experience and techniques. She had sealed most of it so the child would not be overwhelmed, and each skill would open only as her strength grew. Handing everything to one so low-levelled at once would only harm her, whereas a seal would make the little one work, climb, and master each piece in turn.

"Now the inheritance was done," she said. And the full Awakening came next. Yumjira shook the fantasy loose. After a full awakening, the child could only shift into a fire marshall god state at will, and that state burned through stamina and mana too fast to hold for long. Seeing Huawa's face fall, she softened and promised that even without staying so tall, the girl would end up much taller than before. That lifted Huawa's mood in an instant.

The child begged to be awakened quickly and asked what she had to do. Young Gira told her not to rush since one thing had to come first. A level 30 dragon corpse lay here with a beast core inside. And by absorbing it, the child would master a second flame and thicken her foundation. Beyond that, she would need to take in Yumira's own core and master her flame as well.

Between those two and her native fire, she would command three flames. And after awakening, she could hold three combat forms. Counting her ordinary shape that made four. Even among the elemental spirit clan, three combat forms were almost unheard of, and the thought left the old spirit openly eager. Even so, regret moved through Yomira. She would scatter completely before she could see what Huawa became once she matured.

She regretted too that the child already had a human lord, since a free Huah might have grown into another pillar for the elemental spirit clan. Still under the regret ran a quiet pride that she could raise such a prodigy with her last strength, and the thought drew a faint smile across her fading face. She told Huawa to hold still and watch.

The child obeyed without a flicker of doubt, since to her this sister carried no ill will, only care. Yomira looked at the long spear still gripped in her corpse's hand, and nostalgia and reluctance moved through her eyes together. Holding the spear, she rose above the dead dragon and drove the point into his skull. The weapon extended, dug deeper, then twisted and drew out a dark red beast core. She brought it before Huawa, and blood red flame bloomed in her hand and scorched the core until it melted and turned a black flame. The working thinned her spirit further, and she paid it no mind.

She held the fire out and warned the child that absorbing it would hurt badly, that she had to endure. Huawa did not take it right away and asked with a sad face whether the sister was all right. Yomira only smiled and told her not to worry, to hurry and absorb it.

The child said no more, took the black flame and guided it to the tribal pattern on her forehead, the way the new knowledge told her. The flame sank in without resistance. Then the pain came. As it entered, a burning agony bloomed, and Huawa's small face crumpled, though she bit down and let only muffled groans escape.

The pain spread from her head and rolled downward until it wrapped her whole body, sharpening as it went, and her frame shuttered in waves. She held one thought like a handrail. She would not give up. She would grow stronger, grow taller, whatever it cost. As she watched, the elder felt a pain of her own, yet her face stayed level.

This was a trial the child had to pass, and passing it young was far kinder than passing it late. There was something moving in it, too. So young and the child endured such pain without a cry. Proof of a will far stronger than her size. So long as she did not die early, the child would climb far higher than Yaungira ever had.

Yomira moved above Chino Fenigan and formed a seal, and blood red threads spread down and settled over the dragon's body. Both flames settling perfectly. Now the awakening," she said. But Huawa looked at the nearly transparent spirit and asked whether once the awakening was done, her flame sister would fade and never be seen again. The child, not knowing the elers's thoughts, was already searching. Soon she found a platform in her sea of consciousness, but not the one described. It was no red circle with one pillar.

When the description came, even her long experience left her dumbruck, half convinced the little one was a child of destiny. Her luck simply beyond reason. Not only had the child woken the spirit planting platform, she had woken a three-colored one, a treasure among the elemental spirit clan. The reason the talent counted as strategic was plain.

When an elemental spirit died, a trace of its true spirit lingered, and one who held the spirit planting platform could house those remnants on the element pillars. There, the lingering spirits drank energy from the hollow at the center, the energy pool, and slowly recovered. They would never reclaim their old power. Yet, they could begin again, keeping the talent, quality, and memories of the life before.

To preserve highquality members this way was in effect to preserve the clan's top combat power, which was why the gift earned its name. That alone would not have shaken Yung Giraso, for she had met bearsers of the spirit planting platform before. What stunned her was the three colors and three pillars, when one pillar alone meant the power to seat a single spirit.

Three pillars meant three spirits. The red fire pillar could seat a fire elemental spirit, the blue water pillar a water spirit, the black dark pillar a dark spirit, the black dark pillar a dark spirit. Yomira understood exactly what that meant, and it was why the word prodigy kept surfacing, why she kept thinking child of destiny, she laid the whole wonder of it out for Huawa.

Excited both by the discovery and by what it meant for her, that she might survive. The child was to spend energy on her own strength first and not stall herself for the elder's sake. When she was strong, then she could help, and the help would count for twice as much. Huh. She rose fast toward the surface, unable to wait to see her lord's face when he saw what she had become. Up at the crater, the weight had begun to gnaw at me. I stood at the crater and looked down again and again, waiting for a familiar shape. When my eyes fell to the lava below. One more time, my gaze sharpened.
