Dark Moon said it looked like Huawa, and even with the changes on her, we all knew her. She broke the surface of the lava, rose free of it, and came to us. The moment she landed, she threw herself into my arms and called out for her Lord in that sweet voice. I stroked her hair on reflex. It had gone blood red.

Halfway through the motion, my hand stopped and I took her by the shoulders and looked at her face. And then I asked, startled, whether she could talk. She nodded and told me proudly she had fully awakened, that she had grown taller, too, and that she would stay this height from now on. I laughed, glad, because it was plain she had gained a great deal down there.

Windsor and the others laughed with me. Ha. Rui and Di kept circling her. Curious, I asked nothing more for the moment, and we started down the mountain together, cutting through the plant sea monsters that came at us on the way.

Back on the ship, we hunted plant sea monsters again, driving the tower ship through them, and I never turned down a chance to raise a stat point when the tower ship's diving time ran out. We began the climb back. I had meant to travel this stretch of sea, but plans bend the circumstance, and the strange volcano had held us in place without much progress.

Even so, I felt no regret. Everyone's attributes had risen, and Huawa's fortune below had made her far stronger. The moment the towers ship surfaced, I felt the fury of the wind and the waves. Nothing like the calm below. I told Liuk the only priority now was the towers ship's safety and nothing else, and he answered at once and went to his post.

I had Windsor take Huawa and the little ones back to the core cabin since we were not traveling anyway and they could play there. I stayed on deck and laid a shield over myself so the wind would not trouble me. I took out my resource fishing rod since I had not spent today's chance yet and did not care to waste it.

Maybe luck would break my way and pull up a golden legendary. I cast. In a moment, the first item came up and I checked it. A blood fusion agent, blue quality, the sort of material a demon pattern master needed to fuse blood together. That mattered to me. My own dragon blood needed a blood fusion agent to merge.

And my attributes had already climbed past 50, meeting the requirement. But I had never had the agent in hand. So, the two portions of dragon blood had sat idle. I took the dragon blood out and tried the agent on it, and nothing happened. The quality was simply too low. It stung a little, though I had braced for it.

The agent was still a blue item I could feed to 100 transformations, and maybe it would come back gold. I put it away and cast again. The second item was a reconnaissance watchtower blueprint. Green, a structure raised around a territory to warn of enemies drawing near and cut the danger. Not bad, but only one, which limited how useful it could be.

A whole ring of them around a territory would let me see trouble coming from any direction, and that would be a joy. One was better than none, though, and it might find a use somewhere. The third item was an infantry rhinoceros full armor manufacturing blueprint. Green teachable only to a blacksmith.

Lower quality again, but I was content since it was an equipment blueprint, and equipment is a treasure in any world, whether for my own use, for arming troops, or for selling. With the items stowed, I headed for the core cabin since I still had not heard the whole of what Huawa meant under the volcano. I pushed the door open and the sight nearly gave me a nose bleed.

Windsor and Huawa were playing while Dark Moon got caught in it now and then. Huawa wore her fiery red dress and Windsor and Dark Moon wore silk sleepwear, purple and black. The three of them tumbled about, flashing more skin than was fair, and Ha Rui and the little fox dowi had tucked into a corner, wanting no part of it.

I coughed and the whole room went still and turned to me. Windsor and Dark Moon straightened their clothes in a hurry while Huawa and Haui rushed over and I caught them both and sat down by the bed. I let Windsor and Dark Moon be elawa and asked her to tell me what she had met beneath the volcano and why it had kept her so long.

She launched straight into it, chattering out everything, and the rest of us sighed at the turns of her story as it went. I told her plainly that her luck was extraordinary and her flame sister kinder still and that when she was strong enough one day she had to restore her.

Huawa nodded over and over promising she would and then added that her flame sister had told her not to waste time or resources on it now but to build her own strength first. I agreed since Yomira was right and I would have said the same anyway. Sharpening the knife never delays the chopping of wood. Then she took a jade bottle from her storage ring and gave it to me.

A gift from her flame sister. I checked it and found the essence blood of the dead dragon Chino Fen. It was a golden demon flame twinwing dragon emperor blood. One that would grant its bloodline once consumed, though only after every basic attribute crossed 50. That made a third kind of dragon emperor blood in my hands.

I was pleased and vexed at once, glad for another golden portion, sore that I still had not even sorted the blood fusion agent to merge the first two. If anyone knew my situation, they would call me insufferable, and I would not blame them. I put the dragon blood away for later.

Then I checked the space ring Yong Gira had left with Huawa, and it held only some element gems and a little purple crystal, nothing more. I checked the long spear, too. It was a bloodthirsty demon spear, golden, lifting the wielder strength by 40%, and it carried a passive life steal that drank an enemy's blood after every wound.

I raised my eyebrows since a spear like that was a fine thing, and I handed it back to Hawa. She slid it into her storage ring and put the ring on her hand. Then Huawa squirmed in my arms and told me all sweetness that she wanted to sleep beside me the way Sister Windsor did. I had no words.

The little Vixen. I glanced at Windsor, whose face went red as she muttered that it was all my fault, and I had no words for that either since she had been happy enough at the time. I rubbed Huawa's head and told her that was grown-up business and had nothing to do with a child.

She huffed that she was no child in this, that her flame sister had taught her all about it, and my eyes went wide. Yo, Gira, you truly will teach anything. I pinched Huawa's cheek and told her firmly to behave and stop this. The truth was I could not bring myself to do anything with her. This morning she had still been a little sprout and now she was a lovely young woman.

I had spent so long treating her as a little sister that my mind simply could not turn the corner. She did not like it, but one look at my serious face and she held her tongue, her small face pulling into a sulk. She snorted that her lord was mean and she would ignore me, then slipped out of my arms and crossed to Windsor.

Windsor gathered her in and could not help laughing at me. I shot Windsor a look, thinking she was far too pleased to watch me squirm. And then I caught the faint smile at the corner of Dark Moon's mouth as well. In my head, I promised the two of them they would both pay for it that night. I told them to keep playing and went out to sit for a while.

On the sofa, I called my over to work the knots out of my shoulders, and I opened the chat channel. The waves had thrown the whole sea into a panic, and the wall of messages showed it. People wailed that their boats were tossing at the mercy of the storm.

Some had already given up and were living it up on their last supplies, while a stubborn few shouted that anyone who held on still had a chance. One player bragged from a small island he had settled safe from the swells, saying he had given up on lordship to fish and live quietly.

The channel swung between envy and mockery, then circled back to the same vow that they would survive and build their territories first. I scrolled the endless refresh and turned it over in my mind. The disaster had gutted most people and driven many to the edge of surrender. I was sure the disasters would only grow and that more and more would be cut down as they did.

What surprised me were the ones settling on islands too small for a territory, meaning to give up lordship and live at ease. I was not hopeful for them. This was a world of lords, and they were still novice lords without a territory, no different from anyone at the start.

When others founded territories and grew strong on those resources, and the spirit and demon realms went to war again, these idol ones would still be standing where they began. Their fate would need no spelling out. I watched the channel a while longer, dropping in a word here and there, and every time I appeared, it set off a fresh round of clamor.

Evening came before I noticed. After dinner, I settled into my hundred transformations for the day. And this time, I meant to work on things other than equipment. I took out the first item, the blue blood fusion agent, fixed it as the target, and called the change in silence. When I checked what sat in my hand, it was a bloodline evolution potion, gold, a brew that pushed a bloodline to evolve.

Though the higher the bloodline's quality, the weaker its effect. It was a small glass tube of shimmering purple potion flecked with light. I was surprised and a little let down. Surprised because I had scored a critical hit again, leveling twice into a gold item. Let down because the critical had not given me the gold blood fusion agent I actually wanted.

Still, an agent would let me merge my three golden dragon bloods and drink them, and then this potion might drive the result past gold entirely. The thought alone was worth savoring. I put the potion away and took out an item from the treasure hunt. A beautiful magic mirror, blue, which showed you whatever face you imagined and fed your fantasies.

The description left me speechless. What a wretched little prop. Pure selfdeception. A way to bury your head in the sand and believe you were beautiful while everyone else somehow saw it, too. All it would ever do was rot a person's honest sense of their own face.

It was no different from the beauty filters back on Blue Star, only more powerful and more twisted, useless to me, which meant it was perfect for a transformation. I fixed it and called the change, and out came a thousand transformation mask, purple, that let the wearer shift appearance at will.

The mask and the mirror were clearly cut from the same cloth, both able to change a face, except the mask outclassed the mirror by a mile. The mirror only fooled its owner, while the mask fooled everyone once it was worn, and it had real practical uses. Besides, it let me shape a new face for any errand where I would rather not be recognized.

That interested me a great deal. I set the mask on my face and pictured Shakpan and it flowed over my skin like water. When everything went still, I looked in the mirror and the man there was Shakpan to the life. I was very pleased with it. My eyes wandered. A mischievous grin tugged at my mouth and I shaped the mask into something new.

Then went to the bedroom. When I opened the door, only Windsor and Dark Moon were inside since Hui, Huawa, and Dowi had wandered off to other rooms. They both turned toward the door, and the waiting face startled them. It startled Windsor most of all, since it was her own face looking back, only the hair different. I grinned in triumph and asked how it looked, whether it passed for me.

Windsor caught my voice and marveled that it was so alike she could not tell the difference herself. I peeled the mask off and tossed it to her, telling the two of them to play with it. And once she caught it, they forgot me entirely and set to amusing themselves. I did not mind. I still had one item left to transform.

I took out the third, something I had picked up from Wang Mock's trade list, an automatic toothbrush, blue, which vibrated once you slotted a spirit crystal, and gave a wonderfully thorough clean. Truly, the world was wide and nothing in it too strange.

I fixed it and called the change, and what came back was a purple pleasure toy, a small thing that boasted it could take a thousand shapes and hold anything a person might want. I blurted out a curse at the sight of it. It was a clear cube, and I had no idea how it changed until I idily wished, and the cube in my hand turned into a cat's tail.

I put it away without a word, tucked into a corner of my spatial ring. That used up the day's 300 transformations, and I was satisfied since every one of them had proven useful. I pushed the bedroom door open and stopped short. Both women inside now wore dark moon's face, which meant Windsor had put on the mask.

I smiled, drew the cat's tail out of my spatial ring, and thought I would let Windsor help me judge its effect. After breakfast the next morning, I laid a shield over myself and went to the deck for the day's fishing. The first item came up quickly, a jade clear flute, green, that raised the wielder strength by a tenth.

It surprised me a little since I had not known this world held such an instrument, and I could not guess which profession would use it. It was no good to me, so I put it away and kept fishing. This cast took longer, more than half an hour, before the second item finally bit. It was a white jade pendant set with a red vermilion bird, a finel looking thing.

And when I checked it, my breath caught a Vermilion Bird Jade Pendant, purple, carrying a one-time skill called Vermilion Bird Descent, that could deal real harm to a level 24 creature. It was also marked as a token of the Vermilion Bird Clan. Good for asking them a single favor. I could hardly believe my eyes.

Fortune had blessed me again, pulling up a purple item, one that could wound a level 24 creature and stand in for an extra life at a desperate moment. Better still, the token could summon a favor from a whole clan, which was worth far more than the one-time skill. I put the pendant away and decided against feeding it to 100 transformations.

A transformation would surely give me a golden item, but a golden item might not prove all that useful. This pendant, by contrast, could open a real connection with the Vermillion Bird Clan and even earn their goodwill. Golden items I could always make later from purple ones, but the friendship of a clan was worth far more than any single golden piece.

I set it aside and cast for the third item. My mind calm now since two good pulls in a row already felt like a stretch. The third came up soon and it was an egg nearly half a meter tall. The shell was gray white like stone scrolled all over with mismatched colored patterns, a perfect chaos of them.

I checked it and read that it was a chaos heaven strange beast egg golden able to hatch a naturally born exotic beast of the same name. A creature the note called limitless in potential. I stared at it stunned. Fortune had possessed me twice in a row, and this pull outdid the last by far. An actual golden beast egg, a naturally born exotic beast, and limitless potential.

Either one alone was outrageous. And this chaos heaven strange beast carried both at once. There was no imagining how terrifying it would grow. Exciting as the egg was, it set a problem in front of me at the same time. The problem was simple. I had no idea how to hatch a naturally born exotic beast and coax it out of its shell.

I ran a hand over the chaos heaven strange beast egg and the shell was rough, exactly like stone. I crooked a finger and wrapped it and it was hard. There was no solving it just then, so I put the egg away for the time being. Back in the core cabin, the others were playing cards, and with nothing better to do, I joined them.

The waves were rougher today. The tower ship lurched now and then, and Lee Ruk had already reported that it had slipped past his control and could only drift with the current, that forcing it might damage the hole. I told him not to push it and just keep the tower ship safe. Aside from an afternoon of hunting at sea, the whole day went to playing with Windsor and the rest.

What left me speechless was that Huawa was a different creature now, no longer the innocent little sprout. She spent the whole day burrowing into my arms and finding excuses to touch, and it wore on me to no end. More than once, I cursed Yomira in my head, grumbling that she must have been some kind of witch. If Yomira were awake to hear it, she would have sworn she was wronged.

She had passed that knowledge to Huawa out of worry that I might make a move on the child so the girl would understand such things and guard herself. Instead of using it to keep her distance, she was turning it into a siege against me. And had the old spirit seen it, she would have coughed blood.

That evening, after dinner, I took out the chaos heaven strange beast egg and set it in a corner of the living room. With no way to deal with it yet, it would serve as an ornament for now. Then I sat down on the sofa and began the evening's hundred transformations. The first item was a triangular manatee beast core blue one I had gotten while hunting that afternoon.

A level 10 blue sea monster dropping a beast core had been a lucky thing. This was my first time running a beast core through 100 transformations. So I set no restrictions and chose a random change. Curious what might come of it. A statue appeared in my hand. A cyan sheep with a purple ring marked at the center of its brow.

I checked it and found an Azure sheeperd god statue purple that raised a territory sheep production by 40% when placed in a building like the holy temple. I was glad to have it since anything that lifted the output of food and the like was welcome. Food was the cornerstone of everything and without it the rest was a castle built on air.

I set the statue aside and moved to the second item, a blue scroll of demon vine entanglement, a magic scroll that cast a strong single target bind and some damage. I had picked it up long ago in the Merur folk territory and never used it since a scroll like that could save a life at the right moment.

But that morning, I had fished up the Vermilion Bird Jade Pendant, whose Vermillion Bird descent did much the same work and hit harder. So, I fed the scroll to 100 transformations to see if it would give me something new. I left the type open again and called a random change. A purple skill stone showed up in my hand, and Joy jumped in me since skill stones were excellent.

I checked it and read Angel of Light. Purple. A skill that once released could summon a single powerful angel of light or a whole flock of weaker ones learnable by a light element profession. My grin widened. Only yesterday I had been thinking Windsor lacked an area attack. And today an area skill stone dropped into my hands like a pillow arriving the instant I felt drowsy.

By the skills description, the angel of light worked much like my own mystic ice puppet. Both being summons. The difference was that the ice puppets held a steady strength and scaled only with how much mana I had. The angel of light was more flexible, letting the caster raise one strong angel or a swarm of weaker ones, matching the means to the enemy at hand.

I carried the skill stone to the bedroom and gave it to Windsor, who lit up the moment she saw it, since she knew her own gap as well as I did. With the skill, she would climb another step in strength, and in her delight, she leaned in and kissed me a few times.

I went back to the living room and took out the third item, a spirit crystal the size of a thumb pad. I was curious what a spirit crystal might become. So, I left the type open once more and called a random change. What appeared in my palm caught me off guard since it was a small green elf.

She was only palm size with pointed ears and no wings, dressed in a jade green gown, and she sat in my hand and smiled up at me sweetly. I was shaken looking at her. A spirit crystal had turned into a living elf through 100 transformations, which was more than I had ever expected. I checked her and read a wood elf epic at level two.

Every stat sitting at 24, her magic at 240, her profession hero and forest archer. Her skills were 10 bead volley and vine entangling arrow and flower burst arrow. She carried no equipment and her talent was crystal seeker. I was dumbruck. I had never imagined this palmsized creature was a hero and an epic grade one at that.

What startled me more was the talent, since among everyone on my ship, only Huawa and I held one, and Huawa had only gained hers the day before. Yet, here was another small creature with a talent, a wood elf. Her gift, the crystal seeker. The moment I saw the crystal seeker talent, my first thought was that it could hunt for spirit crystals, and a wave of joy rolled through me.

If the talent worked the way I guessed, riches were about to fall into my lap. From now on, this little one could search out spirit crystal veins for me. And the value of spirit crystals needs no explaining. A few veins found in mind would give me energy to spend or goods to sell. The thought curved my mouth into a smile.

I looked at the little elf in my palm, more and more pleased, and reached out a finger to poke her head. In high spirits, I even put a little thought into a name and told her that from now on she would be called Ha Chong Day. She took to it at once, a warm smile spreading over her tiny face, and she hugged my finger and kissed it with her small mouth.

Then she thanked me and said the name sounded lovely in a clear little voice. I looked at her startled. What was going on? A creature the size of my palm could talk while Hui, so much bigger, could not. It left me confused. She called me Lord, which put her in the same place as Huawa and the others.

A pet. Yet, she had carried hero status from the instant she appeared, which no pet should, and she showed no loyalty or affinity readout at all. I could not make sense of it. It seemed running 100 transformations on a spirit crystal had created some strange new creature. Still, whatever she was, it was no bad thing, and I could feel her attachment to me plainly.

I told her that as long as she was happy, we would go, that I would take her to meet the others. I set the little one on my head and went to find Huawa and the rest. Hachiong Day lay in my hair, gripping a fistful of it, giggling without pause. I brought her before Huawa and Harui, had them introduce themselves to one another, and left.

Back in my room, I looked at Windsor and Dark Moon, undressed, and went to bed to rest. It was a night to remember. The following morning after breakfast, I came out to the deck, one hand pressed to my lower back. It was Saturday, the last day of the week. The sky clear and the sea flat and calm without a breath of wind.

I rubbed my waist since Windsor and Dark Moon had let their guard down. They had only grown more eager, and I was starting to feel I could not keep up. If a few more join them down the line, the strain would wear me to the bone. More and more, I wanted the blood fusion agent so I could consume the dragon blood, take a bloodline, and strengthen my body.

Since the Dragon Clan's frame was first rate. I waved Lee Ruk over from the sails and asked whether we could still find that island. He answered a little reluctant that the island was still in sight, but a full 10 days off. I smiled and told him not to worry and to come with me, then walked to the bow.

He followed, puzzled and curious, while Windsor and Dark Moon rose from their lounge chairs and came to my side. I smiled and took out an item, a trajectory fixed point teleportation map, single use, that recorded the user's path and could return them to any point along it once and only once. I had activated it the moment I got it, so my whole route sat recorded inside.

When Luke made his report that day, I had remembered this map, which was why I never panicked. I picked the point nearest the island and used it. I watched ahead, nervous that it might teleport only one person, since that would have left me weeping. The odds were small, though, since the description had set no limit on the size of what could pass.

Sure enough, a blue teleportation gate opened before the tower ship, so wide the whole vessel could sail through with room to spare. Everyone stared at it, dumbruck, plainly, not expecting me to have such a method. Lee Rrook asked what it was, and Windsor and Dark Moon looked at me with the same question.

The size of the gate came as a real relief since a oneperson gate would have made the whole scene painfully awkward to watch. I explained that the gate could carry us back to any point along the tower ship's earlier path and that I had chosen the spot closest to the island.

Leuk marveled aloud that it was incredible, then broke into open joy, saying that if so, we would reach the island in a day or two. I nodded and steered the tower ship into the gate. Passing through felt the same as it had during the two domain competition before. The tower ship sailed out the far side, and when I looked back, the gate at the stern was slowly closing.

I did not simply glance and move on. I turned to Lee Ruk and told him we would not fish now, that we would sail without stopping day and night. Ordinary rods rarely caught anything while the tower ship was moving. So I used to hold still through the day to gather white stone and keep fishing, but that no longer mattered.

Le understood, his voice firm and edged with excitement. I waved him off to his work and we settled onto the lounge chairs where I took out my resource fishing rod and cast for the day. I asked Windsor, lying to my right, where Huawa and the others had gone.

She said they were playing in the room and that ever since Huawa awakened, she had grown more and more mischievous, like an older sister now, likely from the influence of Yomira<unk>'s memories. Among the little ones, she plainly ranked herself the boss and had gathered her own small crew. What left me more helpless still was that Huawa now had her eye on me, forever trying to push me down.

That was the thing I truly struggled with. My mind had not made the shift, and I still did not see Huawa as a woman, while she plainly saw me as a man and kept up the teasing without pause. What made it harder was that she was genuinely alluring, and part of me had begun to want to keep my distance from her.

Out loud, I only said it was fine, that her strength was more than enough to play the big sister, and that they could amuse themselves while I finally got some peace. Windsor and Dark Moon laughed at that since they knew my recent troubles well enough. Just then, the first item surfaced, and I checked it.

A waste disposal site blueprint, green, the sort of facility no large territory could do without since a territory that never handled its waste was no territory at all but a garbage heap. Another building blueprint, not high in quality, but genuinely important. Back on blue star, waste disposal had been a serious matter in every big city, and with this in hand, my territory could round out and improve a little further.

I put it away, cast the rod, and waited for the second item to bite. Then a burst of chatter came from across the deck. Huawa led the way. One and a half meters tall in her fiery red dress. Behind her flew Hui while Dowi ran along the ground with the wood elf Chong Day perched on her head.

The whole troop bore down on me in grand style with Huawa at the front and a full head of momentum. She reached me, straddled my lap without a word, and leaned into my chest. With her soft weight against me, and the morning what it was, a fire kindled in me at once, feeling the change in me, she only twisted harder.

I gave her backside a slap and told her to behave, that I was busy. Her body jerked and she went quiet, settling into my arms without further trouble. Her nose sniffing away at me. I could almost hear her thinking her lord's scent was a little intoxicating. Her calm let me breathe again and I looked around. Haoui had landed in Windsor's arms and she was playing with the little one.

Dalgi had dropped into Dark Moon's lap. The wood elf Chung Day was clambering onto my lounge chair. So I smiled and set her up on my head. Soon the second item came up and I checked it in my hand. A commoner summoning card, white, that summoned a white quality commoner when used. I felt nothing much at the site and used it straight away.

A female commoner appeared, and when I checked her, I found she even had a profession, a weaver. I sent her off to report to Sock Pan. Then I pushed away Huawa's head as it crept toward my face, cast the rod, and set to fishing for the third item. Before the third arrived, a message from Shionmo reached me first.

Two announcements at once. The first told me a new week had ended and this week's survival rewards were being handed out to check them carefully. The second told me the week's top 10 would receive extra rewards to choose and claim them in good time. Neither surprised me. I held off on claiming and open the survival points ranking to look it over first.

I held the top spot, my points sitting near 3,000, a good stretch ahead of the rest. Bake held second and Lee Kio third with Yay Bake Pong, James, and Kaidi filling out the next places. Then Isabelle Elizabeth Cook and Jyn Moong down to 10th. I had upgraded nothing this week, yet my points had still climbed a fair amount.

My best guess was that my seawater converter, flower producer, and plant cultivation box had all been turnurning out goods without pause. So, my stockpiles had swelled, which was what pushed the points up. Everyone else's points had risen, too, though not by much. And the reason was plain.

The further along you went, the more whit stone and upgrade demanded, which made it harder to advance technology or ships, and harder for points to climb. There had been one small shuffle in the ranks this time, with Isabelle edging past Elizabeth. I gave it a glance and closed the list. As long as my first place held, no one but Bake Seol and Lee Kio drew my attention.

Both of them my own Chianhe pavilion. Then I turned to the reward. I thought silently of choosing it, and three items appeared before me. Last time I had taken first place 3 weeks running, which let me pick three of five. But this week held no special bonus, so I could choose one of three. I checked each in turn as always, and the moment I read the first, I swore aloud.

I blinked to be sure I had not misread and looked again. A dark domain skill stone, gold, that formed a black domain on release inside which the caster's speed rose by 40%, learnable by a dark element profession, its range set by the caster's strength. I had not expected a gold item this time, and it made me wonder whether all three might be gold.

Instead of settling on the skill stone at once, my eyes ran quickly over the other two. The second was a talent awakening stone, gold, which gave a non-lord a certain chance to wake a talent. The third was a ninetailed demon emperor's heart blood essence, gold, that granted the ninetailed demon emperor's bloodline once consumed. And for a fox race creature, it would raise bloodline purity and cultivation.

Both two in a row, and both gold, exactly as I had guessed. I had not thought a fourth week first place could pick a gold item, and it was a real surprise. The other top 10 had likely seen their rewards rise a tier as well, most of them probably drawing purple this time. I let the thought go and fixed my gaze on the three items in front of me.

Three golden quality items lay in front of me, and I wanted every one of them. The dark domain skill stone, the talent awakening stone, and the ninetailed demon emperor's heart blood essence would all serve me well. The catch was that I could keep only one, so two of them had to be given up. A happy sort of pain that choosing between treasures.

I thought it over calmly. The dark domain skill stone was the obvious prize since dark moon could learn from it directly. Dark domain was a domain skill, and mastering it would blunt the environment's drag on her and lift her from the ground up. The talent awakening stone was meant for those who were not lords, offering a chance to wake a talent.

I knew how fearsome talents could be, yet that stone was a gamble I did not like. Using it guaranteed nothing, and even a talent that woke might turn out weak, so the uncertainty ran too high. The last one, the ninetailed demon emperor's heart blood essence, worked much like the dragon emperor blood I already owned, granting a bloodline to whoever took it.

I carried three kinds of dragon emperor blood, so the essence meant little to me, though it would surely do wonders for Dowi. Dji belonged to the ninetailed race, too, only as a ninetailed charming heart fox. And I had no idea what breed this demon emperor had been. If she swallowed the essence, who knew what surprise might follow?

I ruled out the awakening stone and wavered between the skill stone and the heart blood. In the end, I chose the dark domain skill stone. Dark moon stood higher in level than DGi right now, and the stone would raise her strength at once and sharpen the whole team. The stone was also a sure thing, learned the moment it was used, with no gamble attached.

And Dowi had already reached golden quality, so the heart blood would not overturn her foundation. I did not believe for a second that she could break past golden quality. I handed the skill stone to Dark Moon without hesitation and told her to learn it. She took it and chose to learn it once, no longer standing on ceremony with me since even she counted herself as mine now.

When she was done, she gave me a small nod to say she had mastered it. I smiled since this was exactly what I had expected, and I had opened enough skillstones myself to know there would be no surprises. Then the resource fishing rod trembled, and the third item of the morning arrived. I reeled it in and checked it with my spirit eye.

The first two catches today had been one green quality item and a white quality summon card. Nothing to make my eyes light up. I hope the third would answer me better, but 100 transformations must have spent all my luck because this catch gave me nothing good either. It was a snack gift box, blue quality, which opened into representative snacks from various races.

The last chance had passed with nothing to startle me. Still, it was a blue item, and if I ran 100 transformations on it tonight, I could push it up to purple. I put the rod away since the sea hunt was still a while off and opened the chat channel to see what the day had thrown up.

Yesterday's storm had been an apocalypse and the channel was thick with survivors marveling that they had lived at all. One man said he had eaten his last supplies with his crew as a final meal and now faced a food crisis after all. Another crowed that he had refused to give up, landed safely, and drawn a blue item from even the basic survival reward.

The pattern was clear enough that the rewards had climbed a tier for everyone. Green where there used to be white and blue where there used to be green. Then someone wondered aloud what the first place Big Shot could have drawn, whether it might be a golden legendary item. The channel stalled. Then the messages sped up, everyone begging their number, one player to speak up and show what he had pulled.

A long string of calls scrolled past with my name buried in them. I was a little surprised to be dragged out while lurking, and though I did not mind letting them see, my reward this time had already gone to Dark Moon. My eyes drifted and I decided the spend point reward hardly mattered.

What they wanted was a golden item and it did not have to be that one since I still had a golden item or two lying around. I had nothing else to do so I might as well stir the pot in the world channel. I took out two items to consider. One was a Valhalla blueprint golden quality, a special building where unit heroes left a mark.

After death, they condensed into hero souls who kept all their living strength and could fight in the territo's main city. Though a hero soul's strength could never be raised. The other was Icebound Thousand Miles, a golden magic scroll that unleashed a wide field of frost. In the end, I chose the Valhalla blueprint, took a picture, and posted it in the world channel.

The crowd froze mid-aru, not reacting until someone noticed it had come from me. One player reposted it, then another, and in no time, the whole channel had seen a golden item shared by their big shot. That single move set the feed on fire. Some had never laid eyes on golden quality in their lives and said so. Half in awe and half in envy.

Others marveled that a building could revive troops and heroes, while a cooler head pointed out the heavy limits that strength could not be raised and the hero souls could not leave the main city. A few simply thanked me for letting them glimpse a golden item they might never see again. I read the fuss and laughed since their words were amusing and pleasant to hear.

Huawa was squirming against me, so I patted her little backside and told her to stop wiggling before she made me forget my manners. She only grinned and grew a touch bolder. And by now I was completely helpless against her. Time slid on to noon. After lunch, I switched on the diving technology and took the tower ship down to begin the day's hunt.

The whole run went smoothly without a single accident, and my luck held, dropping a few interesting items along the way. Once we surfaced, I ate dinner, sent Huawa and the others off to other rooms and settled in for tonight's hundred transformations. The first item was something I had hunted from the sea that afternoon, a black robe patterned with blue clouds and hemmed at the cuffs with golden lace.

Its information named it a golden silk cloud pattern robe. Blue quality the work of master Taylor Kachi Carlo worn to raise the wearer's charm. Charm did nothing for me. I was not looking to seduce anyone and the girls already around me were more than enough to look after with no need to go picking flowers outside.

So I ran a random hund transformations without specifying a type and the robe became a magic pattern wardrobe of purple quality. Master carpenter Ronovs and Master Magic patterner Caderolon had crafted it to dust itself, fold clothes, and clear odors. I looked at that and felt thoroughly let down, since a piece of furniture did me no good at this stage.

If I ever grew into a powerful lord, a fine wardrobe might add taste to my rooms. But I was still building for nothing, and a purple hero or troop nest would have served me far better. Anyone who overheard me complain would have wanted to throttle me for whining over a treasure most would kill for. I put it away anyway since there was nothing else to be done.

The second item was also from the afternoon hunt. A white round pill marked with blue patterns, blue quality, a mystic origin treasure healing pill that mended injuries. This time I specified the same type before running 100 transformations. What appeared was still a pill, but its face had shifted from white and blue to red and purple, richer to look at.

It was a purple dragon blood essence pill, purple quality. It stirred the body's potential for 1 hour and erupted with triple combat power, after which the user fell into weakness or even lost their life. The side effects were brutal. Death included. Yet, this was a genuine trump card for saving my own skin.

Triple combat power was terrifying enough since 100 points of attributes would become 300 the moment I swallowed it, which was close to invincible. I slid it into my storage ring beside the magic scrolls. Since these were all last stitch things for the worst moments, the scrolls in this pill gave me a fair spread of lifelines. Now, those lifelines settled something in me and lifted my sense of safety.

After the pill went away, I took out the third item, the snack gift box I had fished up that morning and saved for tonight. A blue item could turn purple with a little luck, so I had kept it back on purpose. I did not specify a type again since a random transformation was riskier, but far likelier to throw up something with a startling effect.

I chanted 100 transformations in silence, and the box flashed into a black cube a meter to a side with a round hole half a meter wide cut into the top. It read as a random food box, purple quality, which produced food of purple quality or lower once a day in no fixed amount. I nearly laughed at how useless it looked since I did not lack for food.

Then I caught the sense of it that a one-time snack had become a source of steady supply. Of today's three, only the purple dragon blood essence pill had truly pleased me, but a food box was still a food box. I set it beside the chaos heavenly exotic beast egg, and reached my hand down through the hole, curious what it would give.

The day's portion had not been used, and I saw no reason to waste it. My fingers found a plate, which I drew out, a silver white dish holding two yellow fruits shaped rather like pears. Two fruits struck me as a stingy spread to call food, but I picked one up and bit into it, and the taste was genuinely good, juicy, and sweet.

I finished it in no time, then felt my attributes tick up ever so slightly. I took it for a trick of the mind until I checked my personal information and found the rise was real. The remaining fruit read as a spirit origin water pear fruit green quality a heavenly and earthly treasure that randomly raised basic attributes by three or four points on eating.

I stared at that then back at the box hardly believing it could produce heavenly treasures at all. This suddenly made the random food box very valuable. The description promised food up to purple quality which meant a purple quality treasure was not impossible and even a slim chance was still a chance. It was another road to heavenly treasures opening under my feet and I had made a huge profit.

I ate the second fruit on the sofa and gained another three points. My one regret was that it had to be steeped in origin essence since a raw one would have burst into airflow and vanished before I ever tasted it. Dawn came and I got up, washed, and went to check on the mermaid.

She had stopped growing since the growth liquid inside was nearly spent, enough now to keep her alive, but not to develop her further. I was not worried because the remaining liquid could sustain her for a long while, and once I reached the island, I could build a mermaid growth pool and settle the matter for good.

Then I went to the chaos heavenly exotic beast egg and ran a hand over its shell, wondering how to hatch it. Ordinary eggs woke with warmth, though I had no idea whether that held for an innate strange beast egg. So I decided to have Huawa roasted gently and see. An egg this tough was hardly going to cook.

I ate breakfast trading jokes with Windsor and the others, and we walked out of the core cabin together onto the deck. The day was clear, but cool rather than hot. I found Huawa, told her my plan for the egg and warned her to raise the heat slowly and watch for changes. She had no objection, only a demand, a kiss on the cheek.

I gave up in the end and kissed her cheek, which was startlingly soft, and she bounced off delighted. Then I sat on the lounge chair as usual, took out the resource fishing rod, and started the day's fishing. The first item came quickly, a palms-sized silver white box holding a silver white token.

The face carried a red character for command and the back showed a black castle flanked by blue plum blossoms with blood red centers. My spirit I named it a shadow fortress plum blossom token blue quality. The shadow fortress sat on shadow island under the shadow emperor and ran an assassination trade in a blue token could command one general level assassin to act once.

I was quietly surprised that this world held organizations built on killing for hire. Still, the token did not impress me since the general level sat between level 13 and level 18. And even there, a blue general and a golden legendary general were worlds apart. With as many golden heroes as I kept, a general level blue token was nothing to me.

I decided it was better to run 100 transformations on it and chase a purple item, specifying a special type first, since a token this odd might reward me with something fine. I put it away and waited for the second bite, which did not take long. The second catch was a small cage, its sides a hand span across, built of thin silver rods with a little golden hook at the top.

What threw me was that it had no door at all, and I could not see how so small a cage was meant to be used. My spirit eye set me straight. It was a beast cage, gold quality. Once I dripped blood to claim it, it could capture any monster or sea beast three levels below me whose quality did not exceed my own.

The target unable to resist, one at a time. That set my heart racing since I had not expected a gold item. My second ever from the resource fishing rod. The function was frankly insane. Anything three levels under me and no higher in quality could be caught with total certainty. No resistance allowed.

If my strength ever climbed to the ceiling of level 30, seizing a level 27 gold quality monster outright would be simple, and taming it afterward would hand me high-end combat power for free. I was more than satisfied and dripped blood at once to bind it to me. Then I dropped the line again, and my racing pulse slowly eased.

The third item was a white quality commoner summoning card, which I barely glanced at after a gold catch. I summoned the commoner and sent them off to Siok Pan since all the commoners answered to him now and he was handling them well.

With a day's fishing done, I put the rod away, leaned back, shut my eyes, and slept since last night had run me a little ragged and I needed to catch up. At noon, my woke me along with Windsor and Dark Moon. And after lunch, we set out on the day's hunt. The journey went smoothly, no trouble at all.

And after gathering some white stone and ordinary resources, we rose back to the surface. Evening had come, the sun sinking into clouds of color, and I stretched out in the cool sea breeze, feeling wonderfully at ease. Before I could enjoy it, a black dot in the distance began rushing toward us, and I snapped alert and shouted for everyone to watch the sky.

By the time I called out, it had already closed to within 200 m. It looked like a bald eagle, only with two heads. Its body holy red with white feathers over head and neck. I read it at once with my spirit eye. It was a double-headed white-headed eagle, blue quality.

At level 16, its strength and agility and defense all near 100, carrying extreme speed and sharp claws and wind vortex and windb blade. The number startled me, but I did not panic. I could not handle a monster with 100 points to its name. Yet Windsor could, and Huawa was stronger still. I told Windsor it was hers and shouted for everyone else to fall back into the cabin.

The eagle did not dive, circling instead as if hunting for its opening, and I retreated as well since my strength would only distract Windsor if I stayed on deck. Everyone drew back while Windsor stood alone in the open. Then I noticed Huawa had not gone in either since she had wandered to the lounge chair and sat down with a bored look.

I felt a flicker of helplessness, though it did not truly worry me because I had not needed to fret over Huawa since her awakening. Windsor wasted no time, and to keep the eagle off the ship, she spent twothirds of her mana to summon an angel of light. The angel stood three meters tall in a white battle skirt, two pairs of white wings on her back glowing a milky light.

She was gold-haired and goldeyed, a long sword in her hand, whose hilt was gold and blade silver white. She beat her wings and charged, and the eagle met her head on, the two tangling in the air at once. The angel loosed milky sword light, while the eagle threw out cyan wind blades and wind vortexes. And as the clashes wore on, both took wounds of every kind.

Blood ran bright across the angel's wings, and windb blades opened cuts on her legs and arms, and the damaged wings began to drag at her flight. The eagle fared worse, drenched in its own blood, one of its two heads blown clean off. Then a white light bloomed over the angel, and her wounds began to knit, which was Windsor casting healing from below.

The eagle understood it had to leave or die here, so it wheeled and flapped away into the distance. Windsor did not give chase, calling the angel back down to her side. Right then, the eagle spun around and came back faster than before, clearly riding its extreme speed.

It did not strike at Windsor, though, but flew at Huaha on a lounge chair, unwilling to leave wounded and empty-handed, so it gambled on a surprise blow. It had chosen the wrong prey. As it swept in and stretched out its claws to seize her, it saw its target smiling, white teeth bared.

A long spear appeared and drove into its body, and it felt the spear drinking its blood, felt itself thinning and weakening. Its last sight was a figure in blood red armor with blood red hair, and those blood red eyes terrified it most of all. Huawa drew back her spear and let her body return to the red dress, then stood and looked down at the shriveled corpse of the double-headed white-headed eagle.

She bent, her figure caught close by the dress, lifted the body, and flung it into the sea. Clapping her hands, she skipped over to Windsor and said they should head back inside. Windsor rubbed her head fondly and agreed, saying I was still waiting for them. The two of them walked off the deck toward the core cabin together.

I heard the door and looked up to find Windsor back just as I had thought. Huawa ran straight at me, knocked me onto the sofa, and claimed the kill, insisting she had been the one to finish the double-headed white-headed eagle, and demanding a reward. I was thrown for a moment, wondering how the credit had landed on her until Windsor sat beside me and told me what had happened.

I chuckled and said the eagle had been truly unlucky to ambush Huawa, that it must have thought it was not dying fast enough. Huawa nodded along eagerly, agreeing that it had dared to jump her and pressing again that she had killed it and so deserved her reward.

As she spoke, she kept rubbing and swaying against me, and I felt the softness of her and a heat rising in my chest that I quickly forced back down. I gathered my scattered thoughts, smiled, and asked what reward she wanted. Without a breath, she blurted that she wanted to sleep together. The smile froze on my face since I had forgotten Huawa's one steady wish.

And this was exactly it. Windsor caught my expression and broke into laughter, saying, "A look like that on me was a rare sight. And her laughing left me faintly embarrassed and privately resolved to deal with her tonight. For now, I had to soothe Huawa first. It was not that I felt nothing for her since I was a normal man and no man is blind to a beautiful woman.

The trouble was that my picture of her was still the little pet she used to be, and my mind had not caught up to the rest. I knew well enough that under her bold direct campaign I would come around before long, but I could not agree to this right now. I told her the condition would not work and to choose another one.

Her small face fell at once and she pouted plainly unhappy, so I knew I could not simply leave her with nothing. I told her that although I could not grant her wish, a kiss was on offer and though she was still a little sulky, she brightened at any progress at all. She pointed to her red lips and said she wanted it there.

I did not refuse, lowering my head to kiss her soft lips and feeling her warm breath. Just as I moved to pull away, she caught my head, pressed it down, and deepened the kiss herself. A few minutes later, I left the core cabin in a hurry and stepped out onto the deck since if I had stayed, I would have been swallowed by Huawa's flames.

Behind me, Windsor poked Huawa's forehead and called her truly bold, and Huawa only giggled that she was happy. Then hugged Windsor's arm and drew her into a whispered exchange. The moment I reached the deck, I ran into Lee Ruk, who looked agitated. His whole body strung tight with excitement. My own expression shifted as a guest rose in me, and the instant it surfaced, my heart would not stay calm.

Before he could get a word out, I told him to skip the formalities and just say it. His face flushed as he reported that we were nearly at our destination and would arrive in one more hour at most. I had suspected it, yet hearing it confirmed aloud set me off anyway.

I was finally going to reach this island, the destination I had fixed on the very first day I woke in this world. I had never grasped how vast the world was back then, since the map made it look close. When the crossing had taken a full 25 days, four whole disaster weeks through heavy rain and blistering heat and killing cold and towering waves.

I had slaughtered in the demon domain, fought through a clash of two territories, and hunted again and again beneath the sea. I had risked killing the extreme ice giant bear, brushed past powers like the gym mountain turtle and the azure sea dragon king, explored the Muroke city under the water, and joined a treasure hunt.

I had started on a single wooden plank, and now stood on a four-story level three tower ship, gone from utterly alone to surrounded by strong companions. My mood ran tangled as I stood there. I worried the island might be a barren waste where no territory could take root. At the same time, I burned to plant one here and begin fresh.

My head crowded with the ambition of raising a vast domain. I made myself calm and told Lee Ruk to speed the ship, but take no risks. A man who has walked 90 mi of a 100-m road is only halfway done, and I would not have trouble at the very end. He straightened and swore loudly to proceed with care and reach the destination as fast as he safely could.

After he left, I stayed on deck rather than returned to the cabin, watching the horizon for the island to show itself until Windsor came up beside me and gently patted my shoulder. Windsor said softly that Mory had finished dinner and it was time to eat. After a while, I settled and told her Lee Rou had just reported we were nearly at the destination.

Her eyes lit and she was delighted, too, since she had been the first to appear at my side and had walked nearly the whole road with me. She understood then why I had not gone back in and she offered to have Mory bring the meal up so we could eat on deck. I agreed and told her to arrange it that way.

Mory soon laid the food out on deck and I sat on a stool eating with the sunset in my eyes, glancing again and again at the sea ahead for the silhouette I had imagined. By the time the meal was done, the sun had half sunk, its red glow spilling across the deck and the water until sea and sky ran one color.

I was in no mood for the picture perfect view, my thoughts pinned to the distance, and Windsor and the others quieted too and waited beside me. Then a joyful shout carried down from the observation deck, and it took a second cry before I made out the words that the lookout could see the island, a very large island.

Liu came running and told me we had cited it. I was excited, but did not lose my footing, only nodding and telling everyone to head for the bow. I took the first step, and the others fell in behind, and we lined up at the bow, staring ahead. After a moment, a dark silhouette rose into my view, and my hands on the railing tightened without my meaning them to.

As the ship pressed on, the shape swelled and sharpened into an island, a lush green mountain range across its back. Now and then birds and beasts squeealled in the sky above it. The sight of them brought the double-headed white-headed eagle to mind, the one that had jumped us, and I suspected such creatures came from this very island.

We drew close enough that the whole of it slid out of view. I told Le to hold off landing and circle the island once first, recording its size and the shape of its edges and marking where a ship might dock. I wanted a rough measure of the island before anything else since I would raise my territory here and its scale mattered a great deal.

I also needed a good spot for a port, both to land now and to shelter more ships in the future, and it was already evening with night close. No time to be exploring an island in the dark, which was far too dangerous. Those three reasons decided me since I was eager but unwilling to spend lives for nothing.

And after waiting so long, one more night cost me nothing. Lee Roo had no objection, knowing you could not just beach anywhere without risking a grounding, which would be a serious problem. He bowed, said he understood, and ran off to steer the crew. I stayed on deck with Windsor and the others, watching the island's rim as the ship began its circuit along the shore.

From where I stood, I could see the beach, and then the beach gave way to stretches of forest. Some trees rooted right out in the sea. The coastal woods ran long, and we sailed a good while before anything changed. When the change came, we could not help exclaiming since a river slid out of the forest and merged slowly into the sea.

It was only 2 m wide, but that did nothing to dampen me because a river meant fresh water on this island. Once an island had water and food, the foundation of a territory was laid, and the rest was only a matter of time. As we went on, I passed several more rivers of varying size, some barely a meter across, the largest close to 20 m.

I could scarcely imagine how vast the island had to be to feed rivers that wide. That was only what showed at the edge, and I wondered how much wider they ran inland. As we sailed on the coastline, climbed, the drop to the sea growing, until steep cliffs stood between island and water, a full hundred meters high at their tallest.

Past the cliffs, the coastal jungle returned, and then we came on an inward curving triangular bay with a river some 10 m wide, spilling into the sea from its left side. Lieuk quickly had someone record the place since it could well serve as the base for a port. It was fully dark by then, though the sky was clear and the moon bright.

Still, I called him over and told him to hold off any action that we would rest here and check again tomorrow. We were close to the island in the dark and it would be easy to strike a reef. He understood the principle and agreed, then pointed ahead at the triangular bay and asked me to take a look.

I looked where Lee pointed, making the place out roughly under the bright moon, and asked whether there was something special about it since I understood nothing about ports. He explained that the inward curving bay held deep blue water, which proved it was no shallow zone, and with land on both sides, it would make an excellent port once tidied up.

He added that the river running from inland beside it meant a port built here would keep easy contact with the interior, and he pointed the river out again. I nodded and made a note of it since I did not grasp the details myself, but it was enough that someone did. The port would go here in the future.

Lee Ruk fell silent, and after he left, he ordered the crew to stop, and the ship gradually came to rest. I lingered no longer on deck and turned toward the core cabin only to notice that Dark Moon had not followed me. I stopped, turned back, and asked whether she needed something. She told me she meant to land and scout the island.

My first thought was to stop her since going up alone late at night was dangerous. Then I remembered her profession as a night hunter, that the dark was her home ground and land suited her strength even better. So I dropped the objection and told her she could go and investigate, but to be absolutely careful and to take Huawa with her.

Huawa had devoured Chino Fen's flames and gained a form fit for moving in the dark. So I did not fear her being exposed. Otherwise, the moment she used a skill, her red and gold flames would blaze like a beacon in the night. Dark Moon had meant to refuse, but under my settled gaze, she shut her mouth and only nodded.

I looked at Huawa and told her to follow her sister, Dark Moon, and mind her orders, and Huawa smiled and agreed. Then I called Little Blue, and Huawa and Dark Moon climbed onto his body while he loed a transformation skill and shrank himself down. A body that large risks stranding near the coast, but shrunk he could carry the two of them up the river into the island's interior.

I watched them recede without another word, trusting Dark Moon was no reckless sort, and would meet no great danger with a little caution. Then turned back into the core cabin with Windsor, I took off my outer clothes and got into bed, leaning on the headboard to spend today's 300 transformations.

The first item was the Shadow Fortress Plum Blossom token I had fished up that morning, the blue token that could command one general level assassin. I did not specify a type, choosing a random transformation, enchanted 100 transformations in silence. Light flickered over the token, and when it cleared, a red circular token lay in my hand.

A purple character for command sat on its face, wrapped by two green branches, and a stamped character for heaven edged the back between two black curved blades. It's turning into another token surprised me even after a random draw. My spirit eye readed as a Chano purple cloud token, purple quality.

It was the identity token of the city lord's mansion of Chan Mo City in the spirit domain, granting its holder the authority of a commander of that mansion. My mind went briefly blank. With this, I could wield the commander's authority in Shanmo City itself. I had no idea how much authority such a commander held, but I knew very well that Shan Mo City was the core of the whole continent.

Even a minor official there was no small thing, and the title commander did not sound minor at all. I would not be fool enough to march the token into the city lord's mansion and claim the post. Virtue that does not match its position invites disaster. And here strength that does not match its position invites the same.

For a weakling like me to carry it in and demand a commander's power would be a child hauling treasure through a crowded market. Courting death. I put the token away since I could not use it now. It would be a fine thing once my strength caught up because a fitting identity and a solid pretext often matter a great deal.

I took out the second item, a brush I had gathered from the great tree during the treasure hunt. Its handle black, its bristles white, a clear jade bead set at the tip. Its information named it a jade bead snowwolf bristle brush. Blue quality which raised the power of painted spirits by 20% once equipped and was much loved by divine painters.

Surprise caught in me since I had taken it for another lifestyle trinket like the water cups I kept turning up when it was actually a piece of equipment. It was still useless to me and I was not about to keep it gathering dust. So I specified the blueprint type and chanted 100 transformations. The brush vanished into a blueprint, and my spirit eye read it out.

It was a spirit painting pavilion construction blueprint, purple quality, and once built, it could accumulate one painted spirit of strength up to level 24 each day. The level and quality left a chance. I was delighted since this was a fine thing that would grow my strength daily once it stood.

If after a year, the spirit painting pavilion had stacked up 80 or 100 purple quality painted spirits at level 24, that alone would be unbeatable. I knew I was dreaming, of course, since the strength of those painted spirits was random. But dreams are always lovely. And who is to say one will not come true? If a man will not even dare to imagine it, what does he expect to build?

I put the blueprint away and glanced through the window at the island outside, wondering what Dark Moon and the others were doing now. I was curious about them, though I knew there was nothing to do but wait and hope they came back with good news. I took out the third item, a red cup, whose information named it a warming cup, blue quality, able to heat and purify the water inside it.

The cup had come from Wang Mock, whose acquisitions were mostly flashy and practical things like this. I did not specify a type and chose a random transformation. And after a flash of light, a milky white round bead sat in my hand. My spirit eye read it as a healing bead, a special item which granted the body a strong healing power for 10 minutes, quickening recovery and strengthening vitality.

I smiled since I had not expected a cup to turn into something that could save my life. I put the beat away, drew Windsor close, and kissed her, and closed my eyes to rest, since I meant to save my strength for exploring the island tomorrow. I slept well and woke comfortable, and the sight of Windsor's drowsy, hazy face stirred me, though I had important matters today, and could not indulge myself.

After breakfast, Windsor and I came onto the deck, and I called Lee Ruk over and asked outright whether Dark Moon had come back. Then I set to the day's resource fishing and before long I had spent all three chances without checking any of them, meaning to look them over in the evening when I had time. Its area vast and its prospects large with plenty of places for ports and abundant freshwater besides. He called it simply an excellent place to raise a territory. I nodded, excited myself, and told him to find a birth and land now.

Before long, we returned to the recessed triangular bay, and the ship eased in, proving just as good a birth as Li had promised the night before. Grass and trees crowded both banks, and now and then, a beast's roar carried over the water, which did not surprise me since an island like this had to hold many demon beasts.

Within minutes, I spotted the two of them, one in tight black leather and the other in a red dress, sitting on the bankstones, eating grilled fish. The instant Huawa saw as she threw her fish aside, beat her wings across, and hung off my body like an octopus. I patted her and told her to come down since we had to go ashore.
