The wolves many, their leader, a level 18 purple quality giant wolf. My face turned serious at that. Huawa could probably handle a lone level 18 purple wolf at her strength. The trouble was that it was a pack, not one beast. And even a fierce tiger fears a wolf pack that swarms it. Dark Moon had called the place suitable.

So, I had to see it for myself, and if it truly was, I would take it, whatever the cost. We boarded Little Blue and traveled upstream, and I asked Dark Moon what she made of the monster's overall strength after her night on the island.

She said there were many highquality monsters here, that she and Huawa had met five or six purple quality ones in a single night, though not one golden quality beast among them. I nodded. Since golden monsters were never easy to find, yet five or six purple in one night was still striking.

She went on that the overall strength here was nothing to dismiss since deeper in the forest they had run across a level 21 monster. She seemed to relive the moment as she spoke, still shaken. Neither she nor Huawa had dared to breathe for fear of being noticed, and they had been lucky. The thing hurried past without spotting them.

Then she laid out the other beasts they had met, their strength, and roughly where each ranged. Her account gave me a first picture of the island. On the whole, its monsters were not to be underestimated with many purple quality beasts and a real chance of golden ones. Where purple monsters were common, there had to be a good number in their 20s.

So, this place still demanded caution. I checked my own information while we moved. I had reached level 9. My strength sitting in the mid60s with agility and defense close behind, intelligence at 60, and mana at 600. My profession state hero and ice element messenger. My skills spirit eye and water arrow technique and shield alongside the purple freezing spell and mystic ice puppet.

My gear was the golden sky dragon staff and a spread of purple pieces. My talent the legendary 100 transformations. These past two days had lifted me a level and the trip to that strange volcano and the two heavenly treasures had pushed my base attributes up hard. I glanced at Dark Moon's panel next. She was legendary at level seven now.

Her strength and agility and defense all in the 60s before bonuses. Her profession was hero and knigh hunter. Her skills led by the freshly upgraded gold dark domain along with darkness blade and darkness erosion and moonshadow and shadow lurk. Her loyalty and affinity both sat at their ceiling. She reckoned she would reach level eight before long. Windsor had climbed to level 9 as I expected.

Her profession hero and light element messenger. Her skills ran angel of light and healing and light arrow and light shield and more. Her strength passed 100 once her equipment counted in. Even so, she was still no match for Hwa. I opened Huawa's information last. She read as Ha, a fire elemental spirit of the fire marshall god line.

Legendary at level 10. Her strength was 90 with every attribute up near it. Her profession hero and fire element controller. Her skills ran fireball and the three form fire marshall god and fire binding and fire shield and the goldfire meteor fall. Her weapon the golden bloodthirsty demon spear. The volcano had let her finish her awakening. And with Chino Fen's flame and Yong Gira's core, her strength had leapt.

On those alone, she would only match Windsor. But she also carried a monstrous skill, the fire marshall god that had grown out of her old blazing body. The moment she entered the fire marshall god state, her base attributes doubled. So her strength of 90 became 180. And with equipment on top of that, she was terrifying. In that form, she could use new skills the panel would not show.

So I did not know them all. I had only ever seen her bloodthirsty fire marshall god shape, the one born from Yomira's core, the shape that had drained the double-headed white-headed eagle. She was without question our team's trump card, the strongest of us. I did not bother checking Soap Pan since his strength was plainly below my own.

Still, my team lacked a solid front line, and if I could not summon a strong one before long, I would have to train Soap Pan into the role. We pushed on carefully all the way, cutting down a few monsters that came at us until we reached the place Dark Moon had spoken of.

From Little Blue's back, I looked ahead at a river that flowed into a huge lake, and out again from another side, the very river we rode. Around the lake stood no tall trees, only a wide grassland, and behind it, a mountain range curved at the lake into an arc that half circled the water.

Between lake and mountains lay a broad plane that could also hold a territory, though it looked a touch small. There was no rush on that, though, since the wolfpack came first. If I could not deal with the wolfpack, then anything else was just wishful thinking. So I asked Dark Moon where they were since I could not see them.

She pointed to the mountain range behind the lake and said the pack den in a cluster of caves up there. Sometimes they came down to the lake to drink and sometimes went deep into the mountains to hunt. If we left them be, we would have to guard against their raids forever once our territory stood. I nodded since she was right and the trouble had to be settled.

I had taken to the place the moment I saw it anyway, since it was almost too fit for a territory. There was water and trees and a mountain range right beside it to hunt for both meat and experience. Building here carried a risk. Of course, the nearness of the mountains making it easy prey for beasts pouring out of them.

But I was not much worried since any site carried some risk of attack and this one only raised the odds a little. Once the territory stood with walls around it, the safety would climb sharply. I had another way to make the territory safer. Besides, I understood all that, so I said we should simply go and look at this wolf pack and be rid of them as soon as we could.

Little Blue carried us across the lake and we climbed off his back and started for the mountains while he stayed behind in the water. On the way, we put down a few beasts that did not know their place. And under Dark Moon's guidance, we reached a corner of the range. The ground there was tracked with wolf prints and littered with the bones of demon beasts.

And looking up at the mountains, I could see cave after cave. As we drew near, I spotted the first wolf. Its fur isure all over, its limbs thick, its eyes blood red above bared fangs with saliva stringing from the corners of its mouth. The moment it saw us, it crouched low into an attacking stance. I read it with my spirit eye.

It was an azure main wolf, blue quality. At level 18, its strength and agility and defense only near 120, carrying bite, pounce, and windb blade. The first one we met being a blue level 18 at around 120 gave me pause. If the whole pack ran like this, the few of us might not have the strength to clear them.

Even so, my mind was set on raising my territory here, whatever it took. So, this pack had to die. And at worst, I would spend a magic scroll. For now that was clearly not needed. I told the others to begin. Windsor said nothing and threw out a binding spell followed by a light arrow.

She caught the Azure main wolf off guard and though it slipped the binding spell, it did not slip the arrow which took it in the waist and drew an instant gush of blood. The wolf looed a long howl that rolled across the slope. One azureine wolf after another began spilling from the other caves in every size until 20 or 30 of them had surrounded us and pressed in to attack.

At that moment, the ground ahead dropped into blackness and swallowed every wolf. And from inside came only howls thick with rage, blood lust, and a threat of fear. Dark Moon had moved, releasing her dark domain. I used the cover to chant quickly and summon three mystic ice puppets, and Windsor summoned five angels of her own.

They were plainly weaker than the one from the day before, but more than enough for a mob of wolves. I had already checked that the fresh 20 or 30 Azure main wolves were all blue quality, but generally level 10 to 13, few of them higher. A blue level 13 wolf sat around 75 points, easy enough to handle.

Inside her domain, Dark Moon was already hunting them, well able to deal with wolves at that level. 5 minutes on, she stepped back out of the dark domain, shook her windbreaking blade, and looked ahead as the darkness thinned. 11 Azure main wolf corpses lay on the ground. A third of the enemy's strength cut down in those 5 minutes.

The surviving wolves saw their packmates bodies and threw back their heads to help, then fixed their devouring eyes on us and rushed in on all fours. The five angels of light and three mystic ice puppets went forward to meet them while Windsor and I loosed skills to keep the rest in check. Even so, a handful broke through to us.

Then, Huawa moved. She took up the bloodthirsty demon spear as red flames climbed her body and a layer of red armor sealed over her. 8 m. Her cute shape gone cold and bloodthirsty into the fire marshall god. She entered the pack almost unopposed. A demonst among them. The bloodthirsty demon spear plunging into an azureine wolf and draining its blood at once.

By the time she tore the spear free, the wolf was already dead, its fur gone dull, one thrust between entry and exit enough to end a life. Before long, the wolves that had reached us were wiped out to the last, and I was a little startled watching it since this was the first time I had seen Huawa fight on land.

At sea, she had always been badly restricted and could never fully lose her power. Right. Then another howl split the air, deafening and heavy with force, and I turned toward the sound. A giant azure main wolf leapt from a cave mouth and howled up at the sky. Far larger than the rest, a single strand of purple mane on its forehead.

Its eyes were not blood red, but azure. And where the others were raving beasts, this one carried the calm of a thinking creature, a little like the gym mountain turtle. The moment the azureine wolf leader broke from the trees, I swept my spirit eye across it and read the whole thing. It stood at level 18, a purple quality beast whose every attribute had already climbed past 160.

It could bite and pounce, hurl a windb blade, and work a pair of wind arts new to me. I knew my own limits well enough, and nothing in my hands could trade blows with a creature like that. So, I told Huawa the leader was hers to take, and left it entirely in her keeping.

She hovered a moment in the air, that cold and lovely face of hers softening toward me with a slight smile, and it was as if a whole winter melted in an instant into sunlight. Then she beat her wings and closed on the azure main wolf leader at once. The bloodthirsty demon spear in her grip and blood red flames curling around her whole body.

The leader felt her strength in its pupils shrank to points, its body flinching on pure instinct before its mind even caught up. It refused to meet her head on, spinning a green wind vortex under each of its four paws, so that it slid clean out of the path of her strike. Her spear found only empty air, and not a flicker of feeling crossed her face as she pursued.

The wolf kept its distance and threw the odd windb blade back at her to harass. Too cunning to be pinned. That cunning finally pricricked her temper, and she chanted three ropes of blood red flame out of nothing and sent them coiling after the beast. It dodged and dodged until its speed bled away.

And that was the opening she wanted, closing the gap with a bloodthirsty demon spear until the two of them were truly locked together. On my side of the field, mystic ice puppet and Windsor's Angel of Light had begun grinding through the rest of the pack with Dark Moon slipping in and out to worry their flanks. The Azer Wolves thinned and weakened, and before long, the whole pack lay dead.

The leader watched every one of its kin fall, and the pain of it clearly gutted the creature, losing on its own front against Huawa, bleeding from fresh wounds by the second. It was a dead thing that simply had not stopped moving yet. Rage, it turned out, does strange work. The wolf half lost its reason, and the same fury dragged something buried up out of it.

Layer on layer of green wind vortex crawled across its body until the whole beast was wrapped in a spinning shell of it, and its fur began to shift color under the wind. The green darkened toward purple. The beast swelled into a giant purple wolf. Its eyes turned to pale gold, and a dark crescent bloomed on its forehead.

I had never once seen anything like it, and I read the creature again just to be sure I was not imagining it. Purple moon demon wolf. Now, gold quality and level 19. Its attributes vaulted up near 190 and a purple moon demon eye added to the tally. What in the world was this?

Leveling up in a fit of rage was one thing, but jacking up your quality on top of it struck me as a shameless lack of marshall virtue. The old gym mountain turtle had told me once that lifting a creature's quality was brutally hard for any race. It was a breakthrough in its very level of life, one that took both the right chance and the right moment.

To watch it happen to my enemy right there on both counts at once, was almost insulting. I did not panic. Even so, the wolf could trade blows with Huawa now. Yet, one long look at the field told me she still held the upper hand, and killing the thing was only a question of time. She lost her patience soon enough, and her own body answered the change.

The flames around her turned from blood red to bright gold, her armor shifting to a gold plate chased with blood, red, and black flame patterns. It was so richly worked that the void around her rippled and shuddered. The bloodthirsty demon spear left her hand and hung in the air, and the golden flame poured off her body into the weapon until the whole shaft burned gold.

A flick of her hand launched it, and the spear tore toward the purple moon demon wolf, whistling as it split the air. The beast felt death coming and knew that if it failed to slip this one strike, it would simply end.

It grew the vortexes under its paws, spat wind blades and tornadoes to block, even fired twin purple beams from the crescent in its eyes, and none of it mattered in the slightest. The bloodthirsty demon spear punched clean through the wolf's waist and abdomen and nailed it to the ground while it was still howling.

Huawa slid back into her fire marshall god form, dropped to the earth and closed one small hand around the shaft. Blood red patterns rose across her fair little fingers to answer the patterns burning along the spear, and the wolf's blood began to run up the weapon and into her.

Fear flickered in the beast's eyes, though more than fear there was ferocity, and its wars weakened and weakened until its body shriveled, its fur went dull, and it breathed its last. I let out a slow breath at that. A small unexpected turn, but no real damage done. And the whole thing was closed.
